# Herdr-Derived Terminal Client and Native PTY Runtime

**Plan ID:** herdr-terminal-client

## O1: Overview

`kind: framing`

Build `gclient` — the `gobby-client` TUI derived from the Apache-2.0 herdr codebase — and a backend-neutral daemon `TerminalRuntime` contract with two implementations: the existing tmux stack and a new Herdr-derived native PTY host (`gterm`). `gobby` remains today's Python daemon and Click CLI (`start`, `stop`, `status`, `tasks`, …). Users run `gclient` for the workspace. Gobby's daemon and Postgres stay authoritative for projects, sessions, runs, tasks, worktrees, attention, and lifecycle; the host owns only live PTYs, terminal emulation state, scrollback, and recoverable runtime snapshots.

Research findings that shaped this plan (herdr @ the `v0.8.0` release tag, commit `346411fa`, 2026-08-03 — "the fork point"): herdr has no Rust terminal emulator — its VT engine is vendored libghostty-vt (Ghostty's full Zig source tree, ~18 MB, built with `zig` at compile time via `-Demit-lib-vt`, a build mode of the full tree with no separable upstream artifact). Herdr is already a headless-server/thin-client system with a mature bincode frame protocol (two negotiated encodings, multi-client, detach/reattach, per-terminal attach) — this plan adopts and rebrands that protocol rather than designing a new one. Herdr's Ratatui workspace UI runs server-side; Gobby inverts that seam: `gclient` (crate `gobby-client`) renders a Gobby workspace fed by the daemon (roster, attention) and attaches per-terminal frame streams from the host, importing herdr's generic UI chrome (sidebar, tabs, navigator, status, keybind help, dialogs, scrollbar, widgets — ~13.5k LOC) rewired to Gobby data sources and restyled to the Gobby design system (3.3). This is a **one-time fork, owned outright after import** — no upstream tracking, no mechanical re-pin; individual post-fork upstream fixes may be cherry-picked deliberately with provenance recorded in `UPSTREAM.md` (1.2, 1.5). Roughly a third of herdr's ~200k Rust LOC is imported; its agent detection, plugins (deferred to D1), worktrees, integrations, updater, session persistence, JSON API, and app orchestration are not. Upstream lives at `herdrdev/herdr` after its GitHub org migration.

The shipped web terminal (`.gobby/plans/completed/activity-panel-live-terminal.md`) documents tmux-inherent defects this work eliminates for Gobby-managed terminals: cross-socket identity collisions, smallest-client sizing shrinking simultaneous viewers, duplicated VT query replies from multi-viewer relays, and the 200×50-default post-attach repaint handshake. The native backend is also the recorded path to Windows support (ConPTY) once macOS/Linux parity lands.

## C1: Constraints

`kind: framing`

- **License gate (supersedes the sibling constraint).** `herdr-interface-backend-foundation.md` C1 states "herdr is AGPL-3.0. Ideas only — no code". That constraint is superseded: herdr relicensed to Apache-2.0 in commit `cd5ea1be` (2026-07-22), the relicense is recorded in the **released** `v0.8.0` changelog (2026-08-03), and this plan forks from that tagged release. Remaining accepted, documented risk: upstream ships no NOTICE file, and the AGPL→Apache consent mechanism for ~8 outside contributors is not documented in-repo. Apache-2.0 §4 attribution and modification obligations are honored via deliverable 1.5.
- **Fork-once, own outright.** The import is a one-time fork at `v0.8.0`; there is no upstream tracking and no re-pin procedure. Post-fork upstream fixes are adopted only as deliberate per-commit cherry-picks recorded in `UPSTREAM.md` (1.2 names the initial set; 1.5 owns the log format). The registered reference clone lives at `~/.gobby/clones/herdr`.
- **Topology: process and protocol boundaries, not later product names.** Today `gobby` is the Python daemon and Click CLI. Users run `gclient` for the TUI (`gobby-client` package). This epic does not rename either binary, does not add a Python wrapper or exec of the TUI, and does not implement later daemon topology. The Python surfaces this plan adds in P2–P4 stay thin: the durable contract is the JSON-lines control protocol, the bincode frame protocol, and the backend-neutral WS messages (the golden corpus is the enforcement). `gclient` couples to the daemon only through the public HTTP/WS API, never internals. Hosted web viewing in this epic uses those same 2.5 messages through the daemon proxy; D3 owns any later relay privacy stance. `gterm` is permanently a separate supervised process — folding PTY ownership into the daemon would forfeit terminal durability across daemon restarts and lease handoffs, which is the property this plan exists to buy. `gclient` is permanently a separate interactive process and a separate binary (the VT payload is host-only, so multi-call packaging would bloat every client install).
- **`vt-engine` feature gate.** The Ghostty engine (vendored tree, `build.rs` Zig invocation, `src/ghostty/` FFI, and the pane emulation built on it) sits behind a `vt-engine` cargo feature of `gobby-terminal`, enabled by the `gterm` binary and disabled by default. `gobby-client` depends on the light modules only (wire protocol, input encoding, layout, theme) and builds without Zig; protocol-only tests do too.
- **Full-tree vendor.** libghostty-vt is vendored as Ghostty's full source tree at herdr's recorded pin (`c5a21edfcbc2d5b46540ad91b7980aca31f5f1f3`), herdr-style — verbatim `vendor.json`/`patches/` workflow — because `-Demit-lib-vt` is a build mode of the full tree, and pruning would mean owning a curated fork of Ghostty's build graph. Mitigations: code-index and lint exclusions (1.4), nested-license inventory (1.5).
- **Zig is a build-time dependency** (Zig 0.15) only for builds that enable `vt-engine` — the `gterm` host binary and its CI jobs. `gobby-client` and protocol-only test runs never invoke Zig. End users receive prebuilt release binaries; the installer's local-cargo fallback behavior for these crates is defined in 3.6.
- **Naming.** `crates/gterminal` → package `gobby-terminal` → binary `gterm`; `crates/gclient` → package `gobby-client` → binary `gclient`. Users run `gclient` for the TUI. `gobby` stays the Python Click daemon CLI. This epic does not add a `gobby client` wrapper and does not exec the TUI from the Python entry point (3.5). Renaming the `gclient` binary to `gobby` is evolution, not this plan. The `gclient` binary name avoids colliding with the Python `gobby` entry point and with `~/.gobby/logs/gobby-client.log` / `gobby-client-error.log` already used by the daemon restart helper (`src/gobby/servers/routes/admin/_lifecycle.py`). Host logs to `~/.gobby/logs/gterm.log`; TUI logs to `~/.gobby/logs/gclient.log`.
- **Transport.** Host endpoints are Unix domain sockets under `~/.gobby/` with mode 0600 (`gterm-control.sock`, `gterm-frames.sock`). No new TCP port: 60889 and 60891 are already bound (web-dev UI / managed Postgres) and 60890 is reserved (`ROADMAP.md`; nothing binds it yet). Windows named-pipe support arrives with the later Windows milestone; the imported `ipc` layer already abstracts both.
- **No attach-capability token system in this epic.** Local sockets are same-user 0600, and frame clients additionally present the existing install-scoped local CLI token (`~/.gobby/local_cli_token`, the same credential gcode/gwiki already use). Web viewers reach native terminals only through the daemon's WS proxy (4.3), where daemon auth already applies. Short-lived per-terminal capabilities are deferred until a remote/web-direct attach surface exists (D2).
- **The two host sockets do not share a credential, and the trust boundary is the local user account.** Frame clients present `~/.gobby/local_cli_token`; the control socket accepts only `~/.gobby/gterm-control.token`, a separate 0600 file the daemon mints on first host spawn and which no client is ever given (3.1). This is stated as what it is: a same-uid process that can read `~/.gobby/` can forge either credential, so nothing here defends against a hostile process running as the user — that is outside the boundary on both macOS and Linux, and no local mechanism moves it. What the split does buy is that possession of a *client's* credential is not possession of write authority: gclient must hold `local_cli_token` to talk to the daemon at all, and under a shared credential that same token would also unlock every mutating verb on the control socket. The frame protocol additionally has no write verb to reach (3.2), so the read-only guarantee does not rest on the credential alone.
- **Daemon authority.** The host never talks to Postgres and holds no durable state beyond optional scrollback snapshots on disk. All lifecycle, attention, task, and session semantics stay in the Python daemon. Host crash → affected native terminals are marked `orphaned`, affected runs become `interrupted`, and Gobby's normal resume flow applies; live PTYs are never reconstructed.
- **No silent backend switching.** An explicitly requested native launch fails clearly when the host is unavailable; it never falls back to tmux silently. tmux remains a first-class backend for externally launched/discovered sessions indefinitely.
- **SRT sandbox semantics preserved.** Managed provider commands are wrapped by SRT once, after construction and before terminal creation, with `allowPty: true` and fail-closed preflight. The native spawn path (4.2) wraps identically before handing the command to the host. Only the daemon and `gclient` (both unsandboxed) dial host sockets; sandboxed agent processes never do.
- **No backward compatibility.** 0.5.0 is unshipped: tmux-specific columns and WS message names are replaced, not aliased.
- **Session lifecycle unions stay lifecycle-only** (per the sibling plan): attention and terminal state are orthogonal dimensions, never new `SessionStatus` values.
- **Schema changes land in the gcore baseline, not numbered SQL files.** `src/gobby/storage/migrations/` is empty and retired: schema authority is Rust `gcore` (`crates/gcore/src/schema/assets.rs`, `BASELINE_VERSION = 375`, applied via `gdaemon schema apply`), pre-0.5.0 changes fold into `crates/gcore/assets/schema/baseline.sql`, and numbered `EmbeddedMigration`s resume only after the release boundary. A schema change here therefore edits `baseline.sql`, refreshes `BASELINE_CHECKSUM` in `assets.rs`, and updates `crates/gcore/assets/schema/catalog.manifest.json` plus the Python identity mirror `src/gobby/storage/schema_expected_identity.json` — the same shape as commit `4a5b8f9e3`. 2.1 follows this mechanism.
- **Rust conventions.** New crates join the workspace per `crates/CLAUDE.md`: scope all cargo commands with `-p`, never relax lints, register in `rust-ci.yml`. The workspace `members` list is `["crates/gcode", "crates/gcore", "crates/gdaemon", "crates/ghook", "crates/gwiki"]` today; 1.2 and 3.3 append the two new crates. `gobby-terminal`/`gobby-client` keep herdr's edition 2021 to minimize import churn (herdr at v0.8.0 is edition 2021 with no MSRV pin) and pin `rust-version = "1.88"`, the workspace floor shared by all five existing crates (edition 2024; mixed editions are supported). `tokio`, `ratatui`, `crossterm`, and `portable-pty` are first *direct* dependencies in this workspace — tokio already appears transitively in `Cargo.lock` via gcore's `postgres` feature — and they are confined to these two crates; `ghook`'s tiny-binary/no-async policy is unaffected.
- **Testing.** Focused runs only: `GOBBY_TEST_PROTECT=1 uv run pytest <paths>` for Python, `cargo nextest run -p <package>` for Rust. PTY-spawning Rust tests run in a serialized nextest group. Never the full pytest suite.

## P1: Import and Carve the Herdr Terminal Core

`kind: framing`

**Goal**: `crates/gterminal` exists, builds on macOS/Linux with the vendored VT engine, passes the imported terminal test corpus, and contains no herdr agent/plugin/orchestration concepts.

### 1.1 Vendor libghostty-vt and portable-pty with the patch workflow [category: code]

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `crates/gterminal/vendor/libghostty-vt.vendor.json`
- `crates/gterminal/vendor/libghostty-vt.patches.md`
- `crates/gterminal/vendor/patches/libghostty-vt/0001-default-grapheme-cluster-mode.patch`
- `crates/gterminal/vendor/portable-pty.patches.md`
- `crates/gterminal/build.rs`
- `Cargo.toml` (workspace `[patch.crates-io]`)
- `scripts/build_vendored_libghostty_vt.sh`

Import herdr's vendor layer verbatim from the fork point (v0.8.0):

- `vendor/libghostty-vt/` — Ghostty's full source tree at commit `c5a21edf` (herdr's `vendor.json` records `source_commit` and the dist archive; keep that file format unchanged).
- `vendor/patches/libghostty-vt/0001-default-grapheme-cluster-mode.patch` — herdr's single 22-line patch making DEC mode 2027 (grapheme clustering) the default and reset-survivor, applied to the vendored tree, tracked in `libghostty-vt.patches.md` with its upstream-removal criteria.
- `vendor/portable-pty/` — upstream 0.9.0 plus herdr's two Windows-only patches (hash-verified ConPTY loading; raw command tail), tracked in `portable-pty.patches.md`. Unix code is unmodified upstream. Wire the workspace `[patch.crates-io]` redirect for `portable-pty = { path = "crates/gterminal/vendor/portable-pty" }`.
- `crates/gterminal/build.rs` — herdr's build script adapted for the new path: maps Rust target triples to Zig targets (8 supported triples), runs `zig build -Demit-lib-vt -Doptimize=ReleaseFast -Dsimd=true -Dtarget=…` in the vendored tree, links the static lib. The entire Zig invocation runs only when the `vt-engine` feature is enabled (C1); with the feature off the build script is a no-op, which is what keeps `gobby-client` builds Zig-free. Env knobs keep their `LIBGHOSTTY_VT_*` and `ZIG` names; `HERDR_BUILD_*` becomes `GTERM_BUILD_*`.
- `scripts/build_vendored_libghostty_vt.sh` — herdr's helper script, path-adapted.

The two vendor trees are imported **verbatim** as directory copies. Their file-level inventory is the trees themselves plus the provenance, patch, `build.rs`, helper-script, and workspace-patch Targets above. Do **not** enumerate every vendored source in Targets (the 1,259-file libghostty-vt tree is the point of 1.4's index exclusion). 1.1.1 is the close gate that the copied VT tree builds.

An unsupported target triple remains a hard, descriptive build error (matching herdr's `zig_target()` panic), and a missing `zig` binary fails the build with a message naming the required Zig version.

**This leaf cannot close `cargo -p gobby-terminal`.** The package manifest, `src/` skeleton, workspace `members` entry, and lockfile are 1.2's. 1.1 writes the vendor tree, patches, `build.rs`, the helper script, and the workspace `[patch.crates-io]` redirect only. Executable cargo tests that need the package (`build_env.rs`, default-features `cargo build -p gobby-terminal`) live in 1.2. This leaf's cargo-free close gate is the vendor Zig build plus file-level checks that `build.rs` names the Zig version, rejects unsupported triples, and is a no-op unless `vt-engine` is enabled.

**Acceptance:**

- 1.1.1 - The vendored VT tree at `c5a21edf` with the grapheme-cluster patch applied builds via `zig build -Demit-lib-vt` on macOS and Linux, invoked from `scripts/build_vendored_libghostty_vt.sh` (not `cargo -p`). file: `scripts/build_vendored_libghostty_vt.sh`.
- 1.1.2 - Patch provenance files record base commit, patch list, rationale, and removal criteria for both vendored trees. file: `crates/gterminal/vendor/libghostty-vt.patches.md`.
- 1.1.3 - The workspace `[patch.crates-io]` entry serves the patched portable-pty to the workspace. file: `Cargo.toml`.
- 1.1.4 - `build.rs` names the required Zig version in the missing-binary path, rejects unsupported target triples with a descriptive error, and invokes Zig only when the `vt-engine` feature is enabled (source-level; cargo execution is 1.2.8). file: `crates/gterminal/build.rs`.
- 1.1.5 - The helper script documents the same Zig version and triple map as `build.rs` and fails descriptively when `zig` is missing. file: `scripts/build_vendored_libghostty_vt.sh`.

### 1.2 Import the terminal keep-set as crates/gterminal [category: code] (depends: 1.1)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `crates/gterminal/Cargo.toml`

- `Cargo.lock`

Create `crates/gterminal` (package `gobby-terminal`, lib crate; the `gterm` binary arrives in 3.1) and import herdr's terminal keep-set (~41k LOC) from the fork point, rebranded (`herdr` → `gterm` in module docs, env vars, socket names; wire protocol version constant restarts at `1` for the Gobby lineage). `crates/gterminal/src/lib.rs` is the crate root: it declares every production module, applies the `vt-engine` feature gates, and owns the public re-export surface. The `src/ghostty/` FFI and `src/pane/` emulation modules are gated behind the `vt-engine` feature (C1); wire protocol, input encoding, layout, selection, and theme modules compile feature-free.

**Cherry-picks at import (fork-once, C1):** apply three named post-v0.8.0 upstream fixes as patches to the imported keep-set — `e9222d18` (restore keyboard reporting on detach), `d277d2f8` (preserve alt-prefixed control keys), `36074530` (compact large terminal redraws) — then review every remaining `v0.8.0..HEAD` commit touching keep-set paths in the reference clone and record an accept/reject decision per commit in `UPSTREAM.md` (1.5 owns the log format). Import from the fork point:

- `src/ghostty/` — checked-in bindgen FFI (`bindings.rs`) + safe wrapper (`Terminal`, `RenderState`, `Dirty`, `KeyEncoder`, `MouseEncoder`, scrollback/viewport APIs).
- `src/pane/` — `terminal.rs` (emulation adapter: `GhosttyPaneTerminal`, dirty-patch collection, cell conversion), `osc.rs`, `cursor.rs`, `input.rs`, `kitty_keyboard.rs`, `xtgettcap.rs`. **`agent_detection.rs` is not imported.**
- `src/pty/` — PTY spawn and I/O actors (`actor/unix.rs` poll-loop actor, Windows thread actor, `fd.rs`, backends).
- `src/input/` + `src/raw_input.rs` — key/mouse encoding (kitty keyboard protocol, xterm modifyOtherKeys, bracketed paste) and the server-side raw-input parser.
- `crates/gterminal/src/protocol/wire.rs` (bincode framing, `ClientMessage`/`ServerMessage`, `FrameData`/`TerminalFrame`, size limits, version check) and `crates/gterminal/src/protocol/render_ansi.rs` (`BlitEncoder`).
- `src/ipc.rs` — `interprocess` local-socket wrapper (Unix socket / Windows named pipe, 0600 permissions, stale-socket reaping).
- `src/platform/` — per-OS process operations (macOS/Linux/Windows/fallback). Probes that exist solely for herdr agent detection are dropped with 1.3.
- `src/layout.rs` — BSP split tree (`Node`, `TileLayout`, `PaneId`, `NavDirection`) for gclient's workspace.
- `src/runtime/` — the ~725 lines of genuine terminal plumbing from herdr's `src/terminal/` (`runtime.rs`, `id.rs`, `runtime_registry.rs`, `title.rs`), renamed to avoid the misleading herdr layout. **herdr's `src/terminal/state.rs` and `metadata.rs` (6.6k lines of agent-state arbitration) are not imported.**

Also not imported here: `src/app/`, `src/api/`, `src/cli/`, `src/detect/`, `src/integration/`, `src/persist/`, `src/workspace/`, `src/remote/`, `src/server/` (the host in 3.1 is written fresh against the keep-set), `src/client/` (gclient's input capture reuses its ideas), plugins (deferred, D1)/worktrees/updater/sound/session modules. Herdr's `src/ui/` chrome is imported by 3.3 into `crates/gclient`, not into this crate — it is client code, not terminal core.

The root workspace `members` list is explicit (`["crates/gcode", "crates/gcore", "crates/gdaemon", "crates/ghook", "crates/gwiki"]`), not a glob, so a crate that is not added to it is invisible to `cargo -p`, workspace `nextest`, and release packaging. `crates/gterminal` is added to that list **in this deliverable**, together with the `Cargo.toml` that declares the package — the same edit that makes the crate exist makes it buildable. 1.1 touches the same file for the `[patch.crates-io]` redirect only; it cannot add a member for a crate whose manifest does not exist yet, which is why membership lands here rather than there.

In-module unit tests ride along with the imported code: the pane/terminal corpus (~126 tests), wire/blit round-trips (~95), input encoding (~85), ghostty wrapper (~30). Windows-gated code compiles under `cargo clippy --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` but Windows is not a supported runtime this epic.

**Hand-maintained files must finish below 1,000 lines in this leaf.** The keep-set as imported exceeds the repo ceiling (`crates/gterminal/src/pane/terminal.rs` ~6k, `crates/gterminal/src/raw_input.rs` ~2.8k, `crates/gterminal/src/protocol/wire.rs` ~2.2k, `crates/gterminal/src/protocol/render_ansi.rs` ~1.9k, `crates/gterminal/src/pane/osc.rs` ~1.5k). After the import and cherry-picks, split in this same session before the leaf closes: `crates/gterminal/src/pane/terminal.rs` keeps the public type and splits render/I/O into `crates/gterminal/src/pane/terminal_render.rs` and `crates/gterminal/src/pane/terminal_io.rs`; `crates/gterminal/src/raw_input.rs` extracts the framer into `crates/gterminal/src/raw_input_framer.rs`; `crates/gterminal/src/protocol/wire.rs` extracts types and codec into `crates/gterminal/src/protocol/wire_types.rs` and `crates/gterminal/src/protocol/wire_codec.rs`; `crates/gterminal/src/protocol/render_ansi.rs` extracts blit into `crates/gterminal/src/protocol/render_ansi_blit.rs`. Public APIs stay on the original modules. No imported file under `crates/gterminal/src/` may remain at or above 1,000 lines.

**Acceptance:**

- 1.2.1 - `cargo build -p gobby-terminal` and `cargo clippy -p gobby-terminal --all-targets -- -D warnings` pass on macOS and Linux, which requires `crates/gterminal` to be present in the root workspace `members` list. file: `crates/gterminal/Cargo.toml`.
- 1.2.2 - The imported pane/terminal, protocol, and input test corpora pass under nextest. test: `crates/gterminal/src/pane/terminal.rs::grapheme_cluster_mode_is_default_and_survives_full_reset`.
- 1.2.3 - No module under `crates/gterminal/src/` references agent detection, plugins, integrations, updater, or herdr persistence concepts (enforced by a source-scan test). test: `crates/gterminal/tests/carve_guard.rs::no_agent_concepts_in_terminal_core`.
- 1.2.4 - The wire protocol version constant is `1` and version negotiation rejects mismatched clients with a typed error. symbol: `protocol::wire::PROTOCOL_VERSION`. file: `crates/gterminal/src/protocol/wire.rs`.
- 1.2.5 - The three named cherry-picks are applied and every remaining `v0.8.0..HEAD` keep-set commit has a recorded accept/reject entry; `UPSTREAM.md` carries the log. file: `crates/gterminal/UPSTREAM.md`.
- 1.2.6 - `cargo build -p gobby-terminal` with default features (no `vt-engine`) and its protocol/input/layout test subset pass with no `zig` on PATH; the pane/ghostty corpus runs under `--features vt-engine`. file: `crates/gterminal/Cargo.toml`.
- 1.2.7 - Every hand-maintained `.rs` under `crates/gterminal/src/` is below 1,000 lines after the in-leaf splits; the public types still live on the original module paths. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/source_size.rs::no_src_file_at_or_above_1000_lines`.
- 1.2.8 - Missing Zig and unsupported targets produce descriptive cargo build failures naming the requirement when `vt-engine` is enabled; with `vt-engine` disabled the build script invokes no Zig. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/build_env.rs::missing_zig_reports_requirement`.

### 1.3 De-agent-ify PaneRuntime and the terminal runtime API [category: code] (depends: 1.2)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `crates/gterminal/src/pane.rs`
- `crates/gterminal/src/runtime/runtime.rs`
- `crates/gterminal/src/pane/terminal.rs`
- `crates/gterminal/src/platform/mod.rs`
- `crates/gterminal/tests/frame_producer.rs`
- `crates/gterminal/tests/source_size.rs`

Herdr welds agent detection into its pane machinery; this deliverable removes it structurally rather than by dead-code deletion:

- `PaneRuntime` (herdr `pane.rs`): remove the embedded ~300-line detection task (`spawn_basic_detection_task`, herdr `pane.rs:623` ff. at the fork point) and the five detection fields (`detection_content_seq`, `full_lifecycle_authority_active`, `detect_reset_notify`, `pending_release`, `detect_handle`). Remove the `crate::detect` and `crate::integration` references (13 + 6 sites) and the two `foreground_job(shell_pid)` touchpoints in `crates/gterminal/src/pane/terminal.rs`.
- The runtime API (herdr `src/terminal/runtime.rs`): drop agent-flavored methods (`begin_graceful_release(Agent)`, `reset_agent_detection`, `agent_osc_title`, `agent_osc_progress`, `detection_text`, `set_full_lifecycle_authority_active`). Generic OSC title/progress reporting stays as terminal metadata under neutral names (`osc_title()`, `osc_progress()`).
- Rendering inversion: replace `render(&mut ratatui::Frame, area, show_cursor)` as the primary output API with a frame-producer surface — `frame_data(cols, rows) -> FrameData` and `dirty_patch() -> TerminalDirtyPatchOutcome` — so the host (3.1) streams frames without holding a Ratatui `Frame`. A `ratatui` helper widget remains for gclient's in-process use.
- `src/platform/`: keep process-kill/job-object/signal operations; drop foreground-process probes that existed only for detection (herdr's macOS/Linux proc-table scanning), shrinking the platform layer accordingly.

Detection of prompts/idle/stalls remains entirely a daemon concern (the shipped `src/gobby/agents/detection/` registry); the host exposes text snapshots (3.2) that the daemon's detectors consume, identical in role to `capture_pane` today.

**Acceptance:**

- 1.3.1 - `PaneRuntime` has no detection fields, tasks, or `detect`/`integration` references; the carve-guard test covers these symbols. file: `crates/gterminal/src/pane.rs`.
- 1.3.2 - The runtime API exposes only terminal-neutral operations (spawn, write, resize, scroll, encode-key/mouse, snapshot, frame production, kill) with OSC title/progress under neutral names. file: `crates/gterminal/src/runtime/runtime.rs`.
- 1.3.3 - A terminal can be driven end-to-end (spawn shell → write → produce `FrameData` → resize → kill) without any Ratatui `Frame` in the call path. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/frame_producer.rs::end_to_end_without_ratatui_frame`.
- 1.3.4 - Edits in this leaf do not push any `crates/gterminal/src/**/*.rs` file to or above 1,000 lines; further splits land here if they would. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/source_size.rs::no_src_file_at_or_above_1000_lines`.

### 1.4 Register gterminal in CI, nextest, and index exclusions [category: config] (depends: 1.2)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:

- `rust-ci.yml` is a flat sequence of hand-written per-package steps in a single `check` job (no `strategy.matrix`): add `gobby-terminal` clippy/nextest steps mirroring the existing per-package pattern (`gobby-client` follows in 3.6). Zig 0.15 setup (pinned by version) is added only ahead of steps that build with `--features vt-engine`; the default-features step runs Zig-free (C1). Keep the Windows cross-lint (`cargo clippy --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --features vt-engine` with `LIBGHOSTTY_VT_SIMD=false`) as a compile-only gate. Adjacent fix landed with the same edit: `gobby-daemon` currently has no dedicated clippy/nextest step and is missing from both `paths:` trigger lists — add its step and the gdaemon release workflow to the triggers rather than leaving the gap beside the new entries.
- `.config/nextest.toml`: new test-group `gterm-pty` (`max-threads = 1`) for PTY-spawning tests, with a per-package override — the same shape as the existing `serial-db` group (the 2-thread `ghook-process` group is the wrong template for serialized PTY tests).
- `crates/CLAUDE.md`: extend the crate → binary map (`gobby-terminal` → `gterm`, `gobby-client` → `gclient`) and the rebuild-and-reinstall rule.
- Code-index exclusions: the Python `CodeIndexConfig.exclude_patterns` field was deleted in #19400 (zero consumers; `tests/config/test_removed_config_fields.py:52` pins its rejection), so there is no Python half to verify. The code change is gcode's: add `vendor` to `DEFAULT_EXCLUDES` (`crates/gcode/src/index/indexer/util.rs:9`), which lacks it today, and fix that constant's stale doc comment claiming parity with the removed Python defaults. gwiki's own exclude list already carries `vendor`, so this edit ends the gcode/gwiki disagreement and the 1,259-file vendored tree never enters the index. `.gitattributes` currently holds three unrelated entries; this deliverable **appends** the `crates/gterminal/vendor/** linguist-vendored` line.

**Acceptance:**

- 1.4.1 - CI builds and tests `gobby-terminal` via flat per-package steps, with Zig set up only for `vt-engine` steps, a Zig-free default-features step, a dedicated `gobby-daemon` step, corrected `paths:` triggers, and a passing Windows cross-lint. file: `.github/workflows/rust-ci.yml`.
- 1.4.2 - PTY tests run in the serialized `gterm-pty` nextest group (`max-threads = 1`, `serial-db` shape). file: `.config/nextest.toml`.
- 1.4.3 - gcode's `DEFAULT_EXCLUDES` carries `vendor` with its doc comment corrected, gwiki's existing exclusion is unchanged, and `.gitattributes` gains the appended `crates/gterminal/vendor/**` linguist-vendored entry beside its three existing lines. Full discovery and explicit-file routing both skip files below a `vendor/` directory while a neighboring source file remains indexable. file: `.gitattributes`. test: `crates/gcode/src/index/indexer/tests/explicit_routing.rs`.
- 1.4.4 - `crates/CLAUDE.md` documents the two new crate/binary mappings. file: `crates/CLAUDE.md`.

### 1.5 Licensing and provenance artifacts [category: docs] (depends: 1.2)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `crates/gterminal/NOTICE.md`
- `crates/gterminal/UPSTREAM.md`
- `crates/gterminal/LICENSE`
- `crates/gterminal/Cargo.toml`

Follow the repo's vendoring precedent (`src/gobby/install/shared/skills/impeccable/NOTICE.md` — in-directory NOTICE with `## Upstream` and `## Modifications from upstream` sections):

- `crates/gterminal/LICENSE`: Apache-2.0 text. The herdr-derived crates keep Apache-2.0 identity inside the FSL-1.1-ALv2 repo; each derived crate's `Cargo.toml` declares `license = "Apache-2.0"`.
- `crates/gterminal/NOTICE.md`: attribution for herdr (Apache-2.0, upstream `herdrdev/herdr` at the `v0.8.0` release tag whose changelog records the relicense; note that upstream ships no NOTICE file and the contributor-consent caveat), libghostty-vt (MIT, © 2024 Mitchell Hashimoto and Ghostty contributors), portable-pty (MIT, © 2018 Wez Furlong), plus the nested-license inventory of the vendored Ghostty `pkg/` tree (afl++, sentry, breakpad, etc. — present in source, not compiled into lib-vt builds).
- `crates/gterminal/UPSTREAM.md`: the fork manifest — every imported herdr module mapped to its upstream path at the fork point, the not-imported list, the rebrand rules (env vars, socket names, protocol version), and the **cherry-pick log**: the three fixes applied at import plus one accept/reject line per remaining `v0.8.0..HEAD` keep-set commit (1.2). This is a fork record, not a tracking contract — there is no re-pin procedure (C1).
`gclient`'s own NOTICE and manifest license declaration belong to 3.3, the deliverable that creates `crates/gclient/Cargo.toml`; this deliverable owns only artifacts that exist at its execution point, and pinning the client's licensing here would require depending on a later phase. For the same reason this deliverable depends on 1.2 rather than 1.1: it edits `crates/gterminal/Cargo.toml` to declare the license, and 1.2 is the deliverable that creates that manifest. 1.1 alone would let the license work run against a directory with no crate in it.

**Acceptance:**

- 1.5.1 - A NOTICE file exists for `gobby-terminal` with Upstream and Modifications sections and the vendored nested-license inventory. file: `crates/gterminal/NOTICE.md`.
- 1.5.2 - `UPSTREAM.md` maps every imported module to its herdr path at the `v0.8.0` fork point and carries the cherry-pick log with no re-pin procedure. file: `crates/gterminal/UPSTREAM.md`.
- 1.5.3 - `gobby-terminal` declares `license = "Apache-2.0"` in its manifest. file: `crates/gterminal/Cargo.toml`.

## P2: Durable Terminal Resource and TerminalRuntime Contract

`kind: framing`

**Goal**: One durable `terminals` resource and one backend-neutral daemon contract; tmux behavior wrapped with zero behavior change; every consumer resolves terminals through the contract instead of tmux names.

**This phase is a chain, not a fan-out.** 2.1 → 2.2 → 2.3 → 2.4 → 2.5 run strictly in order, and the dependency annotations say so. The reason is concrete rather than stylistic: the five leaves are not target-disjoint. 2.4 and 2.5 both edit `src/gobby/servers/websocket/handlers/core.py`, `src/gobby/servers/websocket/tmux.py`, and `src/gobby/servers/routes/attention.py`; 2.4's reaper and 2.5's discovery both consume the spawn-outcome and in-doubt rules 2.3 establishes; and every leaf from 2.2 onward converts fixtures through the single helper 2.1 introduces. Run as siblings on a shared `depends: 2.2`, two of them would edit the same WebSocket modules and the same shared fake concurrently, and each would be asserting against a half-migrated version of the other's behavior. Serializing costs parallelism inside one phase and buys one owner per file per moment; the phase is still parallel with P1, which shares no targets with it.

New table, landed as a gcore baseline-schema edit per C1 — the DDL below goes into `crates/gcore/assets/schema/baseline.sql`, `BASELINE_CHECKSUM` in `crates/gcore/src/schema/assets.rs` is refreshed, and `catalog.manifest.json` plus the Python identity mirror `src/gobby/storage/schema_expected_identity.json` are updated in the same change:

```sql
CREATE TABLE terminals (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    backend TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (backend IN ('tmux', 'native')),
    ownership TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (ownership IN ('gobby', 'external')),
    state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (state IN ('pending', 'live', 'exited', 'orphaned')),
    spawn_key TEXT,                  -- caller-allocated pre-effect identity; gobby-owned only
    machine_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES machines(id),
                                     -- placement; same class as agent_runs.machine_id.
                                     -- set from require_machine_id() at create_pending
                                     -- and upsert_external. not user identity.
    locator JSONB,                   -- physical resource, once known; tmux: {socket_path,
                                     --   server_pid, server_start_time, pane_id}, all four
                                     --   from one tmux format read;
                                     -- native: {host_terminal_id} only — never host_socket.
                                     -- Unix path is resolved from TerminalHostConfig.socket_dir
                                     -- at attach time and refused on host_epoch mismatch.
    locator_key TEXT,                -- canonical identity, once known (see below)
    session_name TEXT,               -- refreshable tmux metadata, never identity
    window_id TEXT,                  -- refreshable tmux metadata, never identity
    title TEXT CHECK (title IS NULL OR octet_length(title) <= 1024),
                                     -- refreshable metadata; title_max_bytes = 1024 UTF-8
    host_epoch TEXT,                 -- native only: epoch UUID of the owning gterm host
    unresolved_writes JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
                                     --   action_key -> {at, origin}: independent
                                     --   indeterminate writes awaiting resolution;
                                     --   suppresses only each matching automatic
                                     --   re-emission (2.4)
    automatic_write_quarantined_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    automatic_write_quarantine_action_key TEXT,
                                     --   paired nullable columns. both null =
                                     --   not quarantined. both set atomically
                                     --   before the coordinator lock is
                                     --   released at the 2.4 shutdown-drain
                                     --   deadline; restored together when
                                     --   constructing WriteCoordinator;
                                     --   cleared together only by an
                                     --   effect-specific positive observation
                                     --   of that action_key, or an operator
                                     --   write.
    attempt_generation INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
                                     --   incremented before each retry dispatch (2.3);
                                     --   reaper/reconciler CAS this value so a live
                                     --   retry is not reaped as the previous attempt.
    attempt_started_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
                                     --   attempt-specific clock; refreshed only with
                                     --   attempt_generation. never updated_at.
    process JSONB,                   -- native only: {pgid, start_time}. written on
                                     --   spawn_prepared while still pending (3.1);
                                     --   retained at promote_to_live for killpg.
                                     --   not identity; never part of locator_key.
    rows INTEGER, cols INTEGER,
    project_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id),
                                     --   durable project ownership for REST
                                     --   filtering and client persistence.
                                     --   independent of session_id / agent_run_id.
    session_id UUID REFERENCES sessions(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
    agent_run_id UUID REFERENCES agent_runs(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    liveness_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    CONSTRAINT terminals_locator_present_when_attachable CHECK (
        state IN ('pending', 'exited') OR (locator IS NOT NULL AND locator_key IS NOT NULL)
    ),
    CONSTRAINT terminals_locator_pair_consistent CHECK (
        (locator IS NULL) = (locator_key IS NULL)
    ),
    CONSTRAINT terminals_spawn_key_matches_ownership CHECK (
        (ownership = 'gobby' AND spawn_key IS NOT NULL)
        OR (ownership = 'external' AND spawn_key IS NULL)
    ),
    CONSTRAINT terminals_external_is_never_pending CHECK (
        ownership = 'gobby' OR state <> 'pending'
    ),
    CONSTRAINT terminals_host_epoch_is_native_only CHECK (
        host_epoch IS NULL OR backend = 'native'
    ),
    CONSTRAINT terminals_native_attachable_has_epoch CHECK (
        backend <> 'native' OR state NOT IN ('live', 'orphaned') OR host_epoch IS NOT NULL
    ),
    CONSTRAINT terminals_process_is_native_only CHECK (
        process IS NULL OR backend = 'native'
    ),
    CONSTRAINT terminals_pending_has_no_identity CHECK (
        state <> 'pending'
        OR (locator IS NULL AND locator_key IS NULL AND host_epoch IS NULL)
    ),
    CONSTRAINT terminals_external_always_has_locator CHECK (
        ownership = 'gobby' OR (locator IS NOT NULL AND locator_key IS NOT NULL)
    ),
    CONSTRAINT terminals_quarantine_pair_consistent CHECK (
        (automatic_write_quarantined_at IS NULL)
        = (automatic_write_quarantine_action_key IS NULL)
    )
);
CREATE INDEX idx_terminals_run ON terminals(agent_run_id) WHERE agent_run_id IS NOT NULL;
CREATE INDEX idx_terminals_machine ON terminals(machine_id);
CREATE INDEX idx_terminals_project ON terminals(project_id);
CREATE INDEX idx_terminals_live ON terminals(state) WHERE state IN ('pending', 'live');
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_terminals_locator_key_active
    ON terminals(locator_key) WHERE locator_key IS NOT NULL AND state IN ('pending', 'live');
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_terminals_spawn_key
    ON terminals(spawn_key) WHERE spawn_key IS NOT NULL;
```

**The state constraint has to admit a terminal that never existed.** Requiring a locator for every non-`pending` row contradicts the spawn-outcome matrix 2.3 owns: a definitive pre-effect failure and a cancellation between row creation and backend spawn both produce an `exited` row for a resource that was never created, so there is no locator to record and inventing a synthetic one would make the partial UNIQUE index collide on the placeholder. `exited` therefore joins `pending` as a state where the locator may be null, and `live`/`orphaned` still require it — those are exactly the states something can be attached to or reaped from. The complementary rule is enforced in code rather than in SQL because "was ever set" is not expressible in a row-local CHECK: `promote_to_live` is the only writer of `locator`/`locator_key`, and nothing ever nulls them, so a row that reached `live` keeps its locator through `exited` and a post-mortem can always name the resource that ran. The acceptance below pins both halves — the permitted null and the forbidden clear.

**`spawn_key` is a property of a spawn, and discovery has no spawn.** An externally discovered pane is observed already running; there was no caller to allocate a pre-effect name, and the row is inserted straight to `live` with a locator by `upsert_external`. A globally `NOT NULL` `spawn_key` would force discovery to fabricate a value that means nothing and that the reaper would then try to resolve against the backend. So the column is nullable and the CHECK ties it to ownership: `gobby`-owned rows must carry one (they were spawned), `external` rows must not (they were found). The UNIQUE index becomes partial to match, so any number of external rows coexist while gobby spawn keys stay unique.

**Every row-local promise is a constraint, not a comment.** Two invariants this section states in prose were previously enforced only by the code paths that happen to obey them, which meant a direct `INSERT` — a migration backfill, a repair script, a test fixture, a future deliverable's shortcut — could write a row the prose calls impossible. A `pending` row carries **no** physical identity at all: `locator`, `locator_key`, and `host_epoch` are null until the promotion CAS fills them, because none of those values exists before the backend effect. An `external` row carries a locator in **every** state, `exited` included: discovery observes an already-running pane, so there is no pre-effect window in which an external row could lack one, and an external row without a locator would describe a resource nobody can name. Both are now `CHECK` constraints, so the row-local cross-product of state, backend, ownership, and identity is closed at the table rather than at its callers, and 2.1.14 tests each illegal shape by direct insert rather than through a manager method that would refuse it anyway.

`locator_key` is the canonical identity of the physical resource, and the partial UNIQUE index above is what makes discovery idempotent under concurrency. Its form is fixed: `tmux:<socket_path>:<server_pid>:<server_start_time>:<pane_id>` and `native:<host_epoch>:<host_terminal_id>`. Native identity never includes a Unix socket path.

`machine_id` is required on every row, filled from `require_machine_id()` at `create_pending` and `upsert_external`. It is placement (where the PTY/tmux lives), the same column `agent_runs` already has. It is not a `user_id`. `project_id` is required on every row and is a different axis: durable project ownership for `GET /api/terminals` filtering and gclient workspace restore. `session_id` and `agent_run_id` are nullable `ON DELETE SET NULL`, so neither machine nor project may be derived through those links. Do not add `user_id`. #19650 has landed (`machines` is in the baseline): rebase this DDL onto that identity work; do not revert `users` / `machines` / `auth_sessions`. If #18879 (workflow-definition split) has landed, rebase onto that baseline the same way: add `terminals` and replace `agent_runs.tmux_session_name`; do not revert the domain tables (`rule_definitions`, `agent_definitions`, …). Both epics edit `baseline.sql` / checksum / catalog; neither owns the other's tables.

**The daemon runtime role must be able to use the table.** Baseline grants existing relations to `gobby_daemon_runtime` individually. `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES … ON TABLES` later in the same script does **not** retroactively cover `terminals` created above it. This leaf therefore adds, in the same grant section as the other hub tables:

```sql
GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON TABLE terminals TO gobby_daemon_runtime;
```

`2.1.17` still denies the scoped gcode role. The isolated schema privilege inventory in `crates/gcore/src/schema/runner_tests.rs` must assert all four runtime privileges after `gdaemon schema apply`. Title bytes are capped at persist: `title_max_bytes` is **1024** UTF-8 (`octet_length(title) <= 1024`); writers truncate on a code-point boundary rather than failing an OSC flood. 3.2 uses the same constant in the host `list` envelope proof.

**Attach locators are computed, not stored paths.** `attach_locator` for native resolves `gterm-frames.sock` from local `TerminalHostConfig.socket_dir` and refuses when the live host's epoch does not match the row. The roster/REST `AttachLocator` is a request-time handle. A durable `host_socket` in the hub row would attach the wrong machine's host after Stage 3.

**Every frame attach carries a request-time `frame_host_epoch`.** Both frame clients (3.3, 4.1) must compare `Welcome.host_epoch` before `AttachTerminal`. Native rows store `host_epoch` and that value is the expected epoch. Tmux rows are required to keep `host_epoch` null, but their frames still come from the supervised `gterm` host (3.4). `AttachLocator.frame_host_epoch` is therefore filled at request time: native from the row, tmux from the currently adopted/supervised host's epoch (the same value `host_manager` recorded on the last successful `ping`/`hello`). It is never persisted on a tmux row. A tmux attach whose live host epoch does not match `frame_host_epoch` is refused typed the same way a native epoch mismatch is. Host replacement tests pin both backends.

**Identity contains only what the owner cannot rename.** An earlier form of this key also carried `session_name` and `window_id`, which is wrong in both directions and breaks under ordinary tmux use. A `rename-session` changes the session name of the very same pane, so the next discovery computes a different key and inserts a *second* row for one physical terminal while the first goes stale; `break-pane` and `move-pane` change the window (and possibly the session) of an unchanged pane the same way; and a grouped session gives one pane two names at once, so two viewers of the same pane could disagree about its identity. `pane_id` (`%12`) is the value tmux itself treats as the pane's handle: it is assigned at creation, survives rename, move, break, and layout changes, and is never reassigned while the server lives. Identity is therefore socket path + server generation + pane id, and nothing else. Session name, window id, and title move into ordinary refreshable columns: they are re-read on every discovery pass and liveness tick, they are what the roster and the UI display, and they are never compared to decide whether two observations are the same terminal. The acceptance mutation-tests each one — rename, move, break-pane, and a grouped alias must all leave the row's identity and its `terminal_id` unchanged while updating the metadata.

The tmux server identity is part of the key because tmux reuses pane ids (`%12`) after a server restart — without it, a recycled pane id would collide with a stale row for a different physical pane. The pid alone is insufficient for the same reason `sessions.terminal_context` already stores `parent_create_time` beside `parent_pid`: the operating system reuses pids, so a tmux server that dies and restarts on the same socket while the daemon is down can be handed its predecessor's pid, and a pid-only key would then treat a brand-new pane as the old physical terminal. The key therefore carries a **server generation**: the tmux server's start time.

**The generation is read from tmux, not from the process table, so it cannot be a hybrid.** Deriving the generation from the OS — `ps -o lstart=` or `/proc/<pid>/stat` field 22, the source the existing `parent_create_time` guard uses — would require two reads of two different subsystems, and the server can die between them: the pid comes from a live tmux, the start time comes from whatever now owns that pid (or from nothing), and the resulting locator describes no server that ever existed. No amount of retry logic fixes a two-source read; it only narrows the window. tmux removes the need entirely, because it renders the generation itself: `#{pid}` is the server pid, `#{start_time}` is the server start time as Unix seconds, and `#{socket_path}` is the socket, all three expanded from one server-state read in one `display-message -p` invocation (verified on tmux 3.7b: `1658|1784592177|/private/tmp/tmux-501/default`). Every producer of a tmux locator therefore obtains socket path, server pid, server start time, and `pane_id` from a **single** `display-message` expansion — 2.5's two discovery producers and 3.4's attach path all obey this — and none may assemble a key from separately sourced parts. A server that dies before the read makes the whole command fail, so there is no partial result to misinterpret, and the acceptances test the same-pid/different-start-time case rather than only a changed pid.

**A stored generation is revalidated at every dereference, not just at discovery.** A correct locator still goes stale: while the daemon is down, a tmux server can exit and a replacement can claim the same socket path and hand out the same `pane_id`, and an attach that targets only socket plus pane id would then bind a stored row to a stranger's pane. So generation is not merely recorded — it is re-read and compared. Because it costs nothing to include, `#{pid}` and `#{start_time}` ride in the same batched expansion as the pane's own state on **every** poll (3.4) and on every liveness tick (2.5), and a mismatch against the stored `locator` is **confirmed absence**: the host emits `TerminalExited`, no frames are delivered, and the daemon calls `mark_exited` (`live → exited`). Rediscovery of that physical pane then creates a new row rather than reviving this one. `mark_orphaned` is reserved for native host-epoch / host-crash loss (3.1); a tmux generation mismatch must not write durable `orphaned`. The residual is stated rather than hidden, and it is stated as a full tuple: a collision needs the OS to reuse the exact pid for a new tmux server **and** the same socket path **and** the same one-second `start_time` value **and** the same `pane_id`. All four coincide more readily than the pid term alone suggests — a live probe on tmux 3.7b restarted a server twice inside one second and got back the same socket path, the same `start_time`, and the same `%0` — so the pane-id existence check in the same read is *not* a backstop for this case: when the whole tuple matches, the existence check passes, the stale row binds the replacement server's pane, and there is no typed error and no detected mismatch. That outcome is explicitly accepted rather than claimed to be detected. The alternative was weighed and declined: a socket inode plus creation time would discriminate a replacement server, but reading it means a `stat` of the socket path at every dereference — a second subsystem read added to a locator whose whole correctness argument is that it comes from one tmux expansion, which is precisely the hybrid the paragraph above rejects. tmux itself exposes no finer generation than one-second `start_time`, so the honest position is the accepted residual: a same-pid, same-socket, same-second, same-pane restart binds silently, and everything coarser than that tuple is detected.

Two concurrent discoverers of the same pane (SessionStart and the liveness monitor, 2.5) converge on one row via `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (locator_key) WHERE locator_key IS NOT NULL AND state IN ('pending','live') DO UPDATE`, which is safe to replay **when they share a project**; a terminal that has reached `exited`/`orphaned` leaves the partial index, so the same physical locator can be rediscovered later as a new row rather than resurrecting a finished one.

**Active locator ownership is one project.** The active `locator_key` index is global, so two projects cannot each hold a live row for the same pane. `project_id` is written only on insert. Same-project replay and same-project concurrency still converge: metadata (session name, window id, title) refreshes and `project_id` is unchanged. A different project's `upsert_external` — sequential or concurrent with the occupying writer — does **not** `UPDATE`, does **not** insert a second active row, and does **not** return the occupying row's `terminal_id`, title, session, or other fields. It is a typed `project_ownership_conflict`. Only after the occupying row has left `pending|live` may another project insert a new row for that locator. Discovery therefore never transfers or leaks a live resource across projects.

**Pre-effect identity: `spawn_key` (gobby-owned spawns).** A row written before the backend effect cannot carry the locator of a resource that does not exist yet — the native `host_terminal_id` and the tmux window/pane ids are outputs of the spawn — so `locator` and `locator_key` are null while `pending` and are filled atomically by the `pending → live` CAS, guarded by the state-sensitive CHECK above. Nullability alone would still leave a hole: a tmux session created microseconds before the spawn call times out is a live resource that no row can name, so the stale-pending reaper could never find it. `spawn_key` closes that hole by being allocated by the caller *before* the effect and mandated as the backend's creation name. For tmux it is the session name the runtime must pass to `new-session`, deterministically derived as `gobby-<terminal_id>`; for native it is the `terminal_id` itself, which the host stores as opaque metadata from control `spawn` and echoes in `list` (3.1). The reaper therefore resolves any pending row to its possible effect by name on either backend — `has-session -t gobby-<terminal_id>` or a `list` scan — and kills what it finds. Runtimes may not choose their own names, and the UNIQUE index makes reuse of a `spawn_key` a database error rather than a silent collision.

`agent_runs.tmux_session_name` is replaced by `agent_runs.terminal_id UUID REFERENCES terminals(id)` (same baseline edit; no alias — C1). `sessions.terminal_context` **stays** for its non-locator roles (PID-reuse-safe kill via `parent_pid`/`parent_create_time`, handoff identity, `term_program`/`tty`/`cwd`); its tmux-locator keys seed `external`-ownership terminal rows during discovery (2.5).

`src/gobby/storage/terminals.py`: `TerminalManager` with hub-transaction CRUD (psycopg `%s` placeholders), `create_pending(terminal_id, machine_id, project_id, backend, ownership, spawn_key, rows, cols, ...)` called at spawn-begin with no locator, `promote_to_live(terminal_id, locator, locator_key, host_epoch)` which is the single CAS that fills the physical identity and flips `pending → live` in one statement, an idempotent `upsert_external(locator_key, machine_id, project_id, ...)` for discovery, and reap queries (`list_stale_pending`, `list_orphaned_by_epoch`, `list_live_by_epoch`, `list_live_by_machine`). Creating the row **before** the backend spawn closes today's orphan class (`docs/reviews/agents.md:162`: failed spawns leave tmux sessions no monitor reaps because no run row carries the name). `project_id` is required at both writers: agent spawn copies it from the parent session, web `terminal_create` copies the connection project, and discovery copies the discovering session's project **only when it is inserting**. A same-project replay does not rewrite `project_id`. A different-project discovery of an active locator is `project_ownership_conflict` and writes nothing. A row without a project is a constraint error.

**The transition surface is an edge allowlist, not a generic setter.** The natural generalization of today's `clear_tmux_session_name` compare-and-clear is a public `transition(terminal_id, expected_state, new_state)`, and it is the wrong shape: a caller that passes `expected_state='exited', new_state='live'` gets a successful CAS and resurrects a finished terminal, which directly contradicts the rediscovery rule above — a locator that reappears must produce a *new* row, never revive the old one. The same setter also lets `orphaned → live` re-adopt a terminal whose host epoch is gone, and lets any state go back to `pending`. Row-local CHECKs cannot catch these, because each individual row is perfectly legal before and after; only the *edge* is illegal. So the CAS primitive is private, a module-level `_ALLOWED_EDGES` frozenset is the whole specification, and the public surface is exactly the five transitions the lifecycle needs: `promote_to_live` (`pending → live`), `fail_pending` (`pending → exited`), `mark_exited` (`live → exited`, `orphaned → exited`), and `mark_orphaned` (`live → orphaned`). `exited` is absorbing and nothing returns to `pending`. Anything outside the set raises rather than writing, so an illegal transition is a programming error caught in tests instead of a silently resurrected row.

**The indeterminate-write latch set lives on the row because the abstain rule outlives the call.** `unresolved_writes` is an empty object in the ordinary case and maps each logical `action_key` to `{at, origin}`. The coordinator persists that entry as a **write-ahead** marker under the per-terminal lock **before** the first non-idempotent backend dispatch for that key (2.2, 2.4). Waiting until `IndeterminateWrite` is returned is not enough: a hard process death after PTY bytes land and before the runtime result or a post-effect latch write leaves no durable record, and the next automatic tick can repeat an action whose delivered-and-consumed state is observationally identical to non-delivery. A single object-valued latch would be lossy: a second independent indeterminate action could overwrite the first and silently re-enable its automatic retry. The map exists here rather than in daemon memory because the consumers that must not resend are periodic, and a daemon restart between a dispatched write and the next tick must preserve every unresolved action. Clearing is per key rather than time-based — a TTL would re-enable the duplicate it prevents — so a definitive `Delivered` or a no-effect typed failure that is durably processed, a positive observation, or an operator resolution removes the matching key, while cancellation after dispatch, indeterminate settlement, process death, and terminal exit retain or clear as specified in 2.2.

**The map is finite.** A no-TTL latch that admits every distinct `action_key` would grow without bound on a long-lived terminal, and once the JSONB value can no longer be stored the coordinator could not record the indeterminate outcome it is required to persist before exposing it. Validated limits, owned by `WriteCoordinator` and enforced again at `TerminalManager` persist:

- `unresolved_write_action_key_max_bytes` = **256** UTF-8 bytes (empty keys are refused)
- `unresolved_write_max_entries` = **32** distinct keys
- `unresolved_write_max_serialized_bytes` = **65536** (64 KiB) encoded JSONB

Before dispatching any write that would **insert** a new key, the coordinator reserves one slot and the resulting serialized size under the per-terminal lock, then **writes the `{at, origin}` marker** for that key before the backend `write_*` await starts. An existing key (already latched, or an operator/attention write that will only refresh `{at, origin}`) needs no new slot but still refreshes the write-ahead marker before a new dispatch of that same key. If the key is too long, the map is at 32 entries, or the reserved serialization would exceed 64 KiB, the coordinator returns typed `unresolved_write_capacity` and performs **no** PTY mutation and **no** latch write. Operator resolution of an existing key and terminal-exit clearing remain available at capacity. A daemon restart restores the bounded map as stored; it does not admit a 33rd key. A restored write-ahead key whose effect never settled still suppresses matching automatic retry.

**Terminal-wide automatic-write quarantine is a separate durable bit.** `unresolved_writes` still suppresses only matching `action_key`s; a third automatic action is allowed. When 2.4's shutdown drain times out and the coordinator must release the per-terminal lock while a backend effect remains indeterminate, that is not enough: any other automatic action could then dispatch against the same PTY. `automatic_write_quarantined_at` and `automatic_write_quarantine_action_key` are therefore set together on the row **before** that lock release — the timestamp is when, the key is *which* write forced the drain — and restored together when `WriteCoordinator` is constructed after restart. They are cleared together only by an **effect-specific positive observation** of that stored `action_key`, or by an operator write. Observing a different unresolved key, or an unchanged or ambiguous snapshot — including the delivered-and-consumed versus never-delivered pair 2.4 already says can look identical — leaves both columns set. A generic health check or any snapshot that merely succeeded is not resolution. Both columns null means not quarantined. Terminal exit clears them with the latch map.

**One row owner per spawn attempt.** `create_pending` and the `pending → live` CAS belong to the spawn *caller* (`execute_spawn`, 2.3), never to a `TerminalRuntime` implementation. Runtimes receive an already-allocated `terminal_id` and return a backend locator; they never insert or transition rows. This keeps exactly one durable row and one transition owner per attempt on every path — success, backend failure, timeout, and cancellation — and it is the reason 2.3 and 4.1 cannot each create a row for the same spawn.

Removing `agent_runs.tmux_session_name` reaches further than the spawn path: `_lifecycle.py` clears it in four terminal transitions, `_cleanup.py` uses `tmux_session_name IS NULL` as the "never started" predicate and in its stale-run classification, and `_queries.py`, `_selectors.py`, and `_termination.py` each filter or project it. Every one of those becomes a `terminals`-row join or CAS in this deliverable; the consumer-side sweep outside storage is 2.4.

**Shared test seams migrate here, and "unchanged" means behavior, not source.** Deleting a persisted field breaks every fixture that sets it, so no deliverable in P2 may claim existing suites pass *untouched*; what must be preserved is each suite's observable assertions.

**The inventory is the whole indexed match set, not a sample.** `gcode grep -w tmux_session_name src tests web crates` is the authoritative inventory command, and at this repair pass it returns **450 matched lines across 89 code files**. The command is the contract; the implementer re-runs it before starting and treats any file it returns that no section names as belonging to this deliverable by default. (The raw sweep also matches this plan, `.gobby/memories.jsonl`, and generated `wiki/` pages; those are not code and are excluded by the path scope above.) The heaviest test concentrations remain `tests/agents/test_lifecycle_monitor.py` (96), `tests/agents/test_lifecycle_monitor_watchdog_idle_recovery.py` (31), `tests/agents/test_lifecycle_monitor_extra.py` (15), `tests/test_runner_lifecycle_restart_replay.py` (10), `tests/agents/tmux/test_pane_monitor.py` (10), `tests/agents/test_memory_watchdog.py` (9). Production site `src/gobby/runner_init/orchestration.py` (9) is migrated here alongside the storage package.

**A long tail is not an assignment, so the tail is enumerated.** Describing the remainder as "one-to-three-site files across `tests/agents/`, `tests/mcp_proxy/`, and `tests/e2e/`" reads like coverage but assigns nothing, and a re-run of the sweep at review time found seventeen code files — 37 sites — that no deliverable in this plan named anywhere in its text. They are all test modules, they all break the moment the column is dropped, and they are owned here with the rest of the fixture conversion: `tests/test_runner_lifecycle_restart_replay.py` (10), `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/spawn_agent/test_initial_variables.py` (4), `tests/mcp_proxy/test_wait_for_output.py` (3), `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/test_agent_cancellation.py` (3), `tests/agents/test_spawn_executor_droid.py` (2), `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/spawn_agent/test_health.py` (2), `tests/storage/test_agent_capture.py` (2), `tests/storage/test_storage_agents.py` (2), `tests/agents/test_lifecycle_monitor_completion.py` (1), `tests/agents/test_srt_spawn.py` (1), `tests/agents/test_task_recovery.py` (1), `tests/e2e/test_build_dispatcher_autonomy.py` (1), `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/spawn_agent/test_dedup.py` (1), `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/spawn_agent/test_runtime.py` (1), `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/test_cancel_stale_helpers.py` (1), `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/test_spawn_agent_impl_provider.py` (1), and `tests/storage/test_local_model_flags.py` (1). Added at the repair pass and owned here unless a later leaf already names them: `tests/servers/routes/test_config_startup_stragglers.py` (3), `tests/tasks/test_plan_gate.py` (1), `tests/test_runner_shutdown.py` (1), `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/test_spawn_agent_speed.py` (1), `tests/config/test_live_policy_consumers.py` (1), `tests/agents/test_tmux.py` (1), `tests/agents/test_terminal_prompt_monitor.py` (1), `tests/agents/test_lifecycle_task_completion.py` (1). The command is the contract; this list is its value at the repair pass.

Rather than several hundred bespoke rewrites — which would also let two later leaves edit the same shared fake in conflicting ways — this deliverable introduces one helper, `tests/agents/terminal_fixtures.py`, exposing `make_live_terminal(run, backend="tmux", ...)` and `make_pending_terminal(run, ...)`, and converts every call site in the inventory to it. Every converted test keeps its original assertions: a test that asserted "run has no tmux session after cleanup" now asserts "the run's terminal row left `pending|live`", and a test that distinguished never-started from started keeps that distinction through row presence. The backend-neutral server and frontend wire fakes are the parallel case and belong to 2.5, which owns the message rename; 2.2, 2.3, and 2.4 consume this helper and add no competing factory.

**The zero-match gate belongs to the last owner, not the first.** An earlier draft asserted here that after this deliverable the only permitted matches anywhere in `src`, `tests`, and `web` are inside the tmux backend module, which cannot be true and is not even desirable: 2.3, 2.4, and 2.5 each still migrate readers this deliverable deliberately does not touch, so a whole-repo gate at 2.1 either fails honestly or is satisfied by 2.1 doing work the chain assigned elsewhere. The gate is therefore split by ownership. 2.1's acceptance sweeps **only its own slice** — the storage package, `src/gobby/runner_init/orchestration.py`, and the enumerated fixture-bearing test modules — and permits matches everywhere else. The whole-repo zero-match sweep is 2.5's acceptance, because 2.5 is the last leaf in the P2 chain; it is the single place where "no site anywhere still reads the removed field" is both checkable and true.

**Acceptance:**

- 2.1.1 - The gcore baseline creates `terminals` with backend/ownership/state checks and indexes, `BASELINE_CHECKSUM` and both manifests are updated in the same change, and an isolated `gdaemon schema apply`/startup test applies it cleanly. file: `crates/gcore/assets/schema/baseline.sql`.
- 2.1.2 - `agent_runs` carries `terminal_id`; `tmux_session_name` is gone from schema, models, and runtime persistence. file: `src/gobby/storage/agents/_models.py`.
- 2.1.3 - `TerminalManager` exposes CAS transitions; stale transitions (wrong expected state) affect zero rows and report failure. file: `src/gobby/storage/terminals.py`.
- 2.1.4 - A terminal row created at spawn-begin whose spawn fails is discoverable via `list_stale_pending` for reaping, and its `spawn_key` resolves the possible backend effect by name on both backends. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_failed_spawn_leaves_reapable_pending_row`.
- 2.1.5 - Concurrent and replayed upserts of one physical locator produce exactly one active row; a reused pane id under a different `server_pid` produces a distinct row; a reused pane id under the *same* pid but a different `server_start_time` also produces a distinct row (pid-reuse case); and a row that has left `pending|live` does not block rediscovery of the same locator. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_locator_key_uniqueness_replay_and_server_generation`.
- 2.1.9 - Identity survives every owner-driven metadata mutation: after `rename-session`, `rename-window`, `move-pane`, and `break-pane` on a discovered pane, and when the pane is visible through a grouped-session alias, rediscovery resolves the *same* row and `terminal_id` while `session_name`, `window_id`, and `title` are refreshed to their new values; no mutation creates a second row. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_pane_identity_survives_rename_move_break_and_grouping`.
- 2.1.10 - An `exited` row is insertable and reachable with a null locator (pre-effect failure and cancellation), `live` and `orphaned` are rejected with a null locator, and a row that reached `live` retains its locator through `exited` — no code path clears `locator` or `locator_key` once set. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_exit_without_effect_and_locator_is_never_cleared`.
- 2.1.11 - `upsert_external` inserts a discovered pane with a null `spawn_key`, a `gobby`-owned row without a `spawn_key` is rejected by the CHECK, an `external` row carrying one is rejected, and any number of external rows coexist while gobby spawn keys stay unique. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_spawn_key_domain_matches_ownership`.
- 2.1.12 - Restricted to this deliverable's ownership slice, the `gcode grep -w tmux_session_name src tests web crates` sweep returns zero matches in `src/gobby/storage/agents/`, `src/gobby/runner_init/orchestration.py`, and every fixture-bearing test module named above — including the 86-site lifecycle-monitor suite, the watchdog and pane-monitor suites, the four spawn-agent MCP test modules, the session-coordinator suite, and all seventeen enumerated tail modules — each resolving through terminal rows or the shared fixture helper with its original assertions intact. Matches outside the slice are expected here and are gated by 2.5. test: `tests/terminals/test_no_direct_tmux_consumers.py::test_storage_slice_inventory_is_fully_assigned`.
- 2.1.13 - The generation is single-read and revalidated: a tmux locator is constructed only from one `display-message` expansion carrying `#{socket_path}`, `#{pid}`, `#{start_time}`, and the pane id together, no code path reads a process start time from `ps` or `/proc` to build a `locator_key`, a locator whose stored pid and start time no longer match the live server is refused and `mark_exited`s the row instead of binding, and rediscovery of that physical pane then creates a new row. `mark_orphaned` is not called for a tmux generation mismatch. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_generation_is_single_read_and_revalidated_before_bind`.
- 2.1.14 - Illegal rows and illegal edges are both rejected: a one-sided locator (either field null while the other is set), an `external` row in `pending`, a `tmux` row carrying `host_epoch`, and a `native` row reaching `live` without one are all constraint errors; the edge test is generated from the allowlist complement rather than sampled — the full four-state ordered cross-product (sixteen pairs, self-pairs included) is parameterized, exactly the five allowlisted edges (`pending → live`, `pending → exited`, `live → exited`, `live → orphaned`, `orphaned → exited`) succeed, and the other eleven each raise and leave the stored state byte-identical. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_row_and_edge_constraints_reject_illegal_variants`.
- 2.1.6 - Every removed `tmux_session_name` reader in the storage package resolves through `terminals` instead: the four `_lifecycle.py` clear paths, the `_cleanup.py` never-started and stale-run classifications, and the `_queries.py`/`_selectors.py`/`_termination.py` filters and projections. test: `tests/storage/test_agents_terminal_migration.py::test_storage_readers_use_terminal_rows`.
- 2.1.7 - A `pending` row is insertable with a null locator and a non-null `spawn_key`; the CHECK constraint rejects any insert or transition to `live`/`orphaned` that leaves the locator or `locator_key` null; `promote_to_live` fills locator, `locator_key`, and state in one CAS; and a duplicate non-null `spawn_key` is a database error. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_pending_nullable_locator_and_atomic_promotion`.
- 2.1.8 - The shared terminal fixture helper is the only factory constructing terminal rows in the agent suites, and every converted suite keeps its original behavioral assertions (never-started vs started, cleanup clears the terminal, reused-pid liveness). test: `tests/agents/test_lifecycle_monitor.py::test_terminal_fixture_migration_preserves_assertions`.
- 2.1.15 - Every row carries `machine_id NOT NULL` referencing `machines(id)`, set from `require_machine_id()` at `create_pending` and `upsert_external`; a row without one is a constraint error. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_machine_id_is_required_and_local`.
- 2.1.16 - Native `locator` is `{host_terminal_id}` only — no `host_socket` key — and `attach_locator` computes the Unix path from local host config and refuses an epoch mismatch. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_native_locator_has_no_host_socket`.
- 2.1.20 - `AttachLocator` carries request-time `frame_host_epoch`: a native row supplies its stored `host_epoch`; a tmux row supplies the currently adopted host epoch and still stores `host_epoch` null; both frame clients refuse `Welcome.host_epoch` mismatch before `AttachTerminal`. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_attach_locator_frame_host_epoch_by_backend`.
- 2.1.21 - `automatic_write_quarantined_at` and `automatic_write_quarantine_action_key` persist as a pair: default both null; a set pair survives a `TerminalManager` reload; one-sided writes are a constraint error; clearing both to null is the only storage-level lift. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_automatic_write_quarantine_round_trips`.
- 2.1.22 - Every row carries `project_id NOT NULL` referencing `projects(id)`, set at `create_pending` and `upsert_external`; a row without one is a constraint error; listing by project still isolates after `session_id` and `agent_run_id` are cleared. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_project_id_is_required_and_survives_session_clear`.
- 2.1.23 - Project ownership of an active external locator is immutable: same-project concurrent and sequential `upsert_external` converge on one row without changing `project_id`; a different project's concurrent or sequential discovery is typed `project_ownership_conflict`, mutates zero rows, and exposes none of the occupying row; after the occupying row exits, a different project may insert a new row for that locator. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_external_locator_project_ownership_is_immutable`.
- 2.1.24 - `unresolved_writes` persist rejects an `action_key` over 256 UTF-8 bytes, a 33rd distinct key, and a serialization over 64 KiB; a legal 32-entry map at the byte ceiling round-trips. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_unresolved_writes_are_bounded`.
- 2.1.25 - Isolated schema apply grants `gobby_daemon_runtime` `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, and `DELETE` on `terminals`; the scoped gcode denial in 2.1.17 is unchanged. test: `crates/gcore/src/schema/runner_tests.rs::fresh_baseline_grants_terminals_to_daemon_runtime`.
- 2.1.26 - `title` rejects a persist over `title_max_bytes` (1024 UTF-8); a 1024-byte title round-trips; writers truncate OSC/tmux titles on a code-point boundary before insert or refresh. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_title_is_byte_bounded`.
- 2.1.17 - `terminals` is listed in `explicitly_denied_relations` in `crates/gcode/security/managed_postgres_privileges.json`; the privilege-manifest suite asserts the scoped gcode role cannot `SELECT` it. test: `tests/code_index/test_gcode_privilege_manifest.py::test_terminals_is_denied_to_scoped_gcode`.
- 2.1.18 - `create_pending` writes `attempt_generation = 1` and `attempt_started_at`; a native `record_process` may store `{pgid, start_time}` on a still-`pending` row (process is not identity); `promote_to_live` retains that pair; a tmux row with `process` set is a constraint error. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_attempt_generation_and_native_process_metadata`.
- 2.1.19 - `attempt_started_at` is the only clock `list_stale_pending` and the reconciler use for the in-doubt deadline; an unrelated `updated_at` mutation does not refresh attempt age; incrementing `attempt_generation` refreshes `attempt_started_at` in the same statement. test: `tests/storage/test_terminals.py::test_attempt_started_at_is_attempt_specific`.

### 2.2 TerminalRuntime protocol and TmuxTerminalRuntime [category: code] (depends: 2.1)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `src/gobby/terminals/__init__.py`
- `src/gobby/terminals/runtime.py`
- `src/gobby/terminals/write_coordinator.py`
- `src/gobby/terminals/dimensions.py`
- `src/gobby/terminals/tmux_runtime.py`
- `src/gobby/config/terminals.py`
- `src/gobby/config/app.py::*` — scope-reason: TerminalRuntime contract seam introduction per the body
- `src/gobby/agents/spawners/base.py::*` — scope-reason: TerminalRuntime contract seam introduction per the body
- `src/gobby/agents/spawn_models.py::*` — scope-reason: TerminalRuntime contract seam introduction per the body
- `src/gobby/runner_init/orchestration.py::*` — scope-reason: TerminalRuntime contract seam introduction per the body
- `src/gobby/runner.py::*` — scope-reason: TerminalRuntime contract seam introduction per the body
- `src/gobby/app_context.py::*` — scope-reason: TerminalRuntime contract seam introduction per the body
- `src/gobby/runner_init/servers.py::*` — scope-reason: TerminalRuntime contract seam introduction per the body
- `src/gobby/servers/websocket/server.py::*` — scope-reason: TerminalRuntime contract seam introduction per the body
- `tests/agents/test_tmux.py::*` — scope-reason: injection suites stay green against the wrapped runtime
- `tests/terminals/test_runtime_registry.py`
- `tests/terminals/test_composition_roots.py`
- `tests/terminals/test_dimensions.py`
- `tests/terminals/test_tmux_runtime.py`
- `tests/terminals/test_write_coordinator.py`
- `tests/config/test_terminals.py`

`src/gobby/terminals/runtime.py` defines the contract, grown from the three existing seeds (`TerminalSpawnerBase` in `agents/spawners/base.py`, the `ManagedTmux` Protocol in `agents/capture.py`, and `_resolve_tmux_target` in `mcp_proxy/tools/sessions/_terminal_tmux.py` with the facade wrapper in `_terminal.py`):

```python
class PreparedSpawn:
    terminal_id: UUID
    spawn_key: str
```
