```python
    locator: AttachLocator | None          # tmux: after session create; native: attachable host_terminal_id
    process: ProcessIdentity | None        # native: {pgid, start_time}; tmux: None
    host_terminal_id: str | None

class TerminalRuntime(Protocol):
    backend: Literal["tmux", "native"]

    async def prepare_spawn(self, request: TerminalSpawnRequest) -> PreparedSpawn: ...
    async def commit_spawn(self, prepared: PreparedSpawn) -> TerminalHandle: ...
    async def is_live(self, terminal: Terminal) -> bool: ...
    async def snapshot(self, terminal: Terminal, lines: int = 50) -> SnapshotResult: ...# visible text, detector input
    async def snapshot_full(self, terminal: Terminal) -> SnapshotResult: ...            # scrollback, post-mortem
    async def write_text(self, terminal: Terminal, text: str, submit: bool) -> WriteOutcome: ...
    async def write_key(self, terminal: Terminal, key: NamedKey) -> WriteOutcome: ...   # enter/escape/tab/up/down
    async def write_paste(self, terminal: Terminal, text: str) -> WriteOutcome: ...     # herdr paste_payload
    async def resize(self, terminal: Terminal, rows: int, cols: int) -> None: ...
    async def terminate(self, terminal: Terminal, grace_seconds: float) -> None: ...
    async def attach_locator(self, terminal: Terminal) -> AttachLocator: ...            # request-time handle; native path is computed, never a stored socket
```

`TerminalSpawnRequest` carries the caller-allocated `terminal_id` and `spawn_key` (2.1) plus command/env/cwd/dims/labels, and — after a native `reserve_observer` — the returned `reservation_id` plus that attempt's `reserve_key` (tmux leaves both unset). `PreparedSpawn` is the staged handoff between backend effect and caller acknowledgements; `TerminalHandle` carries that row id plus the backend locator the runtime produced after commit. There is no one-shot `spawn(...)` on the protocol: `execute_spawn` (2.3) always calls native `reserve_observer` when the backend is native (4.2), copies `{reservation_id, reserve_key}` onto the request, then `prepare_spawn`, then `record_process` when `process` is present, then the native observer bind (4.2), then `commit_spawn`. `commit_spawn` is refused typed until those acknowledgements are recorded on the prepared object (tmux records a no-op persist and no observer). `TmuxTerminalRuntime.prepare_spawn` creates the session; `commit_spawn` returns the handle. `SpawnResult` (`agents/spawn_models.py:69`) drops its three tmux fields in favor of `terminal_id` + `AttachLocator`.

**A snapshot that was truncated must say so, all the way down.** Returning `str` throws away the one fact a caller needs to record a faithful artifact: whether it is looking at the whole output or the tail of it. That matters most exactly where it is easiest to lose — capture-before-kill on a long-running agent, whose scrollback is the largest and whose response is the most likely to hit the host's byte ceiling (3.2). Silently storing a short capture as if it were complete is a post-mortem that lies. So the contract returns `SnapshotResult(text: str, truncated: bool, dropped_bytes: int | None, total_bytes: int | None)` and the metadata is carried, not dropped, at every boundary below it: `NativeTerminalRuntime` (4.1) maps the host's `{truncated, dropped_bytes, total_bytes}` straight through, the capture-persistence path in 2.4 stores the flags beside the text so an artifact records its own completeness, and detector inputs ignore the flags but never see a different `text` than they do today.

**The counters are UTF-8 bytes, and a backend that cannot know a value says so.** Two details in that signature decide whether the metadata is trustworthy or merely present. First, the unit: the host bounds its response in *bytes*, so `total_bytes` and `dropped_bytes` count encoded UTF-8 bytes and nothing else. Comparing them against `len(text)` — Python's code-point count — is correct only for ASCII and silently wrong for every box-drawing character, emoji, or CJK glyph a real terminal emits, which is precisely the content most likely to be near the ceiling. Any assertion relating text to counters must therefore encode first (`len(text.encode("utf-8"))`), and truncation must cut on a code-point boundary so `text` is always decodable — a byte-exact cut mid-sequence would make the artifact unreadable to prove it was bounded. Second, the honest unknown: the fields are `int | None` because tmux genuinely cannot answer them. `capture-pane` returns whatever `history-limit` still holds, and lines evicted before the capture ran left no trace — there is no count of them to report, and a `dropped_bytes` of `0` would assert completeness that tmux never verified. So `TmuxTerminalRuntime` reports `truncated=True` with `dropped_bytes=None` whenever the captured history is at the limit (the pane may have lost more), `truncated=False, dropped_bytes=0` only when the history is demonstrably shorter than the limit, and `total_bytes=None` in the at-limit case since the true total is unknowable. `None` means "this backend cannot know", never "nothing was dropped", and consumers render it as unknown rather than as zero. The acceptance in 2.4 pins the persistence half and 4.2 pins an oversized wide-Unicode capture end to end.

**Paste is a write, encoded the way herdr 0.8.0 already does.** `write_paste` takes the clipboard/crossterm paste *text* (not pre-wrapped bytes). The runtime applies herdr's `paste_payload`: if the target's live `MODE_BRACKETED_PASTE` / `#{bracket_paste_flag}` is on, wrap `\x1b[200~{text}\x1b[201~`; otherwise send the raw text. Native encoding happens in the host (it owns VT mode) via control `write` `kind: "paste"`; tmux encoding happens in `TmuxTerminalRuntime`, which reads the pane's `#{bracket_paste_flag}` at write time — the host capture poll (3.4) only runs while a viewer is attached, so a poll-cached flag can be stale or absent — then `send-keys -H`. gclient and the browser never wrap.

**`write_key` uses the same at-write tmux query.** Application-cursor and application-keypad flags change no cell and are not on the daemon's write path unless the runtime reads them. Direct `gclient` attachments deliver those flags only on the host-to-gclient frame stream; `terminal_input` travels separately to the daemon and never carries them. `TmuxTerminalRuntime.write_key` therefore queries `#{cursor_keys_flag}` and `#{keypad_cursor_flag}` (with the paste flag already specified) in one `display-message` under the coordinator lock at dispatch, then encodes the named key from that result: an Up arrow is `ESC O A` in application-cursor mode and `ESC [ A` otherwise; application-keypad vs normal keypad is the same rule for the keypad named keys. The 3.4 poll still emits those flags as frames so observers can render mode changes; it is not the encoder's source. Native `write_key` stays on the host, which already owns VT mode. Cap is herdr's 1 MiB UTF-8 (`MAX_INPUT_PAYLOAD`) measured on the raw text, not on any later transport encoding; oversize is a typed refusal and does not disconnect (herdr #1665, already in the 0.8.0 import). The native control client (4.1) then encodes that raw payload as base64 so the 1 MiB contract stays inside the 2 MiB JSON-line cap (3.2). HEAD after `v0.8.0` did not change `send_paste`/`paste_payload` — do not chase post-fork remote image-clipboard or Windows Terminal multiline paste (#670; ConPTY is out of this epic).

**Both backends can lose the answer, so the write outcome is a type and both runtimes produce it.** `write_text`/`write_key`/`write_paste` are backend primitives: they accept a terminal and payload and return `WriteOutcome` — `Delivered` or `IndeterminateWrite` — with genuine failures still raising typed errors. They do not take `action_key` or `origin`. An early form of this plan defined `IndeterminateWrite` as a native-only outcome on the theory that only a dropped control connection can lose a response. That is wrong, and it was disproved against a live tmux server: a `send-keys` whose reply is withheld leaves the client timing out **after** the keys have already been applied to the pane, so the tmux path produces exactly the same ambiguity through a different mechanism. `TmuxTerminalRuntime` therefore returns `IndeterminateWrite` when its `tmux` invocation times out or its client loses the response, and `NativeTerminalRuntime` (4.1) returns it when a control response is lost across a reconnect; neither retries on its own. Nothing above the contract branches on backend to interpret a write result.

**The latch lives on a coordinator that receives the action identity.** `src/gobby/terminals/write_coordinator.py` is the only layer that may return `IndeterminateWrite` to a daemon caller. It accepts `WriteRequest{terminal_id, action_key, origin: "operator"|"automatic"|"attention", kind: "text"|"key"|"paste", payload}` — that is the identity 2.1's `unresolved_writes` map stores. The coordinator owns the per-terminal lock, **reserves latch capacity** for any new `action_key` (2.1 bounds), then **persists the write-ahead `{at, origin}` marker** under that key **before** invoking the matching `TerminalRuntime.write_*`. A failed reserve is typed `unresolved_write_capacity` with no backend call and no marker. A definitive `Delivered` or a no-effect typed failure, once durably processed, clears that key. Cancellation after dispatch, an `IndeterminateWrite` result, and hard process death leave the marker in place so the next automatic tick cannot repeat the effect. Callers never invent identity from payload bytes. `resize` still takes the same lock but is not latched (it is idempotent-by-target).

**One write at a time per terminal, and the lock covers the decision.** Lease-holder `terminal_input`/`terminal_paste`/`terminal_resize`, attention respond, idle/prompt auto-answer, wake keystrokes, and plan-keystroke sequences all reach the same PTY through the coordinator. The coordinator acquires the per-terminal lock first, then — for attention — recaptures and runs fingerprint/episode CAS, then dispatches the backend write, then releases. Recapture and CAS never run outside that lock: a lease-holder write cannot consume the prompt after the decision and before injection. Attention respond is **privileged**: it does not need the client lease, but it is still one discrete locked coordinator operation. The coordinator exposes one entry point so attention cannot re-enter the lock.

**Lease authorization linearizes with the PTY effect.** Operator `WriteRequest`s and lease-gated resizes carry `attachment_id` and `expected_lease_generation`. Lease grant, release of the current holder, takeover, and finalization **of the current holder** acquire the same per-terminal lock (or wait for it) and increment `lease_generation` before capturing `terminal_attach_result` / `terminal_lease_lost` / `terminal_control_result` records that include that generation. Observer, already-released, displaced, and duplicate finalization take the same lock but **retain** the current generation: they forget that `attachment_id` without publishing a new authority. After the coordinator lock is held and immediately before the backend write or resize, the coordinator revalidates that the attachment still holds the current generation; a lost or stale lease is a typed refusal with no bytes and no `TIOCSWINSZ`. Attention and automatic origins skip the lease check. The captured notification records are sent only after the lock is released. gclient and browser reducers ignore any **authority** notification (`terminal_attach_result`, `terminal_control_result`, `terminal_lease_lost`) whose `lease_generation` is below the highest generation already applied for that `attachment_id`. `terminal_attachment_finalized` is applied idempotently by exact `attachment_id` and does not require a greater generation.

**A multi-step sequence is one locked operation, not several.** Wake (`Escape` / text / `Enter` with inter-step delays), prompt-then-Enter, plan-keystroke playback, and watchdog interrupt/reprompt/continuation are ordered actions whose last `Enter` would otherwise submit whatever landed in the gaps. `WriteCoordinator.run_sequence` acquires the terminal lock once, write-ahead-latches the single sequence `action_key` before the first backend step, executes the ordered `WriteRequest` steps and their required delays without releasing, and retains that key across an indeterminate result, a mid-sequence cancel, or process death. A concurrent lease-holder or attention write waits. An indeterminate step aborts the remainder under that same lock. **Cancellation has one settlement per sequence.** Cancel before the first backend effect: no PTY bytes, no latch, lock released, caller sees cancelled. After any step has been dispatched, the in-flight backend await is shielded to settlement, the sequence `action_key` is latched as indeterminate, no remaining step is sent (including no trailing `Enter`), the lock is released, and automatic retry is suppressed. Callers never loop `write_*` themselves for these flows. Native and tmux share this rule because both implement `TerminalRuntime` and both are invoked only from the coordinator.

**Dimension bounds.** `src/gobby/terminals/dimensions.py` is the Python bound set this leaf owns: minimum 1 row and 1 column, plus conservative maximum rows/cols/cells that keep a full grid inside `MAX_FRAME_SIZE` (2 MiB) under a documented worst-cell estimate. Every Python ingress this leaf creates (`spawn`, `resize`, and the module's own validators) rejects zero, negative, non-integer, and product-overflow inputs with a typed error before any row write, allocation, or backend call. Matching those integers to the Rust wire constants is **not** this leaf's close gate: 3.2 measures the imported `FrameData` and `TerminalAnsi` encoders, pins the protocol constants, and updates this module to the same numbers; 2.5 and 4.1 consume the shared module rather than inventing a second set.

**Output streaming is deliberately outside this contract.** `TerminalRuntime` covers lifecycle, snapshots, input, resize, and attach-locator resolution. Continuous output streaming stays backend-specific — tmux via its FIFO output reader, native via a daemon-side frame observer (4.2) — because the two transports have genuinely different shapes and a lowest-common-denominator method would hide that. Parity is asserted on the observable `terminal_output` event stream (5.1) instead of on a shared method, and no deliverable may claim streaming "through the runtime contract".

`src/gobby/terminals/tmux_runtime.py`: `TmuxTerminalRuntime` delegates to the existing tmux modules unchanged — `TmuxSessionManager` (create/kill/capture/send-keys), `text_injection` functions for `write_text`/`write_key`/`write_paste` (preserving the exact literal/paste/named-key byte sequences and the `AttentionInjectionError(stage)` semantics, now wrapped in the `WriteOutcome` above so a timed-out or answer-less `send-keys` reports indeterminate rather than raising as if nothing happened), FIFO output reader for streaming. Zero behavior change on the delivered path: the existing tmux test suites pass against the wrapped implementation. A runtime registry (`src/gobby/terminals/__init__.py`) resolves `Terminal.backend → TerminalRuntime`, constructed at the runner composition root beside `detection_registry` (`src/gobby/runner_init/orchestration.py`). This deliverable registers **only** the tmux backend, because `NativeTerminalRuntime` does not exist until 4.1: resolving `backend="native"` here raises a typed unregistered-backend error. The dual-backend resolution requirement is owned by 4.1, which registers the native runtime and asserts it.

**Composition roots, declared once here.** "Constructed at the runner composition root" is not sufficient in this codebase: a singleton reaches HTTP routes, WS handlers, monitors, and health only if it is declared on the objects that carry it. Four carriers own that plumbing and all four are edited in this deliverable, so no later leaf has to invent its own accessor. `GobbyRunner` (`src/gobby/runner.py:86`) gains typed attributes for the new services — mypy on `src/` rejects undeclared runner attributes, so this is a hard requirement, not tidiness — beside existing declarations such as `attention_manager: AttentionStateManager` (`:181`). `ServiceContainer` (`src/gobby/app_context.py:36`) gains only the subset the HTTP layers actually need, following the existing optional-field style (`attention_manager: Any | None = None`, `:83`). `src/gobby/runner_init/servers.py:33`, the single `ServiceContainer(...)` construction site, propagates the instances the runner built.

The fourth carrier is the one a three-root story misses: **`WebSocketServer` does not read `ServiceContainer`**. It is constructed at `src/gobby/runner_init/servers.py:131` with its own explicit keyword arguments, and the services it needs are attached immediately afterward through post-construction configuration — `runner.websocket_server.web_chat_runtime_manager = ...` (`:147`) and the `configure_attention_ordering`/`configure_attention_metadata` calls (`:150`, `:153`). Every WebSocket terminal handler in 2.5 needs `TerminalManager` and the runtime registry, so without this step 2.5 would have to reach for a module-level accessor or a second construction. This deliverable therefore adds a `configure_terminals(terminal_manager, runtime_registry)` method on `WebSocketServer` and calls it at that same site, deliberately following the existing configure-after-construct idiom rather than extending the constructor: the constructor is invoked positionally with two arguments in eight test fixtures (`tests/servers/test_tmux_mixin.py:65`, `tests/servers/test_websocket_server_disconnects.py:60`, seven sites in `tests/servers/test_websocket_subscriptions.py`, `tests/servers/test_ws_asgi_endpoint.py:116`, `tests/servers/websocket/test_auth.py:87`, `tests/servers/websocket/test_server.py:26`), and a new required parameter would break all of them for no behavioral gain while an optional one would make the wiring silently skippable.

The set declared here is `TerminalManager` (2.1), the runtime registry, and the shared terminal configuration below; 3.1 adds the host manager and 4.1 adds the control and frame clients to the same carriers, each in its own deliverable. Every consumer resolves the *same object* — there is no second construction anywhere, and the acceptance below asserts identity rather than mere availability, because two `TerminalManager` instances over one database would each hold their own idea of pending state.

**Shared terminal configuration lives with the contract, not with the host.** `spawn_in_doubt_seconds` and `default_backend` are consumed by 2.3's spawn matrix, 2.4's reaper, and 2.5's `terminal_create` — all of which precede any host — so defining them in 3.1 alongside the host's own settings would put the owner downstream of its consumers and force a backwards edge. `src/gobby/config/terminals.py` is created here with `TerminalConfig(default_backend: "tmux"|"native" = "tmux", spawn_in_doubt_seconds: float)` wired into `DaemonConfig` (`app.py`) beside `TmuxConfig`, and propagated through the four carriers above. 3.1's `TerminalHostConfig` keeps only what is genuinely host-specific — `enabled`, `socket_dir`, `binary_path`, `health_interval_seconds`, `shutdown_grace_seconds`, plus the capacity, scrollback, attach-history, and poll keys 3.2/3.4 add (`max_attachments_per_terminal`, `max_attachments_total`, `native_scrollback_max_lines`, `native_scrollback_max_bytes`, `max_attached_terminals`, `tmux_attach_history_lines`, `tmux_attach_history_max_bytes` owned by 3.2; `tmux_poll_interval_ms`, `tmux_poll_backoff_ceiling_ms` owned by 3.4) — and *reads* `TerminalConfig` for the in-doubt deadline rather than defining it.

**Acceptance:**

- 2.2.1 - The `TerminalRuntime` protocol and models exist with the staged surface above: `prepare_spawn` / `commit_spawn`, `PreparedSpawn`, and no one-shot `spawn` method. symbol: `TerminalRuntime`. file: `src/gobby/terminals/runtime.py`.
- 2.2.17 - `commit_spawn` is refused typed unless the caller has acknowledged persist on that `PreparedSpawn`; a tmux prepare/commit pair still yields one session. Native observer-bind-before-commit is 4.1.10 / 4.2.9. test: `tests/terminals/test_tmux_runtime.py::test_prepare_commit_requires_caller_ack`.
- 2.2.2 - `TmuxTerminalRuntime` implements the full contract by delegation; injection byte sequences and error stages are unchanged. file: `src/gobby/terminals/tmux_runtime.py`.
- 2.2.3 - The existing tmux spawner/session-manager/injection suites pass against the wrapped runtime with their assertions intact, using the 2.1 fixture helper for any terminal setup they need. test: `tests/agents/test_tmux.py::test_session_manager_injection_unchanged`.
- 2.2.4 - The runtime registry is constructed once at the runner composition root, resolves `backend="tmux"`, and raises a typed unregistered-backend error for `"native"` at this point in the plan. test: `tests/terminals/test_runtime_registry.py::test_registry_composition_and_tmux_resolution`.
- 2.2.5 - Dimension validation rejects zero, negative, above-maximum, and cell-product-overflow values at every Python ingress this leaf owns (`prepare_spawn`, `resize`, and `dimensions.py` validators) with a typed error, before any row write, allocation, or backend call, against this module's own constants. Matching the 3.2 wire constants is that later leaf. test: `tests/terminals/test_dimensions.py::test_bounds_rejected_before_side_effects`.
- 2.2.6 - No `TerminalRuntime` method streams continuous output, and a source-scan test keeps streaming out of the contract surface. file: `src/gobby/terminals/runtime.py`.
- 2.2.7 - `TerminalManager`, the runtime registry, and `TerminalConfig` are declared as typed attributes on `GobbyRunner`, carried on `ServiceContainer`, propagated from the single construction site, **and** attached to `WebSocketServer` via `configure_terminals` at that site, and those four carriers hold the *identical* instances. The eight positional `WebSocketServer(...)` test fixtures still construct successfully unchanged. Later leaves (2.3 spawn, 2.4 attention, 2.5 HTTP/WS, 3.1 health) consume these carriers; they do not have to exist for this leaf to close. test: `tests/terminals/test_composition_roots.py::test_single_instance_reaches_every_consumer`.
- 2.2.8 - `snapshot` and `snapshot_full` return `SnapshotResult` with byte-accurate counters and an honest unknown: a normal capture reports `truncated` false, `dropped_bytes` zero, and `total_bytes` equal to `len(text.encode("utf-8"))` for wide-Unicode content as well as ASCII; a tmux capture at `history-limit` reports `truncated` true with `dropped_bytes` and `total_bytes` both `None` rather than zero; a truncated `text` always decodes because the cut lands on a code-point boundary; and no runtime method returns a bare `str` for snapshot content. symbol: `SnapshotResult`. test: `tests/terminals/test_tmux_runtime.py::test_snapshot_counters_are_utf8_bytes_with_unknown_history_loss`.
- 2.2.10 - The write surface is a typed outcome on both backends: `write_text`/`write_key`/`write_paste` return `Delivered` or `IndeterminateWrite` and never `None`, a `send-keys` whose response is withheld while its effect has already reached the pane returns `IndeterminateWrite` (not success and not a raised failure), and a genuine tmux failure still raises typed with its injection `stage` intact. The coordinator is what inserts the request's `action_key`/`origin` into `unresolved_writes` without replacing other keys. symbol: `WriteOutcome`. test: `tests/terminals/test_tmux_runtime.py::test_write_returns_indeterminate_when_effect_precedes_lost_response`.
- 2.2.12 - Concurrent writes to one terminal serialize through the coordinator on tmux: an attention respond racing a lease-holder `write_text` produces two intact payloads in lock order with no interleaved bytes; the attention write succeeds without holding the client lease; recapture and fingerprint/episode CAS run **after** the coordinator lock is held and **before** backend dispatch. Native serialization is 4.1.11. test: `tests/terminals/test_write_coordinator.py::test_attention_and_lease_writes_serialize`.
- 2.2.13 - The coordinator is the sole latch: two `WriteRequest`s with identical payloads but different `action_key`s produce two independent `unresolved_writes` entries; a runtime `write_*` call from a test double that bypasses the coordinator does not persist a latch; attention recapture racing a lease-holder write after capture and after CAS but before dispatch still serializes under one lock on tmux. test: `tests/terminals/test_write_coordinator.py::test_coordinator_owns_lock_identity_and_latch`.
- 2.2.14 - `run_sequence` holds the per-terminal lock across every step and required delay: a lease-holder `write_text` issued during an `Escape`/delay/text/delay/`Enter` wake cannot interleave bytes or have its input submitted by the sequence `Enter`; an indeterminate middle step records the single sequence `action_key` and sends no remainder. test: `tests/terminals/test_write_coordinator.py::test_sequence_holds_lock_across_steps`.
- 2.2.15 - Sequence cancellation settles once: cancel before the first backend effect leaves the PTY unchanged and records no latch; cancel during a backend await or inter-step delay after the first dispatch latches the sequence `action_key` as indeterminate, sends no remaining step, releases the lock, and does not automatically retry. Both tmux and native paths are covered. test: `tests/terminals/test_write_coordinator.py::test_sequence_cancellation_settles_once`.
- 2.2.16 - A lease-gated write or resize that passes the handler check, waits behind another coordinated action, then loses the lease to takeover or disconnect is refused typed at dispatch and issues no backend effect; attention writes in the same window still proceed. test: `tests/terminals/test_write_coordinator.py::test_lease_revalidated_immediately_before_effect`.
- 2.2.11 - `write_paste` uses herdr 0.8.0 `paste_payload`: a pane with bracketed paste on receives one `\x1b[200~…\x1b[201~` unit whose inner newlines do not submit; a pane with the mode off receives raw text; a payload over 1 MiB UTF-8 is refused typed without a write. test: `tests/terminals/test_tmux_runtime.py::test_write_paste_follows_live_bracketed_mode_and_size_cap`.
- 2.2.18 - Before dispatching a write that would insert a new `action_key`, the coordinator reserves durable latch capacity: a 256-byte+1 key, a 33rd distinct key, and a serialization that would exceed 64 KiB each return typed `unresolved_write_capacity` with no PTY mutation and no latch write; an already-latched key and an operator resolution of an existing key still proceed; a restart that restored 32 keys still refuses a 33rd automatic action. test: `tests/terminals/test_write_coordinator.py::test_unresolved_write_capacity_is_reserved_before_dispatch`.
- 2.2.19 - `TmuxTerminalRuntime.write_key` encodes against flags queried at dispatch: Up is `ESC O A` when `#{cursor_keys_flag}` is on and `ESC [ A` when it is off; the matching application-keypad vs normal keypad pair is asserted for the keypad named keys; a flag flip between two writes is visible on the second write; the runtime does not read 3.4 poll-cached flags. test: `tests/terminals/test_tmux_runtime.py::test_write_key_encodes_against_live_pane_flags`.
- 2.2.20 - The coordinator write-ahead-latches `action_key` under the per-terminal lock before backend dispatch: a hard kill after the marker and before `write_*` starts leaves the key set and the PTY unchanged; a hard kill after PTY bytes land and before the runtime result leaves the key set; a durable `Delivered` or no-effect typed failure clears it; `run_sequence` write-ahead-latches once before the first step so a kill between `Escape` and `Enter` sends no remaining `Enter`. test: `tests/terminals/test_write_coordinator.py::test_write_ahead_latch_survives_hard_kill`.
- 2.2.9 - `TerminalConfig` supplies `default_backend` and `spawn_in_doubt_seconds` from `DaemonConfig`, and no P2 consumer imports a host-owned config module. `TerminalHostConfig` reading that deadline is 3.1.3. test: `tests/config/test_terminals.py::test_shared_terminal_config_precedes_host_config`.

### 2.3 Migrate the spawn and resume paths to TerminalRuntime [category: refactor] (depends: 2.2)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor.py::*` — scope-reason: spawn-path migration to TerminalRuntime and the SRT chokepoint hoist
- `src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor_providers.py`
- `src/gobby/agents/spawn.py::*` — scope-reason: spawn-path migration to TerminalRuntime and the SRT chokepoint hoist
- `src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor_support.py::*` — scope-reason: spawn-path migration to TerminalRuntime and the SRT chokepoint hoist
- `src/gobby/agents/resume_executor.py::*` — scope-reason: spawn-path migration to TerminalRuntime and the SRT chokepoint hoist
- `src/gobby/storage/daemon_resume_keys.py::*` — scope-reason: replace tmux-name and planned-title resume metadata with terminal-row identity
- `src/gobby/runner_lifecycle_agents.py::*` — scope-reason: consume the renamed daemon-stop resume terminal identity written in this leaf
- `tests/agents/test_resume_executor.py::*` — scope-reason: writer/reader assertions for the backend-neutral resume identity
- `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/spawn_agent/_implementation.py::*` — scope-reason: spawn-path migration to TerminalRuntime and the SRT chokepoint hoist
- `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/spawn_agent/_runtime.py::*` — scope-reason: spawn-path migration to TerminalRuntime and the SRT chokepoint hoist
- `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/spawn_agent/_health.py::*` — scope-reason: spawn-path migration to TerminalRuntime and the SRT chokepoint hoist
- `src/gobby/dispatch/spawn_actions.py::*` — scope-reason: spawn-path migration to TerminalRuntime and the SRT chokepoint hoist
- `src/gobby/servers/routes/agent_spawn.py::*` — scope-reason: spawn-path migration to TerminalRuntime and the SRT chokepoint hoist
- `src/gobby/agents/dry_run.py::*` — scope-reason: spawn-path migration to TerminalRuntime and the SRT chokepoint hoist
- `src/gobby/agents/tmux/spawner.py::*` — scope-reason: spawn-path migration to TerminalRuntime and the SRT chokepoint hoist
- `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/spawn_agent/_execution.py`
- `tests/agents/test_spawn_executor.py::*` — scope-reason: spawn outcome matrix, lost-CAS, and cancellation-window tests
- `tests/terminals/test_no_direct_tmux_spawn.py`
- `tests/agents/test_tmux.py::*` — scope-reason: caller-supplied spawn_key assertion

**Split `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/spawn_agent/_implementation.py` first.** That file is 972 lines today. This leaf and 4.2 both add behavior; touching it at or above 1,000 lines is forbidden. Extract execution helpers into `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/spawn_agent/_execution.py` so both modules stay below 1,000 *before* any TerminalRuntime wiring. 4.2 retargets the split modules, not a reconstituted monolith.

**Split `src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor.py` in this same leaf.** That file is 866 lines today and this leaf plus 4.2 add the runtime seam, retry/cancel windows, and native backend selection. Extract the five `_spawn_*_terminal` provider bodies into `src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor_providers.py` *before* the seam wiring so both modules stay below 1,000. `execute_spawn` and the shared wrap/`promote_to_live` path stay on `spawn_executor.py`. 4.2 retargets these split modules. A 2.4 consumer that only replaces one `send_keys` call is not split here.

Route every spawn entry (MCP `spawn_agent`, dispatch actions, HTTP spawn route, resume, dry-run, and 2.5 `terminal_create`) through the runtime registry instead of constructing `TmuxSpawner` directly: `execute_spawn` is the shared row-owning primitive. It selects the runtime from the request's backend (default from config, 4.2 adds `native`), calls `create_pending` (2.1) before backend dispatch, then — for native only — `reserve_observer` **before** `runtime.prepare_spawn(...)`, copies the returned `{reservation_id, reserve_key}` onto `TerminalSpawnRequest` so control `spawn` names that exact reservation, then `record_process` when the prepared object carries `{pgid, start_time}`, the native observer bind (4.2), then `runtime.commit_spawn(...)`, and persists `terminal_id` via `update_runtime`. A native spawn that cannot reserve, or whose reservation identity is missing, stale, or bound to a different `terminal_id`, is refused typed with no fork. Failure after reserve and before `spawn_prepared` `release_observer`s the still-unbound reservation on the still-open control connection. Failure after `spawn_prepared` `kill`s the prepared entry, which drops the prepared-owned reservation; it does not `release_observer` a slot the prepared entry still holds. Control disconnect after `spawn_prepared` does not free that slot. `runner_broadcasting.py` does not reserve or prepare; it only relays from the observer this primitive already bound. The five per-provider `_spawn_*_terminal` functions in `spawn_executor.py` keep their provider-specific command/env preparation but lose direct tmux calls; `_check_tmux_session_alive` becomes `runtime.is_live`; `_tmux_runtime_metadata` becomes terminal-handle persistence. `resume_executor.py` rebuilds the runtime from the stored terminal row instead of reconstructing `TmuxConfig` from resume metadata. **Daemon-stop resume metadata is backend-neutral in this leaf.** `src/gobby/storage/daemon_resume_keys.py` drops `daemon_stop_resume_tmux_session_name` and `daemon_stop_resume_planned_tmux_title`; the writer in `resume_executor.py` and the reader in `runner_lifecycle_agents.py` persist and consume exactly `terminal_id` plus `spawn_key` so a native run can be resumed after daemon stop. No other 2.1 field is a permitted substitute. Behavior is otherwise unchanged for the tmux backend (this is a pure seam refactor; the existing spawn integration tests pass).

`execute_spawn` is the **sole** creator and transition owner of the pending row, per 2.1: it allocates the id, derives the `spawn_key`, writes the `pending` row with no locator, passes id and `spawn_key` inside `TerminalSpawnRequest`, and performs the single `promote_to_live` CAS from the locator the runtime returned. Every terminal outcome is spelled out so no path can duplicate or strand a row — backend spawn raising leaves the row `pending` for the stale-pending reaper (2.4) and CASes it to `exited` when the failure is definitive; a spawn timeout leaves it `pending` and reapable *by `spawn_key`*, which is the only way a session created just before the timeout can be found at all; cancellation **before** backend dispatch CASes straight to `exited`; after dispatch, settlement is shielded (see below). A successful spawn whose `promote_to_live` CAS loses must **reload the row** before destroying anything: if the row is already `live` with the same `backend`, `locator_key`, and `host_epoch`, that is idempotent success (3.1 reconciliation won the race); terminate the just-created backend terminal only when the live identity is a *different* physical resource.

**The concrete backend must stop minting names, or `spawn_key` is decoration.** The caller-owned identity contract is only real if it reaches the process that actually creates the resource, and today it does not: `TmuxSpawner._async_spawn` derives its own session name from a timestamp and a random suffix (`base = title or f"{prefix}-{int(time.time())}"`, `suffix = uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]`) and reports it back through `SpawnResult.tmux_session_name`. Left in place, the reaper would resolve `gobby-<terminal_id>` while tmux held a name nobody recorded, which is precisely the orphan class this plan claims to close. So this deliverable edits the spawner: the name-generation block is deleted, `new-session` is invoked with the `spawn_key` carried in `TerminalSpawnRequest`, the sanitization that made arbitrary titles safe becomes a validation of the caller-supplied key (a key that would need rewriting is a caller bug, rejected typed, not silently mangled), and the tmux-specific result fields are replaced by the locator the runtime returns. `title` survives as display metadata written to the terminal row (2.1), decoupled from identity. No `TerminalRuntime` implementation may generate an identity after this deliverable, and the acceptance scans for it.

**SRT wrapping hoists to one chokepoint at the runtime seam.** Today `SandboxLaunch.wrap()` is called from six sites — one per provider branch in `spawn_executor.py` (claude/qwen/grok/codex/droid) plus `resume_executor.py` — so "wrap once, after construction, before terminal creation" is per-branch discipline that every new provider and every new backend must re-remember. Since this deliverable already routes every spawn path through the runtime, the wrap moves with it: the shared step that hands a finished provider command to `runtime.prepare_spawn(...)` applies the SRT wrap exactly once, immediately before backend dispatch, and the provider branches keep only their command/env preparation. The `_preflight_srt` probe wrap in `srt_runtime.py` is a probe, not a spawn, and is unchanged. 4.2's native path then inherits the invariant structurally instead of reimplementing it.

**A young pending row is in doubt, not dead.** Absence of a resource proves nothing while its spawn may still be running, so no reaper or reconciler may treat `pending` as `exited` on absence alone. Two conditions must both hold: the row is older than `spawn_in_doubt_seconds` (`TerminalConfig`, 2.2, defaulting to the existing spawn timeout plus a margin), *and* resolving its `spawn_key` against the backend finds nothing. A row younger than that deadline is left alone and revisited. Retry of a spawn reuses the same `terminal_id` and `spawn_key` rather than allocating new ones, so a retry cannot create a second physical terminal or a second row, and a reconciler that runs concurrently with a retry converges on the one row either way. This rule is what 3.1's reconnect reconciliation and 2.4's reaper both consume; neither redefines it.

**A retry is a new attempt, not a younger view of the old one.** Reusing `spawn_key` after `spawn_in_doubt_seconds` would otherwise make the live retry immediately eligible for reaping: the reaper can observe no effect, CAS `pending → exited`, and leave the retry to create a PTY against an exited row. Before each retry dispatch the caller CASes `attempt_generation = attempt_generation + 1` **and** `attempt_started_at = now()` on the still-`pending` row in one statement. Stale selection and the reaper/reconciler CAS use the observed generation **and** `attempt_started_at`, never generic `updated_at`. A generation that moved under them is a lost CAS and they leave the row alone. If the increment CAS loses because the row is already `exited` or `orphaned`, the retry does not dispatch and surfaces a failed spawn. 3.1's adoption matrix uses the same generation and attempt clock in its pending-exit CAS. An unrelated metadata update that touches `updated_at` does not refresh attempt age; a daemon restart during the refreshed interval does not make the live retry immediately reapable.

**Pre-dispatch vs post-dispatch cancellation.** "Between row creation and backend spawn" is not one window. **Pre-dispatch** (no bytes written to tmux/`gterm`): cancel may CAS `pending → exited` with no backend effect. **Post-dispatch** (request written, effect may exist, response maybe outstanding): do not mark `exited` and do not drop the `spawn_key`. Shield settlement until the runtime returns, the connection is lost, or the in-doubt deadline expires; then resolve by `spawn_key`/`list` and either `promote_to_live` or reap. An indeterminate pending row is the honest state while the effect is unsettled. The same fence is what stops 3.1 from treating a still-settling spawn as dead.

**Acceptance:**

- 2.3.1 - No module outside `src/gobby/terminals/` and `src/gobby/agents/tmux/` constructs `TmuxSpawner` or `TmuxSessionManager` for spawn purposes (source-scan test). test: `tests/terminals/test_no_direct_tmux_spawn.py::test_spawn_paths_use_runtime`.
- 2.3.2 - All five spawn entries create the pending terminal row before backend spawn and persist `terminal_id` on success. file: `src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor.py`.
- 2.3.3 - Resume rebuilds runtimes from stored terminal rows and daemon-stop metadata names exactly `terminal_id` plus `spawn_key` rather than a tmux session name or planned title. file: `src/gobby/agents/resume_executor.py`.
- 2.3.4 - The existing spawn/resume integration suites pass on the tmux backend with their observable assertions intact after migration to the 2.1 fixture helper. test: `tests/agents/test_spawn_executor.py`.
- 2.3.5 - Exactly one terminal row exists per spawn attempt for every outcome — success, backend failure, timeout, cancellation, and lost CAS — with no orphaned backend terminal left behind in the lost-CAS case, and no `TerminalRuntime` implementation writes a row. test: `tests/agents/test_spawn_executor.py::test_one_terminal_row_per_attempt_all_outcomes`.
- 2.3.6 - A tmux session created immediately before a spawn timeout is found and killed by `spawn_key` resolution; a pending row younger than `spawn_in_doubt_seconds` is left pending by both the reaper and the reconciler even when the backend reports nothing; and a retried spawn reuses the same `terminal_id` and `spawn_key`, producing one row and one physical terminal. test: `tests/agents/test_spawn_executor.py::test_in_doubt_pending_is_not_reaped_or_exited`.
- 2.3.7 - `TmuxSpawner` creates the tmux session under exactly the `spawn_key` it was given, generates no timestamp or UUID of its own, rejects a malformed key typed instead of sanitizing it, and returns a locator rather than a session name; a source scan finds no identity generation in any `TerminalRuntime` implementation. test: `tests/agents/test_tmux.py::test_spawner_uses_caller_supplied_spawn_key`.
- 2.3.8 - SRT wrapping happens at exactly one call site on the spawn path — the shared step ahead of `runtime.prepare_spawn` — with the five provider branches and resume free of `launch.wrap()` calls (source scan; the `_preflight_srt` probe is the only other wrap site), and a spawned provider command is wrapped exactly once with `allowPty` semantics unchanged. test: `tests/agents/test_spawn_executor.py::test_srt_wrap_single_chokepoint`.
- 2.3.9 - `_implementation.py` and `_execution.py` are each below 1,000 lines after the prerequisite split and after this leaf's wiring. file: `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/spawn_agent/_execution.py`.
- 2.3.12 - `spawn_executor.py` and `spawn_executor_providers.py` are each below 1,000 lines after the provider extract and after this leaf's wiring. file: `src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor_providers.py`.
- 2.3.10 - When reconciliation promotes a pending row after the backend effect is visible and before `runtime.commit_spawn` returns, the caller's lost `promote_to_live` CAS reloads and treats same `backend`/`locator_key`/`host_epoch` as success; a conflicting live identity is the only case that terminates the just-created terminal. test: `tests/agents/test_spawn_executor.py::test_lost_cas_converges_when_reconciler_already_promoted`.
- 2.3.15 - Daemon-stop resume metadata no longer stores a tmux session name or planned title: `daemon_resume_keys.py` defines exactly `terminal_id` plus `spawn_key`, `resume_executor.py` writes both fields, `runner_lifecycle_agents.py` reads both fields, and the existing resume executor tests assert a native-capable restart without a tmux locator. test: `tests/agents/test_resume_executor.py::test_daemon_stop_resume_uses_terminal_id_and_spawn_key`.
- 2.3.11 - Pre-dispatch cancel CASes `pending → exited` with no backend effect; post-dispatch cancel (after request bytes, after effect creation, while awaiting response, during promotion) never exits a row that still has an unresolved effect — the row stays pending until promote or reap by `spawn_key`/`list`. test: `tests/agents/test_spawn_executor.py::test_cancel_windows_pre_and_post_dispatch`.
- 2.3.13 - A retry past `spawn_in_doubt_seconds` increments `attempt_generation` and refreshes `attempt_started_at` before dispatch; a reaper that observed the previous generation loses its exit CAS; a reaper that wins first causes the increment CAS to fail and the retry not to dispatch; reload of an `exited`/`orphaned` row is a failed spawn with no new backend effect. test: `tests/agents/test_spawn_executor.py::test_retry_generation_fences_the_reaper`.
- 2.3.14 - Unrelated `updated_at` mutations do not make a live retry stale; a daemon restart inside the refreshed `attempt_started_at` window leaves the row pending; stale selection names the observed generation and `attempt_started_at`. test: `tests/agents/test_spawn_executor.py::test_attempt_started_at_survives_unrelated_updates_and_restart`.

Replace the "has a terminal ≡ `tmux_session_name IS NOT NULL`" idiom with terminal-row resolution, and every capture/inject/kill call with the runtime contract:

- `_get_active_terminal_runs` filters (`agent_health.py:423`, `lifecycle_monitor.py:383`, `idle_check_handler.py:308`) resolve runs joined to `terminals` rows in `pending|live` state.
- Detection inputs: `capture_pane(name, lines)` call sites become `runtime.snapshot(terminal, lines)`; post-mortem `capture_full_pane`/`capture_then_kill` become `snapshot_full` + `terminate` (`capture.py`'s `ManagedTmux` protocol is retired in favor of `TerminalRuntime`). Detectors read `SnapshotResult.text` and behave exactly as today, but the **persistence** path is where the 2.2 metadata has to land: `agents/capture.py` stores `truncated`, `dropped_bytes`, and `total_bytes` beside the captured text, so a capture-before-kill artifact records its own completeness instead of presenting a truncated tail as the whole output. Dropping the flags here would make the 2.2 contract pointless, since capture-before-kill is the one caller whose input is large enough to be truncated in practice.
- Prompt auto-answer and idle reprompt sends (`lifecycle_monitor.py:546`, `idle_check_handler.py:617`, `terminal_prompt_monitor.py`) go through `write_text`/`write_key`.
- The attention respond path: `inject_attention_answer_to_tmux_target` gains a runtime-neutral wrapper (`src/gobby/terminals` re-export) so `servers/routes/attention.py` calls the 2.2 write coordinator with `origin: "attention"` and a stable `action_key`; the tmux implementation and its CAS/recapture/stage semantics are unchanged except that recapture and fingerprint CAS now run **inside** the coordinator lock. Attention is a privileged daemon write: it does not take the client lease, but it cannot observe a prompt, drop the lock, and then inject after a lease-holder has consumed it.
- Kill: `kill.py`'s session/pane kill methods route through `terminate` for `gobby`-owned terminals; the external-session PID-identity path (`parent_pid` + `parent_create_time` guards) stays as-is.
- Cleanup: `agent_cleanup.py`'s `_close_tmux_session` compare-and-bail becomes the 2.1 CAS transition; stale-pending reaping consumes `list_stale_pending` (closing the orphan gap); `runner_lifecycle_agents.py` restart reconciliation lists live terminals via the runtime instead of raw `list_sessions`.
- `_resolve_tmux_target`'s dual resolution (`_terminal_tmux.py`) becomes terminal-row resolution feeding the runtime. Its patchable facade lives one module up in `mcp_proxy/tools/sessions/_terminal.py`, which is where MCP `send_keys` and `capture_output` actually call `tmux.send_keys` and `tmux.capture_pane` against a `TmuxSessionManager` — those two tools keep their public contracts (`via` field included) but resolve a terminal row and call `write_text`/`write_key`/`snapshot`, so they work on native terminals instead of silently failing for them. The registered closures are not constructed in `_terminal.py` alone: `register_terminal_tools` is invoked from `create_session_messages_registry` (`_factory.py`), which `setup_internal_registries` (`mcp_proxy/registries.py`) installs, which the **daemon HTTP** composition root (`servers/http.py`) calls. This leaf propagates the exact composition-root `TerminalManager`, runtime registry, and `WriteCoordinator` through that in-process chain so the registered MCP `send_keys`, `compact_self`, and `capture_output` closures receive those instances; no hop constructs a second service set. The stdio process is a **proxy-only non-participant**: `stdio_server.py` sets `session_manager = None`, discards the `setup_internal_registries` result, and forwards tool calls through `DaemonProxy` to the daemon HTTP surface. Exact cross-process object identity is impossible and is not required. Stdio must construct no second `TerminalManager`, runtime registry, or `WriteCoordinator`. The module's `_backfill_tmux_context_from_sibling` path keeps operating on `sessions.terminal_context`, which is unchanged (2.1). The direct test seams move with those modules: `tests/events/test_wake.py` stops constructing a tmux sender for Gobby-owned rows and drives `WriteCoordinator.run_sequence`; `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/sessions/test_compact_self.py`, `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/sessions/test_compact_self_readiness.py`, and `tests/mcp_proxy/tools/sessions/test_terminal.py` replace `TmuxSessionManager` / `get_tmux_manager_for_context` patches with terminal-row resolution plus coordinator writes, keeping an explicit external-tmux case only where row resolution selects tmux. The same sweep owns `tests/servers/test_attention_respond.py`, `tests/servers/test_session_control.py`, `tests/servers/websocket/test_voice_warmup.py`, `tests/sessions/test_liveness_monitor.py`, and `tests/agents/test_attention_state.py`.
- **Run liveness must not equate "no tmux name" with "dead".** `agents/task_recovery.py` declares `tmux_session_name` on its run protocol and treats `pid is None and tmux_session_name is None` as the never-started predicate. Left alone that predicate recovers the task of a perfectly live native run, because a native run has no tmux name and its process lives in the host, not as a daemon child. The predicate becomes "no terminal row in `pending|live`", the protocol member is dropped, and both branches are tested against a live native terminal and a genuinely never-started run.
- Production field readers this deliverable also owns, previously unnamed: `agents/terminal_cleanup.py` (eight sites; compare-and-kill of the recorded session), `agents/lifecycle_reconciliation.py` (the restart-reconciliation source 2.4.4 already tests), and `agents/watchdog/recovery.py` (field reads plus multi-step `send_keys` for interrupt/reprompt/continuation). All three resolve a terminal row and go through the runtime.
- **`spawn_executor_support.py` writes belong here, construction belongs to 2.3.** 2.3 already targets that file for the spawn-path hoist. Its post-spawn `send_keys` (prompt then Enter) is a 2.4 write-path consumer: migrate those two calls onto `WriteCoordinator.run_sequence` (`write_text` then `write_key` Enter) in this leaf, not a second wrap site in 2.3. The focused suite in `tests/agents/test_spawn_executor.py` must drive that sequence and abort with no remaining Enter when a step is `IndeterminateWrite`.
- `tests/agents/test_capture_consumers.py`'s hard-coded call-site list is updated to the runtime call sites.
- **`NativePlanActionService` is a write-path consumer.** `src/gobby/communications/native_plan_actions.py` talks to tmux through a session manager for plan/dispatch keystrokes. Migrate it here with its tests (`tests/communications/test_native_plan_actions.py`) and its construction sites (`session_notifications.py`, `telegram_actions.py`, `runner_init/servers.py`). Leave it out and 5.3's native default silently breaks comms plan actions.

- Beyond the monitor/capture clusters, a whole-repo sweep of the field name (`gcode grep -w tmux_session_name src tests`) lists these remaining owners, each migrated here: `events/wake.py` (parses a tmux session out of `terminal_context` to deliver wakes — becomes terminal-row resolution with the `terminal_context` PID/identity keys untouched), `hooks/session_coordinator.py` (builds a `=<session>` tmux target for coordination sends — `hooks/factory.py` and `hooks/hook_manager.py` construct it **and** `EventHandlers`, and must propagate the composition-root `TerminalManager`, runtime registry, write coordinator, and the one `TerminalEffectBridge` below into both), `mcp_proxy/tools/agent_cancellation.py`, `agents_lifecycle_tools.py`, `agents_query_tools.py`, and `agents_termination.py` (run payloads and termination targets), `servers/routes/attention.py` (roster/respond target resolution, also 2.5), and `servers/websocket/handlers/core.py` plus `src/gobby/servers/websocket/tmux.py` (input routing and run↔session matching, also 2.5). Two readers are deliberately owned elsewhere and named here so the sweep is exhaustive rather than silent: `runner_lifecycle_shutdown.py`'s restart-preservation reader is migrated in 3.1, where the host PID it must additionally preserve is introduced, and `runner_broadcasting.py`'s five readers are migrated in 2.5 together with the broadcast rename they feed, because migrating them here would leave a producer calling a broadcast API that 2.5 replaces.

- **The field sweep and the call sweep find different consumers, so both are run.** Searching for `tmux_session_name` finds everything that reads the *column*; it does not find code that already holds a tmux pane target and calls a `TmuxSessionManager` method on it. A separate sweep for the concrete call names (`gcode grep -F 'send_keys' src` and the same for `capture_pane`/`capture_full_pane`/`kill_session`) surfaces five live write-path consumers that no field sweep would ever have shown, and every one of them can be pointed at a Gobby-managed provider session — which means each keeps working today only because tmux is the sole backend, and each breaks silently the moment 5.3 flips the default. They are migrated here with the rest: `src/gobby/adapters/plan_keystrokes.py:208` (`tmux.send_keys(pane, stroke.keys, literal=...)`, the keystroke dispatcher), its caller `src/gobby/servers/websocket/handlers/plan_approval.py:331` (`dispatch_plan_keystrokes(tmux, tmux_pane, sequence)` — it resolves the manager and pane and passes both down, so the seam is the resolution, not the send), `src/gobby/servers/websocket/handlers/session_config.py:225` (persona configuration sends keys through `get_tmux_manager_for_context(ctx)`), `src/gobby/servers/websocket/handlers/session_observe_proxy.py:369` (`tmux_manager.send_keys(tmux_pane, content + "\n")`, message delivery to an observed session — resolved through `session_observe.py`'s `get_tmux_manager_for_context` re-export; this leaf removes or replaces that facade import/export with the declared terminal-runtime seam so `test_session_control.py` and `test_voice_warmup.py` patch the same name), and `src/gobby/sessions/compact_continuation.py:289` (`tmux.send_keys(target, f"{prompt}\n", literal=True)`, the post-compaction continuation prompt — the one whose loss is least visible, since a dropped continuation looks like an idle session rather than an error). Each resolves a terminal row and calls `write_text`/`write_key` on the runtime; each keeps its current typed failure behavior. The test seams move with them: `tests/adapters/test_plan_keystrokes.py`, `tests/servers/websocket/test_attached_plan_approval.py`, `tests/sessions/test_compact_continuation.py`, and `tests/servers/websocket/test_set_agent.py` (which patches `session_config.get_tmux_manager_for_context` by name at `:323` and therefore fails the moment that resolution changes). `session_observe_proxy.py` has no direct test today; this deliverable adds one rather than migrating it unverified. A sixth consumer is the one the call sweep almost hides, because its sends live in the daemon's own wiring rather than in a feature module: `src/gobby/runner_init/orchestration.py:31`'s `_send_tmux_session_wake`, the callable handed to `WakeDispatcher` as `tmux_sender`, issues up to **three** sequential `mgr.send_keys` calls per wake — `Escape`, the literal text, then `Enter`, with a delay between each — and converts a falsy return into `RuntimeError`. Every send in that sequence is therefore all-or-nothing on a boolean, so an indeterminate second send either raises (reported as a failed wake when the text may already be on screen) or, if the boolean is true, lets `Enter` submit text whose arrival nobody confirmed. It migrates here with the other five: terminal-row resolution, then `WriteCoordinator.run_sequence` (2.2) for the `Escape`/text/`Enter` steps and delays so the lock is not released between them, and the abort rule below. Prompt-then-Enter, plan-keystroke playback, and watchdog interrupt/reprompt/continuation use the same sequence operation. Its seam is already tested — `tests/test_runner_init.py:322` calls `_send_tmux_session_wake` directly — so the migration has a real assertion site rather than a new one.

- **An indeterminate write needs one rule, applied by every caller.** 3.2 withdraws the cross-reconnect exactly-once guarantee for writes: when a response is lost, the boundary reports the effect as *indeterminate* rather than claiming success or failure, because no ledger that dies with the connection can answer the question. Reporting it honestly is necessary but not sufficient — a caller that receives `indeterminate` and treats it as either outcome reintroduces the bug in a new place. Today's surface has only `stage: none|partial`, so there is no branch to fall into: the attention route captures before injection and re-detects only known-partial errors, and the multi-write callers (idle reprompt's prompt-then-Enter, prompt auto-answer, the plan-keystroke sequences above) would continue to their next substep, or increment a retry counter, or resend on the next loop tick — each producing a double-typed prompt in exactly the case where the first one may already have landed.

  So `IndeterminateWrite` becomes a third outcome of the backend-neutral write surface, and there is exactly one rule for it, stated once and adopted verbatim by every consumer: **an indeterminate write is never retried and never advances state; the caller re-observes the terminal and decides from what it sees.** Concretely, a multi-step sequence aborts at the indeterminate step rather than sending the remainder; the attention route leaves the entry open and re-captures instead of clearing it; the idle reprompt and prompt-auto-answer paths leave their retry counters untouched so the next tick re-evaluates from a fresh snapshot rather than from an assumption; the memory watchdog and MCP `send_keys` return the indeterminate result to their caller instead of collapsing it to a boolean. Re-observation is cheap and already implemented — every one of these callers has a snapshot path — which is why abstain-and-re-observe is the whole policy rather than a per-caller matrix of compensations.

**The rule binds later ticks, not just the failing call.** Abstain-and-re-observe is sufficient only if re-observation can answer the question, and for these consumers it cannot: a prompt that was delivered and consumed and a prompt that never arrived can present the *same* snapshot, so an idle reprompt that abstains on tick *n* re-evaluates the identical state on tick *n+1* and sends the same bytes again — the duplicate the rule was written to prevent, arriving one interval later. So the write coordinator inserts `{at, origin}` under the request's `action_key` in 2.1's `unresolved_writes` map **before** backend dispatch (the write-ahead marker 2.2 owns), and every *automatic* write consults the map: a matching key is suppressed and reported as suppressed rather than sent, while unrelated unresolved actions remain independently protected. A definitive `Delivered` or no-effect typed failure, once durably processed, clears that key; a hard process death after PTY bytes land, a post-dispatch cancel, and an `IndeterminateWrite` result leave it set. Positive observation or an operator resolution also removes only the matching key; terminal exit clears the map. An operator write is never suppressed because a human deciding to retype is the resolution. Multi-step sequences use one sequence action key rather than one key per keystroke, so an aborted `Escape`/text/`Enter` wake does not create three entries.

`TmuxPaneMonitor` keeps its tmux-specific pane-death detection (it monitors the tmux backend specifically) but opens/clears attention through entry ids exactly as today.

**Synchronous hook threads may not invent their own loop.** `SessionCoordinator` is synchronous; `WriteCoordinator` writes are async and must run on the runner loop. `src/gobby/terminals/sync_bridge.py` is the single injected `TerminalEffectBridge`: factory and hook manager pass the runner-loop instance into the coordinator, and hook threads call only `bridge.run(request)`. The bridge is forbidden on the loop thread (`loop_misuse`). It uses `asyncio.run_coroutine_threadsafe` and waits `hook_write_timeout_seconds` (default 5s, floor 0.1s, ceiling 30s). It is an **early-return post-dispatch waiter**, not a settlement barrier: the hook thread is not kept blocked until the backend reports `Delivered`.

The **atomic dispatch marker** is the same post-dispatch line 2.2.15 / `WriteCoordinator.run_sequence` already use: the coordinator has accepted the request under the per-terminal lock and the backend `write_*` await has started. Pre-dispatch timeout or cancel produces no PTY mutation, no latch, and no lock held past return; the caller sees cancelled/failed. Post-dispatch timeout or cancel returns `IndeterminateWrite` to the hook waiter immediately after the unresolved-action latch is inserted. Ownership of the continuing work is the **runner-owned shielded Task**: that Task retains the per-terminal lock until the backend await settles (`Delivered`, `IndeterminateWrite`, or typed error). The already-returned hook waiter is never resumed. Late completion or late failure is observed only by that Task: it records the outcome against the already-latched `action_key` (a late `Delivered` does not clear the latch, so automatic retry stays suppressed) and never retries. Runner-loop shutdown drains every in-flight bridge Task for `hook_write_shutdown_timeout_seconds` (default 5s, floor 0.1s, ceiling 30s). That drain is owned here: extract the helper into `src/gobby/runner_lifecycle_terminal_effects.py` and call it from `_run_graceful_shutdown_sequence` in `src/gobby/runner_lifecycle_shutdown.py` **before** `_close_managers_and_storage` so terminal services and storage are still open when the timestamp is persisted. A still-unsettled backend is recorded `IndeterminateWrite`, `automatic_write_quarantined_at` and `automatic_write_quarantine_action_key` are persisted together on the terminal row **before** the coordinator lock is released, and that terminal is quarantined from new automatic writes until an **effect-specific positive observation** of that stored key or an operator write. An unchanged or ambiguous snapshot after restart — including the delivered-and-consumed versus never-delivered pair this section already says can look identical — leaves both columns set. Observing a different unresolved key does not clear them. A late settlement observed while still quarantined does not admit a different automatic `action_key`; a late `Delivered` after a valid resolution does not re-quarantine. The coordinator restores the pair from the row at construction. The durable `unresolved_writes` map still suppresses only matching keys; the quarantine is the terminal-wide bit those keys do not provide. Every touched hand-maintained production file in this leaf, including `runner_lifecycle_shutdown.py` after the extract, finishes below 1,000 lines. 3.1 still depends on this leaf for the shared shutdown call site. Shutdown never calls `asyncio.run` on the loop thread. There is no second generic `asyncio.run` / `Future.result()` path around terminal writes.

**Acceptance:**

- 2.4.1 - No monitor, cleanup, kill, or capture module reads `tmux_session_name` or calls tmux capture/send directly; all resolve through `TerminalRuntime` (source-scan test). test: `tests/terminals/test_no_direct_tmux_consumers.py::test_monitors_use_runtime`.
- 2.4.2 - Attention respond routes through the write coordinator with unchanged `stage: none|partial` failure semantics, is authorized without the client lease, holds the coordinator lock through recapture, fingerprint/episode CAS, and backend write, and serializes with a concurrent lease-holder write on the same terminal on both backends. file: `src/gobby/servers/routes/attention.py`.
- 2.4.3 - The existing lifecycle/idle/cleanup/kill/capture suites pass on the tmux backend with their observable assertions intact after migration to the 2.1 fixture helper. test: `tests/agents/test_agent_cleanup.py`.
- 2.4.4 - Restart reconciliation and stale-pending reaping operate on terminal rows, including reaping a pending row whose backend spawn failed, and both honor the 2.3 in-doubt deadline. test: `tests/agents/test_lifecycle_reconciliation.py::test_pending_terminal_reaped_after_failed_spawn`.
- 2.4.5 - Every module this deliverable owns is clean of the field and of direct tmux calls — wake delivery, session coordination, the four MCP agent tool modules, attention routing, task recovery, terminal_cleanup, lifecycle_reconciliation, watchdog/recovery, the sessions terminal facade, both websocket modules, the five write-path consumers named above, and the `spawn_executor_support` prompt-injection sends — with each migrated site keeping its observable behavior, including the wake and sessions-terminal test doubles named in Targets. Matches remaining in `runner_broadcasting.py` (2.5) and `runner_lifecycle_processes.py` (3.1) are expected here; the repo-wide gate is 2.5.13. test: `tests/terminals/test_no_direct_tmux_consumers.py::test_owned_consumers_are_backend_neutral`.
- 2.4.6 - MCP `send_keys` and `capture_output` resolve a terminal row and succeed against a native terminal with unchanged tool contracts, `test_terminal.py` and `test_compact_self.py` assert that resolution rather than a raw tmux manager, and task recovery leaves a live native run's task alone while still recovering a genuinely never-started run. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/test_sessions_terminal_tools.py::test_send_and_capture_are_backend_neutral`.
- 2.4.7 - A capture-before-kill whose snapshot was truncated persists `truncated`, `dropped_bytes`, and `total_bytes` with the text, and a reader of the stored artifact can tell a complete capture from a truncated one; an untruncated capture stores the same fields with zero drop, and a tmux capture at `history-limit` persists the unknown counters as unknown rather than as zero. test: `tests/agents/test_capture.py::test_truncation_metadata_is_persisted`.
- 2.4.8 - All five call-sweep consumers resolve a terminal row and write through the runtime, and each succeeds against a native terminal: plan-keystroke dispatch, plan approval, persona configuration, observed-session message delivery, and the post-compaction continuation prompt. A `gcode grep -F 'send_keys' src` sweep returns no `TmuxSessionManager` call site outside `src/gobby/agents/tmux/` and the external-session path. test: `tests/terminals/test_no_direct_tmux_consumers.py::test_write_path_consumers_are_backend_neutral`.
- 2.4.10 - Indeterminate writes suppress their own logical actions on later ticks and nothing else: two different actions can become unresolved without either overwriting the other in `unresolved_writes`; each matching periodic evaluation is suppressed and reported as suppressed instead of sending bytes; a third action and any operator-initiated write proceed; positive observation or operator resolution clears only its matching key, terminal exit clears the map, and a daemon restart preserves every unresolved key. test: `tests/terminals/test_write_outcomes.py::test_unresolved_write_latch_suppresses_only_the_same_action`.
- 2.4.11 - Wake delivery is backend-neutral and honest about indeterminacy: `_send_tmux_session_wake`'s `Escape`/text/`Enter` sequence resolves a terminal row and runs through `WriteCoordinator.run_sequence`, `tests/events/test_wake.py` no longer patches a tmux pane sender for Gobby-owned rows, an `IndeterminateWrite` at any step sends no remaining step, records no delivery, and does not fall through to another delivery method, and `WakeDispatcher` reports the wake as indeterminate rather than delivered or failed. test: `tests/test_runner_init.py::test_wake_send_aborts_on_indeterminate_write_without_recording_delivery`.
- 2.4.9 - An indeterminate write abstains rather than advancing, at every consumer: a multi-step keystroke sequence whose second send returns `IndeterminateWrite` sends no further step and reports indeterminate; the attention route leaves the entry open and re-captures instead of clearing it; idle reprompt and prompt auto-answer leave their retry counters unchanged and re-evaluate from a fresh snapshot on the next tick; and no consumer re-sends the same bytes. test: `tests/terminals/test_write_outcomes.py::test_indeterminate_write_abstains_at_every_consumer`.
- 2.4.12 - `NativePlanActionService` resolves a terminal row and writes through the runtime; its focused suite passes on tmux and native. test: `tests/communications/test_native_plan_actions.py::test_plan_actions_use_runtime`.
- 2.4.13 - The remaining direct tmux test seams named in Targets migrate with their production callers: compact-self readiness, attention respond, session control, voice warmup, liveness monitor, and attention-state each resolve a terminal row and drive runtime/coordinator fakes, retaining an explicit external-tmux case only where row resolution selects tmux. Session-control and voice-warmup patches of `session_observe.get_tmux_manager_for_context` follow the replaced facade in `session_observe.py`, not a leftover tmux export. test: `tests/terminals/test_no_direct_tmux_consumers.py::test_remaining_runtime_test_seams_are_owned`.
- 2.4.14 - `HookManagerFactory` and `HookManager` construct `SessionCoordinator` **and** `EventHandlers` with the same composition-root `TerminalManager`, runtime registry, write coordinator, and `TerminalEffectBridge` the runner holds; a coordination send and a SessionStart `upsert_external` use those instances; no second manager or registry is constructed. test: `tests/hooks/test_session_coordinator.py::test_coordinator_receives_composition_root_terminal_services`.
- 2.4.21 - A reconstructed coordinator after restart still refuses a new automatic `action_key` once `unresolved_writes` holds 32 keys or would exceed 64 KiB; one-over-limit saturation returns `unresolved_write_capacity` with no PTY mutation; operator resolution of an existing key and terminal exit still clear. test: `tests/terminals/test_write_outcomes.py::test_unresolved_write_capacity_survives_restart`.
- 2.4.22 - Hard process death at each write-ahead boundary survives restart: kill after the marker and before dispatch, after PTY effect and before the runtime result, and after `IndeterminateWrite` exposure each restore the same `action_key` and suppress that automatic action with no second PTY mutation; a kill after a durable `Delivered` leaves the key absent so a later different automatic action may proceed; a `run_sequence` kill between `Escape` and `Enter` restores the sequence key and sends no remaining `Enter` after restart. test: `tests/terminals/test_write_outcomes.py::test_write_ahead_hard_kill_suppresses_retry_across_restart`.
- 2.4.15 - The registered MCP `send_keys`, `compact_self`, and `capture_output` closures receive the same composition-root `TerminalManager`, runtime registry, and `WriteCoordinator` the runner holds: `_factory.py`, `registries.py`, and `servers/http.py` forward those instances in the daemon HTTP process, and an identity test fails if any of those hops constructs a second set. Stdio is excluded from this identity chain. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/test_registries.py::test_terminal_tools_receive_composition_root_services`.
- 2.4.16 - Stdio is proxy-only: `create_stdio_mcp_server` constructs no `TerminalManager`, runtime registry, or `WriteCoordinator`; `session_manager` is `None`; the `setup_internal_registries` return is discarded; registered stdio tools reach the daemon HTTP MCP surface through `DaemonProxy`. test: `tests/mcp_proxy/test_stdio_proxy.py::test_stdio_constructs_no_terminal_service_set`.
- 2.4.17 - One injected `TerminalEffectBridge` (`src/gobby/terminals/sync_bridge.py`) is the only sync-to-async write seam. It may be called only from a non-loop hook thread; calling it on the runner loop thread is typed `loop_misuse` and dispatches nothing. The bridge submits the coordinator future onto the runner loop and waits a bounded `hook_write_timeout_seconds`. Cancel or timeout **before** the atomic dispatch marker: no PTY bytes, no latch, lock released, caller sees cancelled/failed. Timeout or cancel **after** that marker: the hook waiter returns `IndeterminateWrite` immediately with the unresolved-action latch set; the runner-owned shielded Task retains the per-terminal lock through backend settlement; a late completion or late failure is observed only by that Task, is not delivered to the waiter, and is never retried. Automatic retry remains suppressed by the latch even if the late result is `Delivered`. test: `tests/terminals/test_sync_bridge.py::test_timeout_cancel_late_completion_and_loop_misuse`.
- 2.4.18 - A timeout racing the dispatch marker is exclusively pre-dispatch (no bytes, no latch) or exclusively post-dispatch (latch plus runner-owned settlement), never both. A never-settling backend leaves the hook waiter with `IndeterminateWrite` and keeps the lock on the runner Task until `hook_write_shutdown_timeout_seconds`. Runner-loop shutdown drains or quarantines every in-flight post-dispatch Task, records `IndeterminateWrite` at that deadline, persists `automatic_write_quarantined_at` and `automatic_write_quarantine_action_key` before releasing the lock, and rejects new automatic writes on the quarantined terminal. After a daemon restart that reconstructs the coordinator from two unresolved keys, only a positive observation of the stored quarantine key or an operator write lifts the pair; observing the other key leaves it set. An indistinguishable delivered-and-consumed versus never-delivered snapshot leaves both columns set. A late-effect race before valid resolution keeps a different automatic action refused; after an effect-specific positive observation of the stored key or an operator write, a later automatic action is admitted. test: `tests/terminals/test_sync_bridge.py::test_shutdown_drain_and_timeout_dispatch_race`.
- 2.4.20 - `_run_graceful_shutdown_sequence` calls the extracted drain helper before closing terminal services and storage: an in-flight post-dispatch Task is drained or quarantined at `hook_write_shutdown_timeout_seconds`, the timestamp is durable across that close, and a reconstructed coordinator after restart still refuses a different automatic `action_key` until effect-specific observation or an operator write. `runner_lifecycle_shutdown.py` and `runner_lifecycle_terminal_effects.py` each finish below 1,000 lines. test: `tests/test_runner_shutdown.py::test_shutdown_drains_terminal_effects_before_storage_close`.
- 2.4.19 - Codex post-spawn prompt delivery in `spawn_executor_support.py` uses `WriteCoordinator.run_sequence` rather than `tmux.send_keys`: the focused suite no longer patches a tmux sender for that path; an `IndeterminateWrite` on the prompt step sends no Enter and records no delivery. test: `tests/agents/test_spawn_executor.py::test_codex_prompt_aborts_on_indeterminate_without_enter`.

- **WS protocol rename (breaking, no aliases — C1).** `tmux_attach`/`tmux_detach`/`tmux_resize`/`tmux_list_sessions`/`tmux_create_session`/`tmux_kill_session`/`tmux_session_event` become `terminal_attach`/`terminal_detach`/`terminal_resize`/`terminal_list`/`terminal_create`/`terminal_kill`/`terminal_event`, keyed by durable `terminal_id` instead of `{session_name, socket}`. The `terminal_input`/`terminal_output` field misnamed `run_id` (actually the streaming id) becomes `terminal_id`. The list payload rows carry `{terminal_id, backend, ownership, state, title, session_id, agent_run_id, dims}` — eliminating the socket-qualified name joins that produced the cross-socket identity collisions documented in the shipped web-terminal plan (see O1). **The rename covers results and server-initiated events, not only requests**: `tmux_attach_result` and `tmux_create_result` (`src/gobby/servers/websocket/tmux.py:364`, `:435`) become `terminal_attach_result` and `terminal_create_result`, and the frontend's exhaustive switch (`useTmuxSessions.ts:183` `case 'tmux_attach_result'`) plus its wire fixtures are renamed with them. A request-only rename would pass a dispatch-table scan while leaving legacy result names live on the wire, so the acceptance below scans emitted types and the frontend union as well as the dispatch table.
- **The rename's real blast radius is the test doubles, and it is larger than the production surface.** `gcode grep` for the legacy message names across `src`, `tests`, and `web` returns matches in fourteen files, and the majority are fixtures rather than production code: `web/src/hooks/__tests__/useTmuxSessions.test.ts` (35 sites), `tests/servers/test_tmux_mixin.py` (26 — the backend WS mixin suite, which both emits and asserts the legacy names), `web/tests/terminal-colors.spec.ts` (11 — the Playwright end-to-end fake that speaks the WebSocket protocol to the browser and would keep the old names alive in the only test that exercises the real renderer), `tests/servers/websocket/test_broadcast.py` (4), `tests/servers/test_websocket_server_disconnects.py` (2), `tests/agents/test_kill.py` (2), `tests/test_runner_lifecycle.py` (1), and `tests/agents/test_capture_consumers.py` (1); production sites are `src/gobby/servers/websocket/tmux.py` (12), `web/src/hooks/useTmuxSessions.ts` (12), `src/gobby/servers/websocket/server.py` (6), `src/gobby/servers/websocket/broadcast.py` (4), `src/gobby/runner_broadcasting.py` (2), and `src/gobby/agents/kill.py` (1). Under a no-compatibility flip (C1), a fake that still speaks the old protocol is not a stale comment — it is a test asserting a contract the server no longer honors, and the Playwright fake in particular would pass while the real UI broke. Every one of those files is migrated in this deliverable, and the acceptance is a zero-match sweep for the legacy request, result, and event names plus the `run_id` correlation key, run over `src`, `tests`, and `web` together.
- **`terminal_attach_result` is what makes the lease messages usable.** Every lease message below is scoped by `attachment_id`, and the browser has no way to construct one — the daemon mints it. `terminal_attach` therefore answers with `terminal_attach_result{terminal_id, attachment_id, rows, cols, backend, frame_delivery, lease_generation}` on success and a typed error result carrying the same `terminal_id` on failure, and the frontend keys its per-pane state on the returned `attachment_id`.
