Every successful attach is observe-only; there is no `mode` field on `terminal_attach` or `terminal_attach_result`. `terminal_create_result` and `terminal_kill`/`terminal_list` results are likewise defined as backend-neutral shapes rather than being left implicit, so the frontend has one exhaustive union to match on.
- **Writer-lease messages.** The daemon's lease policy needs wire shapes on this surface, or the web viewer cannot implement it. Four messages carry it, all scoped by a connection-local `attachment_id` the daemon mints on `terminal_attach` (one browser tab may hold several attachments, and `terminal_id` alone cannot say which): `terminal_take_control{terminal_id, attachment_id, takeover}` requests the lease; `terminal_release_control{terminal_id, attachment_id}` releases it while the pane stays attached and rendering; `terminal_control_result{attachment_id, granted, reason, lease_generation}` answers both, where `reason` is a typed refusal or outcome (`held`, `released`, `terminal_gone`, `host_unavailable`, `stale_attachment`) rather than prose; and the server-initiated `terminal_lease_lost{attachment_id, holder, lease_generation}` event tells a displaced viewer to render read-only. `terminal_release_control` is idempotent: a second release, a release after disconnect cleanup, or a release of an attachment that never held the lease returns `granted: false, reason: "released"` and does not error. A stale `attachment_id` is `stale_attachment`. 3.3 uses this message on focus change instead of detaching. `terminal_attach` carries `frame_delivery: "direct"|"proxy"` (default `proxy`) and **no** `mode`. Every attach is observe-only. `terminal_take_control` is the sole control-acquisition transition: it is the only verb that can grant, hold, take over, or publish a new `lease_generation` for write authority. `direct` mints the `attachment_id` and joins the lease record without opening a daemon frame relay — that is the local `gclient` path, which already reads `gterm-frames.sock`. `proxy` is the browser path: the daemon opens a host frame attach and relays `terminal_output` and, when present, `terminal_attach_history` (4.3). On daemon-WS reconnect, a `direct` client re-registers every live pane and receives a fresh `attachment_id`; old ids are invalid; the pane stays observe/read-only until re-registered and then reacquires control through the normal take-control exchange.

- **One attachment finalizer, every termination path.** One generic finalizer in this deliverable atomically forgets the `attachment_id` and releases any lease it holds — `direct` and `proxy` alike — under the coordinator lock. `lease_generation` advances only when this finalization actually removes the current holder. Observer, already-released, displaced, and duplicate finalization retain the current authority generation. Captured notifications are emitted after lock release. Callers are: daemon-WS orderly close, abrupt socket loss, `terminal_detach`, host-frame EOF on a **proxy** relay the daemon owns, typed lag close on that proxy relay, connection-wide relay overflow or send timeout (every attachment on that WebSocket), and host loss. 4.3's proxy cleanup is not a second policy; it calls this finalizer. On `frame_delivery: "direct"` the daemon does **not** observe the host frame stream, so it cannot invoke the finalizer from host-frame EOF or lag itself: `gclient` must send `terminal_detach{terminal_id, attachment_id}` on frame EOF, typed lag close, epoch loss, or any other frame failure (3.3) so this same finalizer runs before the pane reattaches. The daemon does not invent a second EOF path. Control cannot be reacquired on the same `attachment_id` after finalization: the next writer must attach a fresh frame stream and take control again. Orderly close, abrupt socket loss, reconnect, concurrent takeover, and a second cleanup are all idempotent. A crashed `gclient` or a pane that no longer renders must not remain the lease holder. `terminal_input`, `terminal_paste`, and `terminal_resize` from a non-holding attachment are refused with the same typed reason instead of being silently discarded.

  **Server-initiated finalization is a client-visible transition.** Lease notifications do not cover an observe-only attachment that never held the lease, and proxy EOF, typed lag, relay overflow, and host loss can finalize one attachment while the WebSocket stays open. After the finalizer removes the attachment from daemon state, the still-open connection emits `terminal_attachment_finalized{terminal_id, attachment_id, reason, lease_generation}` where `reason` is typed (`detach`, `ws_close`, `ws_loss`, `proxy_frame_eof`, `proxy_lag`, `relay_overflow`, `host_loss`, `message_seq_overflow`) and `lease_generation` is the **current** authority generation (unchanged unless this attachment was the holder). Lifecycle events on this hop — at least `terminal_attachment_finalized`, `terminal_lease_lost`, and `terminal_control_result` — use reserved outbound capacity that frame/`terminal_output` traffic cannot consume. The daemon does **not** close the whole WebSocket for a single attachment's death when that reserved send succeeds. If the reserved lifecycle send itself times out or cannot be enqueued, the transport is a connection-wide failure: close the WebSocket so every remaining attachment and its reassembly are torn down by this same finalizer. The browser reducer owned by this leaf marks that `attachment_id` non-live and releases all reassembly accounting for it before accepting later messages; a fragment, output, or lease notification for a finalized id is dropped and creates no state. Apply `terminal_attachment_finalized` idempotently by exact `attachment_id`; do not require a greater `lease_generation`. gclient's matching reducer lives in 3.3.

  The handler check is not the linearization point. Each lease record carries a monotonic `lease_generation`. Grant, release of the current holder, takeover, and finalization of the current holder take the 2.2 per-terminal coordinator lock and increment that generation, then capture the `terminal_attach_result`, `terminal_control_result`, and `terminal_lease_lost` records — each carrying that generation — before releasing the lock and sending the captured records. Observer, already-released, displaced, and duplicate finalization take the same lock and do not increment. A delayed grant must not invert a later takeover: the browser reducer owned by this leaf rejects those authority notifications only when `lease_generation` is **below** the highest generation already seen for that `attachment_id`. Equal-generation non-authority-changing results (`held` refusals and idempotent already-released `terminal_control_result`) are applied. Finalized-id tombstones still drop later messages for that id. gclient's matching ignore rule is 3.3.2. Lease-gated writes and resizes enqueue `WriteRequest`/`resize` with `attachment_id` and `expected_lease_generation`; the coordinator revalidates immediately before the backend effect (2.2.16). A generation that moved while the request waited is `stale_attachment` / `held` with no PTY mutation.
- **Attachment-local viewport vs lease-gated PTY resize.** 3.2 already splits host-frame `SetViewport` from control `resize`. The daemon WS must not collapse them: `terminal_set_viewport{terminal_id, attachment_id, rows, cols}` is allowed for every live attachment, validates the shared 2.2 bounds, and forwards only as that attachment's host-frame `SetViewport` (or, for a `direct` client, is a no-op on the daemon because the client already sent `SetViewport` itself). `terminal_resize` stays lease-gated and runtime-dispatched. A read-only browser observer can change its rendered size without mutating the owned PTY or an external tmux owner's geometry.
- **Attachment-local scroll offset.** Native copy-mode cannot move within host scrollback with `SetViewport` alone. `terminal_set_scroll_offset{terminal_id, attachment_id, rows_from_live_edge}` is allowed for every live attachment, is not lease-gated, and forwards only as that attachment's host-frame `SetScrollOffset` (a `direct` client already sent the frame verb and the daemon is a no-op). `0` is the live edge. The value is a non-negative integer clamped to the host's current max offset; the next server frame is `ScrollOffsetApplied{applied_rows, max_rows}` (WS `terminal_scroll_offset_applied`). It never reaches PTY input, mouse-report, or `TIOCSWINSZ`. Tmux observers keep scrolling in client-local copy-mode from the `AttachHistory` they already hold.
- **Paste is a first-class WS write.** `terminal_paste{terminal_id, attachment_id, text, client_write_seq}` is lease-gated like `terminal_input` and dispatched as `write_paste`. The daemon, not the client, applies herdr 0.8.0 `paste_payload`. Oversize (>1 MiB UTF-8) is a typed `terminal_write_outcome` refusal on this message and does not drop the connection.

- **Every client write is correlated and reports a typed outcome.** `terminal_input` and `terminal_paste` are not fire-and-forget. Each carries `client_write_seq`, a per-`attachment_id` JSON number in `[0, terminal_ws_safe_integer_max]` (same ceiling as `message_seq` / `lease_generation`). Missing, non-integer, negative, or `2^53` values are refused typed with no PTY mutation. The 2.5 handler derives the coordinator `action_key` as `ws:{attachment_id}:{client_write_seq}` and sets `origin: "operator"`. Automatic writers keep their own logical keys and never use this prefix. After the coordinator returns, the still-open connection emits `terminal_write_outcome{terminal_id, attachment_id, client_write_seq, outcome, reason}` where `outcome` is `delivered` | `indeterminate` | `refused` and `reason` is a typed code (`null` on `delivered`; `indeterminate_backend` on a lost tmux or native response; lease/`stale_attachment`/`unresolved_write_capacity`/oversize/`write_seq_expired`/`write_seq_conflict`/`write_seq_capacity` on refusal). Write outcomes are ordinary request/response traffic, **not** lifecycle-reserve events: they are too frequent to share the 16-entry reserve. Per attachment, under the coordinator lock, the handler atomically maintains a highest-admitted `client_write_seq` plus at most **64** in-flight or completed records keyed by that seq and a fingerprint of write kind (`input` | `paste`) and the exact payload bytes. A new seq is accepted only when it is **strictly above** the high-water mark; skips are allowed and advance the high-water to the admitted seq. A cached seq with a matching fingerprint joins an in-flight admission or returns the recorded outcome with no second coordinator call and no second PTY mutation. A cached or high-water seq whose fingerprint differs (other kind, or same kind with different bytes) is refused `write_seq_conflict` with no PTY mutation. An absent seq at or below the high-water — including a gap that was skipped and an evicted completed record — is refused `write_seq_expired` with no PTY mutation. When 64 records are already in flight, a new unique seq is refused `write_seq_capacity` with no PTY mutation; admitting a new seq may evict the oldest completed record to stay at 64, and that evicted seq then follows the expired rule. Gaps above the high-water that have not been admitted are not cached and are not expired until a later higher seq admits and they fall at or below the new high-water without a record. The browser reducer owned by this leaf applies an outcome only for a live `attachment_id` and that exact seq: `delivered` clears the in-flight mark and leaves the holder writable; `refused` clears the in-flight mark and stays writable unless the reason is lease-related; `indeterminate` enters an explicit **uncertain read-only** state for that attachment, does **not** resend, and stays read-only until that `attachment_id` is finalized and a fresh attach plus `terminal_take_control` succeed. Socket loss before the outcome arrives is local-uncertain and also does not resend. gclient's matching reducer is 3.3.22. The 4.3 hop exercises lost tmux and native responses through the real daemon-to-client path.
- **Dimension validation.** Only operations whose request schema carries rows/cols validate them: `terminal_resize`, `terminal_set_viewport`, and `terminal_create`. `terminal_attach` carries no dimensions after attach-result geometry is server-supplied. `GET /api/terminals` and `GET /api/terminals/{id}` are read-only and are not dimension ingresses. Hello and control `spawn`/`resize` stay in 3.2.
- **Terminal-WS golden corpus.** C1 says these messages survive Python→Rust byte-for-byte. `tests/servers/fixtures/terminal_ws_golden/` is the canonical JSON fixture set. This deliverable **commits every file listed in Targets** — attach (no `mode`), attach_result (including `frame_delivery`, no `mode`), attach_result_error, detach, resize, set_viewport, set_scroll_offset, scroll_offset_applied, list, create, create_result, kill, input (includes `client_write_seq`), write_outcome, write_outcome_indeterminate, write_outcome_refused, write_outcome_conflict, write_outcome_expired, write_outcome_capacity, output, attach_history, fragment, fragment_last, paste (includes `client_write_seq`), take_control, release_control, control_result, lease_lost, attachment_finalized, event, typed_error. `terminal_attach_history{terminal_id, attachment_id, text, truncated, dropped_bytes, total_bytes}` is the daemon-WS shape for a host `AttachHistory` frame (3.4 / 4.3): one typed event per attachment, before that attachment's first `terminal_output` keyframe, never packed into `terminal_output`. **Fragmentation is a first-class WS shape, not a 4.3 comment.** When a complete `terminal_output` or `terminal_attach_history` JSON (envelope plus escaping) would be ≥ 2 MiB, the producer emits `terminal_ws_fragment` slices instead of the unfragmented event:

  `terminal_ws_fragment{type, event, terminal_id, attachment_id, message_seq, fragment_index, more, encoding, payload}`

  `type` is always `"terminal_ws_fragment"`. `event` is the logical type (`terminal_output` or `terminal_attach_history`) and is identical on every slice of one message. `message_seq` is a per-`attachment_id` JSON number assigned before slicing and incremented once per fragmented logical event; two attachments never share a sequence space. `fragment_index` is a 0-based JSON number (uint32 range). Indexes are contiguous, with `more: false` only on the last slice. `encoding` is `"utf8-b64"`. `payload` is the base64 of one contiguous UTF-8 byte slice of the **complete serialized original JSON**; the reducer concatenates decoded bytes and UTF-8-decodes once. Each fully wrapped fragment JSON, including envelope and payload escaping, is preflighted and must be strictly below 2 MiB; the producer takes the largest payload that still satisfies that bound. The reconstructed complete JSON — the concatenated decoded payloads — is bounded by the shared `terminal_ws_fragment_max_reassembly_bytes` (**16 MiB**) that 4.3's producer and reducer both enforce: a legal host frame's worst-case escaped event must fit; one byte over is typed `fragment_too_large`. The same hop also enforces `terminal_ws_fragment_max_socket_reassembly_bytes` (**64 MiB**) as the per-WebSocket sum of decoded in-flight reassembly bytes across live attachments: the reducer reserves the incoming slice before append and refuses overflow typed without applying an event. An `attachment_id` is live only after a successful `terminal_attach_result` and before `terminal_attachment_finalized` (or connection teardown) for that id. Buffers start only for a currently live `attachment_id`; a fragment after detach, `terminal_attachment_finalized`, or finalizer is dropped and creates no state. Unfragmented events that already fit stay unfragmented.

  **Cross-language counters stay inside JavaScript's safe integer range.** `message_seq`, `lease_generation`, and `client_write_seq` are JSON numbers in `[0, terminal_ws_safe_integer_max]` where that constant is **2^53-1** (`9007199254740991`). They are never strings, never floats, and never a uint64 that `JSON.parse` would alias. Python and TypeScript emitters owned by this leaf refuse a value of `2^53` (`safe_integer_overflow`) and do not increment past the ceiling: a `message_seq` that would overflow finalizes the attachment (and emits `terminal_attachment_finalized` with `reason: "message_seq_overflow"`) instead of wrapping; a `lease_generation` increment that would overflow is treated as unreachable host corruption and refuses the mutation typed; a `client_write_seq` of `2^53` is a typed `terminal_write_outcome` refusal with no PTY mutation. `useTmuxSessions.ts` compares them as exact integers. gclient's matching decode, comparison, and emit-refusal live in 3.3 — this leaf has no `gclient` Targets. Decimal-string encoding is not used: the counters increment once per fragmented event or lease mutation and cannot approach the ceiling in a live session, so a string wire type would add a second representation without buying a reachable value. `terminal_detach` and `terminal_resize` are live request shapes on this surface and must be pinned here; a request-only rename that omitted them would leave two declared verbs unfrozen. 3.3, 4.1, and 4.3 consume those same owned bytes and do not add a second corpus. Python emitters/decoders in this deliverable and the TypeScript hook fixtures regenerate them in the same change as any protocol-version bump — the same rule 3.2 sets for host wire fixtures. gclient regenerates its decoder against those same bytes in 3.3.
- **One authority per terminal, and the host is not a party to it.** The lease is a daemon-held record keyed by `terminal_id`, owned by `src/gobby/terminals/leases.py` and created in this deliverable — before any host exists, because the browser surface needs it first. The design decision this pins is *which* process grants control, and there is exactly one answer: the daemon.

  An earlier form of this rule let the host enforce the daemon's decision, by admitting an attachment to a `Control` mode when it presented a daemon-minted lease token. That is one authority on paper and two in practice, because a token is a *replica* of the lease record and every replica needs a consistency protocol: a mint endpoint, a binding to the attachment and host epoch, a TTL and a clock the two processes agree on, a replay rule, revocation on takeover so a displaced holder's token stops working, and a reconciliation pass for a daemon restart that finds a host still honoring a token it no longer has a record of. None of that machinery was specified, and none of it needs to exist, because the token is only there to let a client write *directly* to the host.

  So clients do not write to the host. The host exposes exactly two surfaces (3.2): a **control channel** the daemon alone connects to, authenticated by a control token no client is ever given (3.1, C1), carrying every write the host itself can perform — spawn, input, resize, kill, all of them against host-owned PTYs; and a **frame channel** local clients connect to, which is **observe-only** and has no input verb at all. A tmux-backed terminal's writes never appear on either channel: the host only *observes* tmux panes (3.4), and the daemon writes to them directly through `TmuxTerminalRuntime`, so "the daemon is the only writer" holds across both backends by two different routes. There is nothing to mint, nothing to expire, and nothing to revoke. gclient takes control by asking the daemon over the surface it already uses for roster, spawn, and respond, and its keystrokes travel the same daemon path the browser's already do (3.3) — one extra hop, on a path already proven for the shipped web terminal, in exchange for deleting an entire distributed-consistency problem. The host still serializes the writes it receives, because two daemon-issued writes to one PTY would interleave bytes, but it makes no admission decision and holds no authority state. If the daemon is unreachable, every client is observe-only by construction: frames keep flowing from the host and no write can be issued by anyone. The single-authority acceptance is therefore a source-level fact — the frame protocol has no write verb — rather than a state machine to test.
- **Every attachment path acquires it, including the one that predates the host.** That includes the existing `TmuxPTYBridge` (`src/gobby/agents/tmux/pty_bridge.py`, constructed at `src/gobby/servers/websocket/tmux.py:51`). Without that, a web tab and a gclient pane would each believe it controls the same physical pane, because the bridge attaches its own `tmux` client with no knowledge of any lease. The bridge therefore acquires and releases the same lease as any other attachment, and refuses input and resize when it does not hold it. Two owner-visible mutations in the current attach path also go away: the bridge calls `set_option(session_name, "status", "off")` and `set_option(session_name, "mouse", "on")` on the target session (`:338`, `:341`), which for an `external` terminal reconfigures a session the user owns. For `ownership='external'` those calls are removed outright — the viewer renders what the owner configured — and for `gobby`-owned sessions they stay, since Gobby is the owner. Unifying the *transport* (one tmux client instead of one per surface) needs the pane tap that 3.4 builds, so 4.3 retires the bridge for the browser surface; this deliverable makes the authority single immediately, so the interim state is one writer and correct refusals rather than two competing clients.
- **`terminal_create` gets the same row owner as an agent spawn.** `_handle_tmux_create_session` (`src/gobby/servers/websocket/tmux.py:409`) creates a tmux session directly today with no agent run behind it, so retaining `terminal_create` without a row owner would put a second, unaudited terminal-producing ingress next to `execute_spawn` and break the sole-owner invariant of 2.1. The handler is rewritten to call the same row-owning spawn primitive `execute_spawn` uses, through a terminal-only request that carries no agent run: it allocates the `terminal_id` and `spawn_key`, writes the `pending` row with `ownership='gobby'` and the configured default backend, and that primitive — not `runner_broadcasting` — performs native `reserve_observer` before `prepare_spawn`, then persist, bind, and `commit_spawn` (4.2). The handler performs the one `promote_to_live` CAS — with the identical per-outcome matrix (backend failure, timeout, cancellation, lost CAS terminating the just-created terminal, native reserve refusal before fork). When 5.3 flips the default to native, this ingress stays inside the same `max_attachments_total - 4` entitlement ceiling. The row carries the creating session's `session_id` and the connection's project, so a web-created terminal appears in `GET /api/terminals` and is reaped like any other. `terminal_kill` on such a terminal routes through `terminate` and the same CAS.
- **`runner_broadcasting.py` migrates with the rename it feeds.** Its five `tmux_session_name` readers (`:184`, `:227`, `:251`, `:265`, plus the payload contract at `:162`) resolve terminal rows, and its output broadcast keys on `terminal_id` instead of `run_id` — the same misnamed field the rename fixes on the wire. This lands here rather than in 2.4 because the readers and the broadcast API they call have to change together; 4.2 then adds only the native frame-observer source behind the unchanged broadcast call.
- **Terminal REST surface.** New `src/gobby/servers/routes/terminals.py` registered in `_app_routes.py`: `GET /api/terminals` (filter by project/state/backend), `GET /api/terminals/{id}` (row + attach locator). This is gclient's roster complement; attention data stays on `/api/attention/*`.

  **Both inventory surfaces are paginated.** Exited and orphaned rows stay durable, so an unpaged `GET /api/terminals` or `terminal_list` would force unbounded materialization, JSON, transfer, and client allocation. Shared constants: `terminal_list_default_page_size` = **100**, `terminal_list_max_page_size` = **500**, `terminal_list_max_encoded_bytes` = **1 MiB**. Both surfaces take a stable cursor (`created_at`, `id` ascending) and a numeric `limit` clamped to the maximum; a page whose complete encoded JSON would exceed 1 MiB is cut before that bound and returns the last included row's cursor. Interactive rosters default to `state IN ('pending', 'live')`. History (`exited`, `orphaned`, or `states=all`) requires an explicit query and still pages. A mutation between pages must not duplicate or skip a row that kept its cursor key. `terminal_list` uses the same cursor/limit/default-filter contract; its golden list fixture is one default-filter page, not an unbounded dump.

  **Every unbounded roster consumer must drain pages.** The producer bound is not enough: `useTmuxSessions.ts` is the sole browser roster and today replaces state from one `terminal_list` response. After this leaf it accumulates pages in `(created_at, id)` order until `next_cursor` is absent. ID deduplication is required. A refresh or reconnect starts a new request/connection generation; stale continuation pages from an older generation are ignored and do not replace or interleave with the in-flight drain. The hook does not declare a display cap — it is an unbounded roster consumer and must follow every page. gclient's matching drain is 3.3.21.
- **Attention roster locator.** `_run_tmux_payload`/`_session_tmux_payload` in `routes/attention.py` are replaced by a backend-neutral `terminal` block `{terminal_id, backend, attach: AttachLocator}` from `attach_locator` (native socket computed locally, never a stored `host_socket`).
- **Discovery seeding, with the server generation resolved by both producers.** The SessionStart hook flow (`_session_start/flow.py`) and session liveness monitor upsert `external` terminal rows from `terminal_context` tmux keys, normalizing them into the canonical `locator_key` of 2.1 — socket path, tmux server pid, **tmux server start time**, and pane id — and writing through `upsert_external` with a null `spawn_key` and the discovering session's `project_id` **on insert only**, so two same-project producers racing on one pane converge on a single row and a replayed SessionStart is a no-op. A different-project discovery of that still-active locator is typed `project_ownership_conflict` at both producers: no row mutation, no occupying-row fields in the result. After the occupying row exits, the other project may insert. Both producers receive the **same** composition-root `TerminalManager`: SessionStart through `EventHandlers` (factory, 2.4.14), liveness through `src/gobby/servers/_app_lifecycle.py`. Neither producer constructs another manager. Session name, window id, and title are written as refreshable metadata, never as identity (2.1). The generation component is not optional for either producer: a discoverer that reads `#{pid}` and omits the start time computes a key that a restarted tmux server can reproduce by pid reuse, and the upsert would then merge a brand-new pane into the finished terminal's row — which is worse than a duplicate row, because the run history and any attachment follow the wrong physical pane. Both producers therefore read pid and start time in the same batched `display-message`, and neither may construct a key from a pid alone. Externally launched CLI sessions appear exactly once as terminals with `ownership='external'`; liveness transitions their state via the 2.1 CAS.

**Discovery must not steal a managed spawn that has not yet promoted.** `prepare_spawn` can create the tmux pane (and therefore a physical locator) before `execute_spawn` CASes `pending → live`. SessionStart and the liveness monitor that observe that pane in the gap must not `upsert_external` a second active row: the unique `locator_key` index would then collide with promotion, or two owners would claim one pane. Before `upsert_external`, both producers resolve a matching gobby-owned `pending` row by `terminal_id`/`spawn_key` (tmux session name is the `spawn_key`) and either wait for that promotion or update that row's refreshable metadata. An `external` row is created only when no managed identity exists for the locator. A deterministic prepare-to-promote discovery race is a required test.
- **Web.** `useTmuxSessions.ts` and `terminalSessions.ts` switch to the new message names and `terminal_id` keys (mechanical rename plus deletion of the socket-provenance join workarounds); `TerminalTab.tsx` and `TerminalSessionList.tsx` consume the same backend-neutral `TmuxSession` / `JoinedTerminalSession` shape. The four fixture consumers that still construct the legacy `{name, socket, pane_*}` objects — `SessionsTab.test.tsx`, `TerminalSessionList.test.tsx`, `TerminalTab.test.tsx`, and `terminalSessions.test.ts` — migrate with them so a focused web typecheck does not keep the old join contract alive. The wterm renderer and attach flow are otherwise unchanged. `web/tests/style-surfaces.spec.ts` ships a second Playwright WebSocket fake that still speaks `tmux_*`; rename it here or the only style-surface e2e keeps the old protocol alive.

**Acceptance:**

- 2.5.1 - No `tmux_*` name survives in any direction: the WS dispatch table serves only the backend-neutral request set keyed by `terminal_id` (source-scan of `server.py:288-322`), every emitted result and server-initiated event type is backend-neutral (source-scan of the emitting sites in `websocket/tmux.py` and `broadcast.py`), and a zero-match sweep over `src`, `tests`, and `web` finds no legacy request, result, or event name and no `run_id` terminal correlation key — covering the backend WS mixin suite, the broadcast and disconnect suites, the runner-lifecycle and kill suites, the frontend hook fixtures, and the Playwright WebSocket fake. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_rename.py::test_no_legacy_names_in_requests_results_or_events`.
- 2.5.2 - `GET /api/terminals` and `GET /api/terminals/{id}` return rows with attach locators through the real app; the list filter is the durable `project_id` column and still isolates after `session_id` or `agent_run_id` is cleared. The collection defaults to `pending|live`, honors cursor/`limit` (default 100, max 500, 1 MiB encoded), and requires an explicit `states` query for retained history. test: `tests/servers/test_terminals_routes.py::test_terminal_rest_surface`.
- 2.5.34 - Both inventory surfaces page: a project with more than 100 `pending|live` rows plus retained `exited` rows returns the default page without the exited set; an explicit history query pages with a stable cursor; inserting or exiting a row between pages neither duplicates nor skips a surviving cursor key; a page at `terminal_list_max_encoded_bytes` stays inside the budget. test: `tests/servers/test_terminals_routes.py::test_terminal_inventory_is_paginated`.
- 2.5.35 - `useTmuxSessions.ts` follows `terminal_list` continuation: more than 100 `pending|live` rows across multiple pages accumulate in `(created_at, id)` order with ID deduplication; an intervening insert or exit between pages neither duplicates nor skips a surviving key; a stale refresh or reconnect page fenced by request/connection generation is ignored. test: `web/src/hooks/__tests__/useTmuxSessions.test.ts::test_terminal_list_follows_pages`.
- 2.5.36 - `terminal_input` and `terminal_paste` carry `client_write_seq` and receive `terminal_write_outcome` with the same identity: the golden fixtures pin request and `delivered` / `indeterminate` / `refused` / `write_seq_conflict` / `write_seq_expired` / `write_seq_capacity` responses; the browser reducer enters uncertain read-only on `indeterminate` and does not resend; a matching-fingerprint duplicate seq returns the recorded outcome; a missing seq or `2^53` is refused with no PTY mutation; `useTmuxSessions.ts` compares the seq as an exact integer. gclient is 3.3.22; the real-hop lost-response cases are 4.3.15. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_golden.py::test_write_outcome_is_correlated`.
- 2.5.37 - The per-attachment write-seq ledger is total: a new seq above the high-water admits and may skip; two simultaneous same-seq same-fingerprint requests join one in-flight admission and mutate the PTY once; reusing a seq for a different kind or different payload is `write_seq_conflict` with no PTY mutation; an absent seq at or below the high-water, including an evicted completed record, is `write_seq_expired`; a 65th distinct in-flight seq is `write_seq_capacity`; the browser reducer in `useTmuxSessions.ts` clears that exact seq's in-flight mark, stays writable on those refusals (not lease-related), and does not resend input or paste. Server-wire goldens remain `test_write_seq_ledger_is_total`; reducer-state closure is this TypeScript case. The real-hop cases are 4.3.16. test: `web/src/hooks/__tests__/useTmuxSessions.test.ts::test_write_seq_refusals_clear_inflight_and_do_not_resend`.
- 2.5.3 - The attention roster carries the backend-neutral terminal block. test: `tests/servers/test_attention_roster.py::test_roster_terminal_block`.
- 2.5.4 - External CLI sessions seed `external` terminal rows at SessionStart and expire via liveness CAS. test: `tests/sessions/test_external_terminal_seeding.py::test_session_start_seeds_external_terminal`.
- 2.5.5 - The web terminal tab lists, attaches, streams, resizes, and detaches via the new messages; the socket-provenance join code is deleted. file: `web/src/hooks/useTmuxSessions.ts`.
- 2.5.6 - Concurrent SessionStart and liveness discovery of the same external pane produce one row, a replayed SessionStart changes nothing, a recycled pane id under a new tmux server pid produces a distinct row, and a recycled pane id under the *same* pid with a different `server_start_time` also produces a distinct row from both producers; a renamed session on an unchanged pane refreshes metadata on the same row. test: `tests/sessions/test_external_terminal_seeding.py::test_concurrent_and_replayed_discovery_is_idempotent`.
- 2.5.31 - Cross-project discovery cannot take or leak an active locator: concurrent A/B SessionStart and liveness against one live pane leave A's row and `project_id` unchanged and give B typed `project_ownership_conflict` with no occupying fields; after A exits, B's rediscovery inserts a new row. test: `tests/sessions/test_external_terminal_seeding.py::test_cross_project_discovery_is_conflict_until_exit`.
- 2.5.32 - SessionStart and SessionLivenessMonitor call `upsert_external` on the identical composition-root `TerminalManager`: factory/`EventHandlers` and `_app_lifecycle.py` forward that instance; object-identity assertions fail if either producer constructs another manager. test: `tests/sessions/test_external_terminal_seeding.py::test_discovery_producers_share_composition_root_manager`.
- 2.5.33 - `terminal_set_scroll_offset` is allowed for every live attachment and changes only that attachment's native host scroll: two observers sit at independent offsets; `0` jumps to the live edge; the daemon emits `terminal_scroll_offset_applied` with clamped `applied_rows`/`max_rows`; no PTY input, mouse-report, or `TIOCSWINSZ` occurs. Tmux observers do not send the host verb. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_viewport.py::test_two_observers_independent_scroll_offsets`.
- 2.5.7 - The lease messages behave per contract: every attach is observe-only (`terminal_attach` and `terminal_attach_result` have no `mode`), take-control is the sole grant path on a free terminal, a held terminal refuses with typed `held` unless `takeover` is set, the displaced attachment receives `terminal_lease_lost`, `terminal_release_control` returns the holder to observe without detaching, and input, paste, or resize from a non-holder is refused with a typed reason. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_lease.py::test_lease_request_result_and_lost_events`.
- 2.5.8 - Out-of-bounds dimensions on `terminal_resize`, `terminal_set_viewport`, and `terminal_create` are refused with a typed error before any row write or backend call. `terminal_attach` and GET `/api/terminals` do not carry rows/cols and are not this test. test: `tests/servers/test_terminals_routes.py::test_dimension_bounds_rejected`.
- 2.5.9 - `terminal_attach` returns `terminal_attach_result` carrying the minted `attachment_id` and `frame_delivery` and no `mode`, a failed attach returns a typed error result for the same `terminal_id`, and a client can construct and complete a `terminal_take_control` exchange using only fields the daemon sent it. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_lease.py::test_attach_result_supplies_attachment_identity`.
- 2.5.10 - The tmux PTY bridge acquires the same daemon lease as any other attachment and refuses input and resize when it does not hold it; attaching to an `external` tmux terminal changes no session option (`status` and `mouse` are byte-identical before and after), while a `gobby`-owned session keeps today's option behavior. test: `tests/servers/test_tmux_bridge_authority.py::test_bridge_shares_lease_and_leaves_external_options_alone`.
- 2.5.11 - A web `terminal_create` produces exactly one terminal row through the shared spawn primitive for every outcome (success, backend failure, timeout, cancellation, lost CAS, native reserve refusal before fork), with ownership, backend, project, and session recorded, visible in `GET /api/terminals`, reapable when its spawn fails, and no orphaned backend terminal in the lost-CAS or reserve-refusal case. Native saturation is 4.2.14. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_create.py::test_web_create_uses_row_owning_primitive`.
- 2.5.12 - The lease record is the only grant point and the host holds no authority state: exactly one holder exists per `terminal_id` across every acquisition path (WS take-control and the PTY bridge today, plus gclient's daemon-side take-control once P3 lands), a takeover moves the holder in one place and the displaced attachment is refused on its next write, and no lease token, TTL, or expiry exists anywhere in the codebase (source scan). test: `tests/terminals/test_lease_authority.py::test_single_grant_point_across_all_paths`.
- 2.5.13 - The whole-repo field gate closes here, at the last leaf of the P2 chain: `gcode grep -w tmux_session_name src tests web crates` returns matches only inside `src/gobby/agents/tmux/`, the `sessions.terminal_context` identity seam, and `runner_lifecycle_processes.py`'s restart-preservation reader deferred to 3.1, and a substring sweep finds no `daemon_stop_resume_tmux_session_name` or `daemon_stop_resume_planned_tmux_title` anywhere — every other site in the repository, across all files the live inventory command returns, resolves through terminal rows. This leaf owns and updates `test_repo_wide_field_sweep_is_empty` to that final allowed remainder; 2.4's earlier sweep of the same file keeps the wider mid-P2 remainder so 2.4 can close independently. test: `tests/terminals/test_no_direct_tmux_consumers.py::test_repo_wide_field_sweep_is_empty`.
- 2.5.14 - `terminal_set_viewport` is allowed for every live attachment and changes only that attachment's render size: two observers of one native terminal and one external tmux terminal can sit at different viewports while native PTY dimensions and the external owner's geometry stay unchanged, and `terminal_resize` from a non-holder is still refused. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_viewport.py::test_two_observers_independent_viewports`.
- 2.5.15 - `terminal_attach` with `frame_delivery: "direct"` mints an `attachment_id` and joins the lease record without opening a daemon host-frame attach; `proxy` still opens one. After a daemon-WS drop, a `direct` client that re-attaches receives a new `attachment_id`, the old id is refused, and the pane is observe-only until take-control succeeds again. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_lease.py::test_direct_delivery_registers_without_frame_relay`.
- 2.5.16 - `terminal_paste` from the lease holder reaches `write_paste`; from a non-holder it is refused typed; a payload over 1 MiB UTF-8 is refused typed without disconnecting. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_lease.py::test_paste_is_lease_gated_and_size_capped`.
- 2.5.17 - Python emitters/decoders reproduce `tests/servers/fixtures/terminal_ws_golden/` byte-for-byte for attach/result (no `mode`), detach, resize, viewport, set_scroll_offset/scroll_offset_applied, list/create/kill, input/output, write_outcome including conflict/expired/capacity, attach_history, fragment/fragment_last, paste, take-control/release-control/result/lease-lost, attachment_finalized, lifecycle events, and typed errors; the TypeScript hook fixtures consume the same files. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_golden.py::test_python_matches_terminal_ws_golden_corpus`.
- 2.5.24 - `terminal_attach_history{terminal_id, attachment_id, text, truncated, dropped_bytes, total_bytes}` is a first-class WS event: Python emits it from a host `AttachHistory` frame, the TypeScript reducer applies it before the first keyframe for that `attachment_id`, and a maximum-size history plus the first keyframe are two messages. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_golden.py::test_attach_history_precedes_first_output`.
- 2.5.25 - `terminal_ws_fragment` is in the golden corpus and the TypeScript union: `fragment.json` is a non-last slice and `fragment_last.json` is a last slice of the same `event`/`message_seq`; both carry `attachment_id`, contiguous `fragment_index`, `encoding: "utf8-b64"`, and a payload that is a byte slice of the complete original JSON; a fully wrapped fragment fixture is strictly below 2 MiB. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_golden.py::test_fragment_envelope_is_pinned`.
- 2.5.18 - Disconnect of a `direct` or `proxy` connection releases every attachment_id and any lease it held; a subsequent attach from a new connection does not see `held` from the dead holder; a second cleanup is a no-op; a takeover racing disconnect leaves exactly one holder. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_lease.py::test_disconnect_releases_direct_and_proxy_leases`.
- 2.5.19 - `terminal_release_control` drops the lease and leaves the attachment observing: a subsequent `terminal_input` is refused typed; a second release is `reason: "released"`; a stale attachment id is `stale_attachment`; a release racing takeover leaves exactly one holder; disconnect after release is a no-op. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_lease.py::test_release_control_is_idempotent_and_races_takeover`.
- 2.5.20 - Lease mutations and lease-gated writes share one linearization point: a take-control or a disconnect that **removes the current holder** and wins the coordinator lock while an already-validated `terminal_input`/`terminal_paste`/`terminal_resize` is queued increments `lease_generation`, refuses that queued effect typed, captures `terminal_lease_lost` under the lock, and sends it only after releasing the lock. An observe-only disconnect on the same terminal does not increment and does not refuse the holder's queued write. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_lease.py::test_lease_write_linearizes_with_takeover`.
- 2.5.21 - `terminal_attach_result`, `terminal_control_result`, and `terminal_lease_lost` carry `lease_generation`; the browser reducer owned by this leaf rejects only a generation **below** the highest already applied for that `attachment_id`. A delayed grant delivered after a later takeover is ignored and the reducer converges on the higher generation. An equal-generation `held` refusal and an equal-generation already-released `terminal_control_result` are applied and resolve the in-flight request. A finalized-id tombstone still drops later messages for that id. gclient's matching ignore rule is 3.3.2. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_lease.py::test_stale_lease_generation_is_ignored`.
- 2.5.26 - `message_seq`, `lease_generation`, and `client_write_seq` are JSON numbers in `[0, terminal_ws_safe_integer_max]` (`2^53-1`): Python and TypeScript emitters owned by this leaf refuse `2^53` and never emit strings or floats for these fields; `2^53-2` and `2^53-1` remain distinct in `useTmuxSessions.ts`. gclient decode, comparison, and emit-refusal are 3.3.18. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_golden.py::test_seq_and_lease_generation_are_safe_integers`.
- 2.5.22 - Before `upsert_external`, SessionStart and liveness resolve a matching gobby-owned pending `terminal_id`/`spawn_key` for that locator; a prepare-to-promote race produces one managed row and never an external duplicate. test: `tests/sessions/test_external_terminal_seeding.py::test_discovery_does_not_steal_unpromoted_managed_pane`.
- 2.5.23 - The generic attachment finalizer runs on daemon-WS close, `terminal_detach`, proxy host-frame EOF, proxy typed lag close, connection-wide relay overflow or send timeout (all attachments on that socket), and host loss: if this attachment holds the lease, the lease is released under the coordinator lock and `lease_generation` increments; otherwise the current generation is retained. Control cannot be reacquired on the finalized id until a fresh frame attach succeeds. A `direct` attachment whose frame stream dies while the daemon WS stays up is finalized only when the client sends `terminal_detach` (3.3.17); the daemon does not observe that host-frame EOF itself. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_lease.py::test_frame_and_lag_paths_finalize_the_lease`.
- 2.5.27 - After the finalizer removes an attachment from daemon state, a still-open WebSocket emits `terminal_attachment_finalized{terminal_id, attachment_id, reason, lease_generation}` on reserved lifecycle capacity (including observe-only attachments that never held the lease); the socket is not closed for that single attachment when the reserved send succeeds. The browser reducer marks the id non-live by exact `attachment_id` (no greater-generation requirement) and releases reassembly before later messages. A reserved-send failure is connection-wide close. gclient's matching reducer is 3.3.19. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_golden.py::test_attachment_finalized_is_pinned`.
- 2.5.28 - Observer finalization does not steal the holder's generation: attachment A holds N; observe-only B finalizes; A still writes with `expected_lease_generation=N`. B is stale after finalization and cannot take control. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_lease.py::test_observer_finalization_keeps_holder_writable`.
- 2.5.29 - Takeover then old-disconnect keeps the new holder writable: attachment A holds N; a **fresh** attachment B2 takes over (generation N+1); A's later disconnect finalizer retains N+1 and leaves B2 writable. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_lease.py::test_takeover_then_old_disconnect_keeps_new_holder_writable`.
- 2.5.30 - Equal-generation non-authority-changing results resolve: a `held` refusal and a duplicate `released` result at the current generation are applied by the browser reducer; a delayed lower grant is still ignored; a finalized attachment_id remains a tombstone. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_lease.py::test_equal_generation_held_and_release_results_are_applied`.

## P3: gterm Host and gclient Workspace

`kind: framing`

**Goal**: A daemon-supervised native PTY host and a Gobby-native terminal workspace client, covering the full milestone loop — project selection, roster/attention, tabs/panes, attach (native and tmux-embedded), spawn, respond, terminate, detach/reattach.

### 3.1 gterm host process and daemon supervision [category: code] (depends: P1, 2.2, 2.4)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `crates/gterminal/src/bin/gterm.rs`
- `crates/gterminal/Cargo.toml`
- `crates/gterminal/src/host/mod.rs`
- `crates/gterminal/src/host/control.rs`
- `src/gobby/terminals/host_manager.py`
- `src/gobby/config/terminal_host.py`
- `src/gobby/config/app.py::*` — scope-reason: host supervision, shutdown preservation, and health wiring per the body
- `src/gobby/runner.py::*` — scope-reason: host supervision, shutdown preservation, and health wiring per the body
- `src/gobby/app_context.py::*` — scope-reason: host supervision, shutdown preservation, and health wiring per the body
- `src/gobby/runner_init/servers.py::*` — scope-reason: host supervision, shutdown preservation, and health wiring per the body
- `src/gobby/runner_init/orchestration.py::*` — scope-reason: host supervision, shutdown preservation, and health wiring per the body
- `crates/gterminal/tests/host_lifecycle.rs`
- `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs`
- `tests/terminals/test_host_manager.py`
- `tests/terminals/test_host_shutdown_preservation.py`
- `tests/servers/test_admin_health.py`
- `src/gobby/runner_lifecycle_subsystems.py::*` — scope-reason: host supervision, shutdown preservation, and health wiring per the body
- `src/gobby/runner_lifecycle_processes.py::*` — scope-reason: host PID preservation lives in `_preserved_agent_terminal_pids`, not in shutdown.py
- `src/gobby/runner_lifecycle_shutdown.py::*` — scope-reason: host supervision, shutdown preservation, and health wiring per the body
- `src/gobby/servers/routes/admin/_health.py::*` — scope-reason: host supervision, shutdown preservation, and health wiring per the body
- `src/gobby/runner_rollback.py::*` — scope-reason: construction-failure rollback of the host supervisor
- `src/gobby/runner_lifecycle.py::*` — scope-reason: run_daemon failure paths must await ordered terminal rollback before PID cleanup or exit
- `tests/test_runner_rollback.py`

`gterm host` is the first long-running Rust child in the repo; the daemon owns its lifecycle:

- **This leaf owns the control surface its own supervision consumes.** Health checks, adoption, reconciliation, construction rollback, incompatible-host replacement, and full stop are all control-protocol calls, so the leaf that promises them must also implement them — a supervision leaf depending on a later protocol leaf would be a backwards edge. `crates/gterminal/src/host/control.rs` is therefore created here with the newline-delimited-JSON socket listener, the mandatory authentication handshake, the two read-only verbs `ping` and `list`, and the host-level `host_shutdown{grace_ms}` drain. The remaining verbs (`spawn`, `kill`, `resize`, `snapshot`, `write`, `subscribe_events`, `reserve_observer`) and the whole frame protocol belong to 3.2, which depends on this leaf. Nothing here spawns a terminal, so the split is clean: 3.1 proves a host exists, answers, can be inventoried, and can be drained; 3.2 makes it do work.

  **`host_shutdown` is the only host-wide destructive verb.** Per-terminal `kill` (3.2) cannot substitute for an atomic host transition. After a valid `hello`, `host_shutdown{grace_ms}` is authenticated like every other post-hello verb. `grace_ms` is a non-negative integer clamped to `shutdown_grace_seconds`. Acceptance is a typed `{accepted: true, draining: true}` response. The host then enters a single idempotent draining state: reject new `spawn` and `attach` work typed (`host_draining`); reap every prepared and committed process group with the configured grace; close both sockets; exit the serving process. A second `host_shutdown` while already draining returns the same accepted response and does not start a second drain. A lost response followed by verified host death (pidfile PID gone, socket gone) is success for the caller. 3.2 pins the request and response bytes in the golden corpus; 4.1 exposes the matching host-client method and uses it for replacement, full stop, and construction rollback.
- **Authentication before the first verb, with a credential the clients never hold.** The control socket is 0600 on a same-user path, and it carries every mutating verb, so the credential it accepts must not be the one every frame client already has. The first request on every control connection must be `hello{protocol_version, control_token}`, answered with `{host_epoch, version, protocol_version}`; any other request on an unauthenticated connection is refused with a typed `unauthenticated` error and the connection is closed, and a token mismatch or unsupported protocol version is refused typed before any terminal state is read. `ping` is an ordinary post-hello verb rather than a credential carrier, because a request that both establishes and uses a connection cannot report rejection ordering cleanly. The control token is `~/.gobby/gterm-control.token`: 32 bytes of `secrets.token_urlsafe`, written 0600 by the daemon before the first host spawn, read by the host at startup, rotated whenever a host is replaced rather than adopted, and handed to no other process — `gclient` never receives it and has no need for it, because it writes through the daemon (3.3). Presenting `local_cli_token` on the control socket is refused exactly like any other wrong token. The frame socket keeps its own `Hello{version, encoding, local_token, ...}` (3.2) validated against `~/.gobby/local_cli_token`; both handshakes appear in the golden corpus with negative cases, including the cross-credential ones. Neither credential defends against a same-uid process that reads `~/.gobby/` (C1 states that boundary plainly); the split exists so that a legitimate client's credential is not by itself write authority.

- **Host (Rust).** `crates/gterminal/src/host/` written fresh against the keep-set (herdr's 10k-line `server/headless.rs` is app-UI-coupled and not imported): a tokio process that owns `PaneRuntime` instances keyed by host-terminal id, binds `~/.gobby/gterm-control.sock` and `~/.gobby/gterm-frames.sock` with 0600 modes and stale-socket reaping (imported `ipc` layer) — serving the control verbs named below, with the frame protocol attached to the frames socket in 3.2 — generates a fresh `host_epoch` UUID per start, writes `~/.gobby/gterm.pid` and logs to `~/.gobby/logs/gterm.log` (env-filtered `tracing`). PTYs survive client disconnects by construction (host owns them). Graceful shutdown drains PTYs with SIGHUP then SIGKILL after a grace period. Cargo auto-discovers `src/bin/gterm.rs`; this leaf therefore edits `crates/gterminal/Cargo.toml` to declare `[[bin]] name = "gterm"` with `required-features = ["vt-engine"]` so `cargo build -p gobby-terminal` with default features stays Zig-free (1.2.6). The lockfile is not retargeted here unless this edit changes the crate's dependency set.
- **Supervision (Python).** `src/gobby/terminals/host_manager.py`: spawns `resolve_native_bin("gterm")` with `start_new_session=True` (the detached-child pattern from `servers/routes/admin/_lifecycle.py:130`), health-checks via a control `ping` (which returns `host_epoch`, version, and `host_pid`), restarts on crash with backoff, and on epoch change marks `native` terminals of the old epoch `orphaned` and their runs `interrupted` (2.1 CAS) so Gobby's normal resume flow applies — live PTYs are never reconstructed (C1). Constructed at the runner composition root; startup health failure degrades native availability without failing the daemon (mirroring `_check_tmux_health`).

  **Construction-failure rollback includes the supervisor.** `rollback_runner_resources` (`src/gobby/runner_rollback.py`) is the fixed inventory invoked when runner construction fails after a resource is acquired. This leaf adds the host supervisor to that inventory: stop the supervisor task and close any control client it opened. A **newly spawned** gterm (this construction created the process) is drained with `host_shutdown` on the still-open authenticated control path using the configured grace, then reaped — a failed daemon start must not leave a stray host. An **adopted** gterm (pidfile/socket already served a live host) is left running; construction failure must not orphan live PTYs that a later daemon can adopt. 4.1 adds the native control/frame clients to the same inventory. Normal shutdown still uses the 2.4/3.1 graceful path, not this rollback helper.

  **Terminal resources settle in one ordered awaited coroutine, not independent `settle()` tasks.** On a running loop the current helper schedules each async close independently and returns; that races client closure against a drain that still needs the authenticated control path, and a caller that then raises `SystemExit` or calls `sys.exit` can cancel the unfinished drain. The core is therefore one async function — `rollback_runner_resources_async` — that: (1) stop reconnect and health producers, (2) drain and reap a newly spawned gterm through the still-open authenticated control path via `host_shutdown` while leaving an adopted host running, (3) close frame and control clients, (4) await every rollback task. `GobbyRunner.create` and both `run_daemon` failure branches (`src/gobby/runner_lifecycle.py`) **await** that core **before** `cleanup_owned_pid_file`, `SystemExit`, or `sys.exit`. `GobbyRunner.__init__` keeps a separate no-running-loop wrapper (`rollback_runner_resources`) that drives the same core with `asyncio.run`. Independent settle of clients-before-drain, and any running-loop caller that schedules the helper and returns, are forbidden. 4.1 adds the client-close steps to this same coroutine.
- **Adopt before spawn.** Host lifetime is decoupled from daemon lifetime, so daemon startup **adopts** rather than replaces: the manager first dials the existing `gterm-control.sock` and authenticates with the persisted control token (which is why that token is a file rather than a per-spawn secret: the daemon that adopts is usually not the daemon that minted it), issues `ping`, then validates the protocol version against its own floor, the **`host_pid` field on that ping response** against `~/.gobby/gterm.pid` (and that the PID is live and is a `gterm` process, guarding PID reuse), and records the returned `host_epoch`. A socket that answers without `host_pid`, or whose `host_pid` does not match the pidfile, is a PID-identity failure. `hello` stays `{host_epoch, version, protocol_version}` and is not the PID source. A host passing every check is adopted with its epoch and terminals intact and no orphaning; a replacement is spawned only when no socket answers, the PID identity fails, or the protocol version is incompatible (in which case the stale host is drained first, then its terminals orphaned by the existing epoch-change path). A planned daemon restart (`gobby restart`, the reload path in `servers/routes/admin/_lifecycle.py`) leaves the adopted host running; only an explicit full stop (`gobby stop`) drains it with `host_shutdown`, terminating PTYs with the configured grace. This is what makes the 5.1 and E1 daemon-restart continuity requirements reachable — without it a straightforward manager would replace the host on every restart, change the epoch, and orphan every native terminal.

**Host crash still orphans rows; the process tree must also die.** A `gterm` crash marks native terminals `orphaned` and their runs `interrupted` (settled). It does **not** reconstruct PTYs. Walking the dead host PID with `pgrep -P` or `/proc/<pid>/task/.../children` is not enough: once `gterm` is gone, children are reparented and that walk is empty.

**Process identity is durable before the child can survive the host.** `setpgid` happens in the child after fork, but a spawn response that carries `{pgid, start_time}` can still be lost if `gterm` dies before it is written. The child therefore **cannot exec until the daemon has persisted that pair on the still-`pending` row** (2.1 allows `process` on pending; it is not identity). Native `spawn` is a prepare/commit barrier:

1. Host forks, the child `setpgid(0, 0)` and then **blocks** on a pipe (or equivalent close-on-exec barrier). It does not exec.
2. Host sends `spawn_prepared{terminal_id, spawn_key, pgid, start_time, host_terminal_id}` on the control connection. The `host_terminal_id` is attachable immediately: the PTY exists, the child has not exec'd.
3. Daemon `record_process` on the pending row.
4. Native launches insert the 4.2 internal-observer bind here (persist succeeded, child still blocked). This leaf's host-manager tests use a fake control client and do not open a frame attachment.
5. Daemon sends `spawn_commit`.
6. Host closes the barrier; the child execs; host returns the existing spawn result (`host_terminal_id`).
7. If the host dies at any point before commit, the pipe closes and the child exits without exec. If the host dies after commit and before the spawn result, the pending row already has `process` and crash cleanup can `killpg`.

**Daemon or control-connection loss after `spawn_prepared` must also converge, and replay must be reachable.** Host death closes the pipe; daemon death does not. Every prepared spawn carries a **connection owner** (the control connection that sent `spawn`) and a **host-side commit deadline** (`commit_deadline_ms` on the spawn request, equal to `TerminalConfig.spawn_in_doubt_seconds` in milliseconds). Control disconnect before `spawn_commit` does **not** reap: the host keeps the prepared entry and the blocked child until `commit_deadline_ms`, clears the live owner, keeps the prepared-owned reservation charged (`list.observer_bind: reserved`, not `none`), and reports `commit_state: "prepared"` on `list`. The next control connection authenticated with `gterm-control.token` **atomically claims** that owner slot. Replay is not a bare `spawn_commit`: the claimer first idempotently `record_process`es `{pgid, start_time}` from the prepared `list` row. Native launches then bind or reuse the internal observer (4.2) before this leaf's host manager is allowed to send `spawn_commit` (idempotent against `terminal_id`/`spawn_key`). Deadline expiry — with or without a live owner — closes the barrier, reaps the unexecuted child/process group, and removes the prepared registry entry. A live-control abort uses `kill` on the prepared `host_terminal_id` (3.2) rather than waiting out the deadline. Adoption and reconnect reconciliation promote `pending → live` only for a **committed** host terminal; a prepared-only entry is not "host has the terminal" and is never promoted. If the host already expired the entry, the row follows the existing pending-miss rule. A full stop (`preserve_agents` false) aborts remaining prepared entries instead of waiting out the deadline.

On observed host death (socket gone, pid dead, or epoch replacement) the daemon `killpg(pgid, SIGTERM)` then `SIGKILL` after `shutdown_grace_seconds` for every native row whose `process` is set — pending included — skipping the group when the live process at that pgid has a different start time (PID reuse). The same `killpg` runs when a replacement host is spawned. Descendants that call `setsid`/`setpgid` to leave the group are an accepted residual and are logged, not reconstructed. Tests start a SIGHUP-ignoring leader plus grandchild, **wait until `gterm` has fully exited and the children have been reparented**, then assert both members of the recorded group are gone within the grace plus one second. A second test kills the host after `setpgid` and before `spawn_prepared` / `spawn_commit` / spawn-result and asserts the child is gone and no unreaped process group remains. A third test drops the daemon/control connection after `spawn_prepared` and before `spawn_commit` and asserts the child is still blocked (not exec'd), the prepared registry entry remains with no live owner, `observer_bind` stays `reserved`, and the pending row is not promoted to `live`; reconnecting before the deadline claims the owner, persists `{pgid, start_time}` if missing, attaches that same reservation, and only then may commit. Observer reuse-or-rebind before that commit is 4.2.9 / 4.1.10. A fourth test lets `commit_deadline_ms` expire after the same drop and asserts the unexecuted child is gone, the prepared entry is gone, and the pending row is not promoted.
- **Surviving the daemon's own child reaper.** Adoption is unreachable unless shutdown stops killing the host. `_preserved_agent_terminal_pids` lives in `src/gobby/runner_lifecycle_processes.py` (not `runner_lifecycle_shutdown.py`) and currently builds the survive-restart PID set from *agent runs that have a `tmux_session_name`*. `_run_async_shutdown_cleanup` in `runner_lifecycle_shutdown.py` hands that set to `reap_remaining_child_processes` under `ShutdownIntent.preserve_agents`. A `gterm` host is not an agent run and carries no tmux name, so it is reaped on every restart today. This deliverable makes the preserved set terminal-backed: for `preserve_agents` shutdowns it includes the host PID from `~/.gobby/gterm.pid` (identity-checked as in the adoption bullet) plus its PTY children, and the migrated tmux branch selects on `pending|live` terminal rows instead of the removed field (the reader 2.4 deliberately left here). A full stop (`preserve_agents` false) sends `host_shutdown` with the configured grace and then lets the reaper proceed, so `gobby stop` leaves no stray PTYs.
- **Adoption reconciliation matrix.** Verifying host identity is not enough: the daemon can die between the host-side effect and the database commit, so adoption reconciles both directions. The host's `list` response returns the daemon-supplied `terminal_id` and `spawn_key` (the host keeps both as opaque metadata from `spawn`), which makes the join possible at all. A `list` row is a match only when both equal the durable row. Every cell is defined: DB `pending` + host has a **committed** terminal → CAS to `live` with the reported locator and epoch (this is the crash-after-spawn-before-CAS case that would otherwise leave a live PTY behind a pending row); DB `pending` + host has only a **prepared** entry → not a promotion: if a control connection has claimed the owner, persist process and leave commit to the 4.1/4.2 replay path; if the host already expired it, treat as a miss; DB `pending` + host does not, **and the row is past the 2.3 in-doubt deadline** → CAS to `exited` on the observed `attempt_generation` and reap by `spawn_key`; DB `pending` + host does not but the row is *inside* the deadline → left `pending` and revisited on the next sweep, because a spawn issued moments before the disconnect may still be executing and its terminal may not yet appear in `list` (exiting the row here is what would make the eventual successful spawn lose its CAS and get killed, turning a recoverable connection loss into a failed launch); DB `live` + host has it → adopt unchanged; DB `live` + host does not, same epoch → CAS to `exited` and run the normal finalization; any DB row from a different epoch → `orphaned` with its run `interrupted`; and a host terminal whose `terminal_id`/`spawn_key` is unknown or absent from the database → killed after a bounded grace and logged, because no owner can ever claim it. The destructive unknown-terminal branch is ordered, not a single snapshot pair: the sweep **lists the host first**, then performs a fresh `READ COMMITTED` lookup for **each** listed `terminal_id`/`spawn_key`, and repeats that exact lookup **immediately before** `kill` after grace. If a `pending` or `live` row appears in either lookup, cleanup is skipped. Listing the database first and the host second is forbidden: a `create_pending` that commits between those reads, followed by a `prepare_spawn` that appears on `list`, would otherwise classify a legitimate in-flight terminal as host-only. A database read failure makes the inventory indeterminate, kills nothing, records the typed failure, and retries the entire sweep after backoff. Reconciliation runs on adoption and on every reconnect of the control client (4.1), not only at startup, and it never runs concurrently with a retry of the same `terminal_id`: the spawn caller holds the row and the reconciler skips in-doubt rows, so the two cannot both decide the outcome.
- **Operator visibility.** `TerminalHostConfig`-derived host state is reported read-only through the existing daemon health/status surface (`src/gobby/servers/routes/admin/_health.py`, alongside the degraded-services list that `_get_degraded_services` builds): enabled/running, adopted-or-spawned, `host_epoch`, protocol version, restart count and current backoff, live and `orphaned` terminal counts, and the last startup or health-check error. `gclient` (3.5) surfaces the same diagnostic when the selected or default backend needs the host, so "native launches are failing" is diagnosable without reading `~/.gobby/logs/gterm.log`. This surface never mutates host state.
- **Config.** `src/gobby/config/terminal_host.py`: `TerminalHostConfig` (`enabled`, `socket_dir` default `~/.gobby`, `binary_path` override, `health_interval_seconds`, `shutdown_grace_seconds`), wired into `DaemonConfig` (`app.py`) beside `TmuxConfig`; 3.2 adds the keys its own compile-time, memory, and list-envelope close gates consume (`max_attachments_per_terminal`, `max_attachments_total`, `native_scrollback_max_lines`, `native_scrollback_max_bytes`, `max_attached_terminals`, `tmux_attach_history_lines`, `tmux_attach_history_max_bytes`) and 3.4 adds only the poll-timer keys its loop consumes (`tmux_poll_interval_ms`, `tmux_poll_backoff_ceiling_ms`) to the same model. It holds only host-specific settings: the two keys P2 consumers need — `spawn_in_doubt_seconds` and `default_backend` — belong to `TerminalConfig` (2.2), because a config owner cannot sit downstream of its own consumers, and this deliverable *reads* the in-doubt deadline from there rather than redefining it. The host manager is declared and propagated through the same carriers 2.2 established (`runner.py`, `app_context.py`, `runner_init/servers.py`, and `WebSocketServer.configure_terminals` where a WS surface needs it), so health, spawn, and `gclient` read one instance. This leaf therefore depends on 2.4: both rewrite `runner_init/orchestration.py` and `runner_init/servers.py`, 2.4 extracts the terminal-effect drain into `runner_lifecycle_terminal_effects.py` so this leaf's later edits to `runner_lifecycle_shutdown.py` stay below 1,000 lines, and expansion must not run them concurrently.

**Acceptance:**

- 3.1.1 - `gterm host` starts, binds both sockets at 0600, reaps a stale socket file, and after a valid `hello` answers `ping` with `host_epoch`, version, and `host_pid` (the serving process) and `list` with an empty inventory, and accepts `host_shutdown`. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/host_lifecycle.rs::host_starts_and_serves_ping_and_list`.
- 3.1.2 - The host manager spawns, health-checks, and restarts the host; an epoch change orphans old-epoch terminals and interrupts their runs via CAS. test: `tests/terminals/test_host_manager.py::test_crash_orphans_and_interrupts`.
- 3.1.3 - `TerminalHostConfig` is wired into `DaemonConfig` with the documented host-specific defaults and defines no shared key; the in-doubt deadline it enforces is read from `TerminalConfig` (2.2). file: `src/gobby/config/terminal_host.py`.
- 3.1.4 - Daemon startup with the host unavailable degrades native availability without failing startup. test: `tests/terminals/test_host_manager.py::test_degraded_startup_without_host`.
- 3.1.5 - Daemon restart adopts the running host: `host_epoch`, the PTY child PID, and the `terminals` row are unchanged across the restart, and no terminal is orphaned. Resumed frame-stream verification after adoption is 5.1.2. test: `tests/terminals/test_host_manager.py::test_restart_adopts_host_preserving_epoch_pid_and_row`.
- 3.1.6 - Adoption refuses and replaces on PID-identity mismatch, dead PID, or incompatible protocol version, draining the stale host before orphaning its terminals; an explicit full stop drains the host while a planned restart does not. test: `tests/terminals/test_host_manager.py::test_adoption_rejects_mismatch_and_stop_drains`.
- 3.1.17 - Adoption compares `ping.host_pid` to `~/.gobby/gterm.pid` and to a live `gterm` process: a stale pidfile, an unrelated live gterm PID, a socket served by another process, and a recycled PID each refuse adoption and replace; a matching live pidfile plus matching `host_pid` adopts. test: `tests/terminals/test_host_manager.py::test_adoption_requires_ping_host_pid_proof`.
- 3.1.18 - Unknown-host cleanup is race-closed: host `list` is read first; each listed identity is looked up with a fresh `READ COMMITTED` query; the same lookup runs again immediately before `kill` after grace. A `create_pending` that commits between the first lookup and grace, and a `prepare_spawn` that appears on `list` after the first lookup, each skip cleanup because a `pending` or `live` row is present. Inverting the list/inventory order is not a permitted implementation. test: `tests/terminals/test_host_manager.py::test_unknown_host_rechecks_before_kill`.
- 3.1.19 - Construction-failure rollback stops the host supervisor: after a later init failure, a newly spawned gterm is drained and gone; an adopted gterm is still running with the same epoch; `rollback_runner_resources` is the call site. test: `tests/test_runner_rollback.py::test_host_supervisor_rollback_drains_spawned_preserves_adopted`.
- 3.1.20 - One later-init-failure rollback with supervisor and native clients fully acquired is ordered: spawned gterm is drained through a still-open control path then clients close; adopted gterm stays up with the same epoch and clients still close; reconnect/health producers are stopped first; independent settle of clients-before-drain is a failing contrast. test: `tests/test_runner_rollback.py::test_terminal_rollback_orders_drain_before_client_close`.
- 3.1.21 - Every running-loop caller awaits the ordered rollback core before propagating failure or terminating the process: `GobbyRunner.create` and both `run_daemon` failure branches wait for drain, client close, and task settlement against a blocked-drain sentinel; `__init__` uses the no-loop wrapper that drives the same core with `asyncio.run`; PID cleanup and `SystemExit` / `sys.exit` occur only after settlement. test: `tests/test_runner_rollback.py::test_terminal_rollback_awaited_before_exit`.
- 3.1.22 - Authenticated `host_shutdown{grace_ms}` is accepted typed, is idempotent while draining, rejects new spawn/attach as `host_draining`, reaps prepared and committed groups, closes sockets, and exits; a lost response plus verified host death is success; an unauthenticated call is refused like any other verb. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/host_lifecycle.rs::host_shutdown_drains_and_is_idempotent`.
- 3.1.7 - The daemon health surface reports host enabled/running state, epoch, protocol version, restart count/backoff, live and orphaned terminal counts, and last error, including in the degraded-host case. test: `tests/servers/test_admin_health.py::test_health_reports_gterm_host_state`.
- 3.1.8 - A `preserve_agents` shutdown keeps the host PID and its PTY children out of the reaped set so the next daemon adopts the same host; a full stop sends `host_shutdown` first and leaves no surviving PTY; and the migrated tmux branch selects live terminals from rows rather than the removed field. test: `tests/terminals/test_host_shutdown_preservation.py::test_restart_preserves_host_stop_drains_it`.
- 3.1.9 - Every adoption-matrix cell is exercised, including a daemon crash after host spawn but before the pending-to-live CAS (row converges to `live`, PTY retained), a live row missing from the host list at the same epoch (finalized as exited), an old-epoch row (orphaned with its run interrupted), and a host terminal unknown after a successful database inventory read (killed after grace, logged); an injected database inventory failure leaves that same terminal alive, records the failure, and retries. test: `tests/terminals/test_host_manager.py::test_adoption_reconciliation_matrix`.
- 3.1.10 - A control request other than `hello` on a fresh connection is refused `unauthenticated` and the connection closed; a bad token and an unsupported protocol version are each refused typed before any terminal state is read; a `hello` presenting the frame channel's `local_cli_token` as its control credential is refused with the same typed error; and a valid `hello` with the control token admits `ping`, `list`, and `host_shutdown`. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::hello_required_before_any_verb`.
- 3.1.12 - The control token is minted, scoped, and rotated as specified: the daemon writes `~/.gobby/gterm-control.token` 0600 before the first host spawn, the host authenticates with it across a daemon restart that adopts the same host, replacing a host rather than adopting it rotates the file, and no client-facing surface (`gclient`, any WS payload) ever emits its value. test: `tests/terminals/test_host_manager.py::test_control_token_is_minted_scoped_and_rotated`.
- 3.1.11 - A reconnect that lists before an in-flight spawn becomes visible leaves the young pending row alone; the spawn then completes, wins its CAS, and the terminal ends `live` with no kill issued. Past the in-doubt deadline the same shape reaps by `spawn_key` and the observed `attempt_generation` and exits the row; a generation that moved under the reconciler is a lost CAS. test: `tests/terminals/test_host_manager.py::test_reconcile_does_not_exit_inflight_spawn`.
- 3.1.13 - After a host crash, native rows are `orphaned` and runs `interrupted` as settled, **and** a SIGHUP-ignoring process leader plus its grandchild in the recorded `pgid` are gone after SIGTERM then SIGKILL within `shutdown_grace_seconds` + 1s even when the test waits until `gterm` has exited and the children have been reparented; a recycled pid with a different start time is not signalled; no PTY is reconstructed. test: `tests/terminals/test_host_manager.py::test_host_crash_reaps_sighup_ignoring_tree`.
- 3.1.14 - Against a fake control client, `host_manager` on `spawn_prepared` calls `record_process` on the still-`pending` row before any `spawn_commit`; a `record_process` failure sends no commit; a control drop after `spawn_prepared` does not CAS the row to `live`. Native observer bind-before-commit is 4.2.9. Wire verbs are 3.2; process-tree death windows are 4.1.10. test: `tests/terminals/test_host_manager.py::test_prepare_commit_persists_before_commit`.
- 3.1.15 - Adoption treats a prepared-only host entry as not-yet-live: it does not promote `pending → live`. After control loss the prepared entry stays claimable until `commit_deadline_ms`; a new authenticated control connection claims the owner and persists `{pgid, start_time}` before any commit. Observer bind-before-replay is 4.2.9. Host-side expiry follows the pending-miss rule. test: `tests/terminals/test_host_manager.py::test_reconcile_does_not_promote_uncommitted_prepare`.
- 3.1.16 - `crates/gterminal/Cargo.toml` declares `[[bin]] name = "gterm"` with `required-features = ["vt-engine"]`. `cargo build -p gobby-terminal` with default features does not compile `src/bin/gterm.rs`; `cargo build -p gobby-terminal --features vt-engine --bin gterm` does. file: `crates/gterminal/Cargo.toml`.

### 3.2 Gobby frame and control protocols on the host [category: code] (depends: 3.1)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:

- `crates/gterminal/src/host/frames.rs`
