- **Frame protocol (bincode, imported and narrowed to read-only).** The rebranded herdr wire protocol at version 1: `Hello{version, encoding, local_token, cols, rows}` → `Welcome{host_epoch}`; `AttachTerminal{host_terminal_id}`; `SetViewport{rows, cols}`; `SetScrollOffset{rows_from_live_edge}`; `Detach`; server → `Frame(FrameData)` | `Terminal(TerminalFrame)` | `Graphics` | `AttachHistory` | `ScrollOffsetApplied{applied_rows, max_rows}` | `TerminalExited`. Per-terminal attach only (herdr's whole-app frame mode is not exposed). The `local_token` in `Hello` is verified against `~/.gobby/local_cli_token` (same-user defense in depth; C1). `AttachHistory{text, truncated, dropped_bytes, total_bytes}` is a standalone first-attach scrollback frame (3.4), bounded independently by `MAX_FRAME_SIZE`, and is never packed into a keyframe.

  The narrowing is the point: herdr's `Input`, `InputEvents`, `Resize`, and the `mode`/`takeover` fields of `AttachTerminal` are **not** imported. This channel has no verb that mutates a terminal. `SetViewport` is the one thing that looks like a write and is not — it changes only the grid size this attachment's frames are rendered into, so observers each read at their own size for the focused encoding, and it can never reach `TIOCSWINSZ` (that path exists only on the control channel). `SetScrollOffset` is the matching read-only scroll verb: `rows_from_live_edge` is a non-negative integer, `0` is the live edge, and the host clamps to the current scrollback max and answers `ScrollOffsetApplied` before the next keyframe from that offset. It never reaches PTY input, mouse-report, or `TIOCSWINSZ`. Two attachments of one native terminal hold independent offsets. This is what eliminates tmux's smallest-client shrinking: a viewer's geometry and scroll position are rendering parameters, not properties of the terminal.

- **Two surfaces, and only one of them can write.** The mechanics above are per-connection; the rule binding them is pinned here once so gclient (3.3) and the web proxy (4.3) cannot disagree. The host serves exactly two sockets: the **frame socket**, which local clients connect to and which is read-only as just described, and the **control socket**, which only the daemon connects to and which carries every state-changing verb the host implements (all of them against host-owned PTYs; a tmux pane is written by the daemon itself, never through the host — 3.4). There is no path by which a local client writes to a terminal, resizes one, or acquires anything resembling a lease from the host. The two sockets also accept different credentials — frame clients present `local_cli_token`, the control socket accepts only `gterm-control.token` (3.1, C1) — so a client cannot reach the writing surface by reusing the credential it already needs for the daemon. The read-only property does not depend on that, since the frame enum contains no write verb at all, but the two facts fail independently rather than together.

  This is a deliberate reversal of an earlier design in which the host admitted a `Control` attachment on presentation of a daemon-minted lease token. That design had one authority in name and two in fact, because a token is a replica of the daemon's lease record and a replica demands a consistency protocol — mint, bind, expire, revoke-on-takeover, reconcile-after-restart — every part of which would be new mechanism whose only purpose was to let a client skip a hop. Removing the client write path removes all of it. The host holds no authority state, makes no admission decision, and needs no clock agreement with the daemon; it serializes the writes the daemon sends it because two writes to one PTY would interleave bytes, and that is the whole of its involvement.

  The lease itself lives where 2.5 puts it: a daemon-held record keyed by `terminal_id`. gclient asks the daemon for control, sends keystrokes to the daemon, and renders read-only until the daemon says otherwise; a displaced holder learns of it from the daemon's `terminal_lease_lost` event and must render a visible read-only state (silent input loss is a defect, not a degradation). If the daemon is unreachable, frames keep arriving from the host and nobody can write — observe-only is the failure mode by construction rather than by policy. The cost is one extra hop for local keystrokes, on exactly the path the shipped web terminal already uses.
- **Backpressure and slow consumers.** Frame production must never be gated on the slowest reader, and every hop is bounded — a reader that never reads cannot be promised delivery *and* bounded memory, so the contract is bounded-effort delivery with a typed failure instead of an absolute guarantee. Each **attachment** (not each terminal) gets two bounded queues. The **delta queue** is droppable: 64 entries **and** 2 MiB serialized bytes; on either overflow the host discards that attachment's queued frame deltas, marks it desynchronized, and schedules a fresh full keyframe, so a stalled reader costs itself latency rather than costing the writer and healthy observers throughput. The **control queue** is 16 entries **and** 64 KiB, non-coalescing, carrying `TerminalExited` and typed errors; these are never dropped *silently*, but they are not delivered unconditionally either — if the control queue overflows or a control event exceeds a **2s** delivery deadline, the host closes that attachment. A permanently blocked peer may never read the typed lag error: emit it best-effort, then EOF/close. The **delta-lag timeout is 5s**. Serialized frame and control-line max is herdr's `MAX_FRAME_SIZE` **2 MiB**; `write`/`paste` stay at **1 MiB** raw UTF-8, encoded on the wire as base64 with `encoding: "utf8-b64"` so worst-case JSON escaping cannot expand a legal payload past the line cap. Every control request is preflighted as a complete encoded JSON line before any socket write; an encoded line at or above 2 MiB is refused `request_too_large` without closing the connection. Spawn `argv`/`env`/`cwd` use the same preflight. Snapshot `max_bytes` defaults to **256 KiB**, `max_lines` defaults to **500**, both clamped before allocation. The daemon→browser relay (4.3) uses the same 2 MiB / 64-entry / 5s lag numbers. Caller inputs are rejected typed before any grid or buffer allocation.

  Memory is allocated per attachment **and** per distinct tmux observer. This leaf owns the shared wire enums and numeric constants its own close gates use. `observation_state` is `live` | `stale` | `orphaned_observation`. `observation_reason` is the closed vocabulary `poll_spawn_failed` | `poll_timeout` | `poll_permission` | `poll_fd_exhausted` | `poll_unparseable` | `geometry_exceeds_max_cells` | `observation_ceiling`, or JSON `null` when `live`. `TerminalHostConfig` owns `max_attachments_per_terminal` (default **8**, floor **1**, ceiling **8**), `max_attachments_total` (default **128**, floor **4**, ceiling **128** — floor is the four host-health/`list`/lifecycle slots so `N - 4` is never negative), `native_scrollback_max_lines` (default **10000**, floor **500**, ceiling **50000**), `native_scrollback_max_bytes` (default **8 MiB**, floor **256 KiB**, ceiling **32 MiB**), `max_attached_terminals` (default **64**, floor **1**, ceiling **64**), `tmux_attach_history_lines` (default **500**, floor **1**, ceiling **2000**), and `tmux_attach_history_max_bytes` (default **256 KiB**, floor **1 KiB**, ceiling **256 KiB**). A value outside its range is a typed startup error on both the Python `TerminalHostConfig` and the host; neither process starts with an unvalidated multiplicand. The 3.4 attach-history cache is a second independent allocation of up to `tmux_attach_history_max_bytes` for each of `max_attached_terminals` distinct tmux observers. 3.4 maps poll outcomes onto this vocabulary and reruns the shared bounds tests with real tmux observers; it does not redefine the enums or those three constants. Internal daemon observers are a separate class from user attachments: **one output observer per admitted live native terminal** (4.2) plus a fixed reserve of **four** slots for host-health/`list`/lifecycle. Those internal slots scale with live native terminals; they are taken from `max_attachments_total` first by refusing a new user attach. The host does **not** preempt existing user attachments: every user frame connection is read-only and omits `reservation_id`, including the current daemon lease holder's direct `gclient` frames, so the host cannot tell a controller from an observe-only viewer. `reserve_observer` under saturation fails typed and leaves every existing attachment in place. Reservation is a **control-protocol operation**, not daemon-side arithmetic and not a host-side lease token: `reserve_observer{terminal_id, reserve_key}` on the authenticated control socket (the daemon's `gterm-control.token`, never `local_cli_token`) atomically holds the slot and returns `{reservation_id}`. `reserve_key` is the daemon's per-attempt fingerprint. A lost `reserve_observer` response replayed on the **same** control connection with the same `terminal_id`/`reserve_key` returns the same `reservation_id` and does not allocate a second slot. The reservation state machine is `reserved(control connection, terminal_id, reserve_key)` → `reserved(prepared registry entry)` no later than successful `spawn_prepared` → `bound(frame connection)` → then either `released` or `entitled`. Successful `spawn_prepared` atomically transfers ownership from the control connection to the prepared registry entry, keyed by `terminal_id`, `spawn_key`, `reserve_key`, and reservation generation. Control `spawn` names that reservation with `reservation_id` and `reserve_key`; the host validates them against the live reservation for that `terminal_id` and the stored generation **before fork**, then transfers that exact reservation. A missing, mismatched, or stale-generation identity is a typed refusal with no PTY. A second live reservation for the same `terminal_id` under a different `reserve_key` is not transferred and stays connection-owned until `release_observer` or control disconnect. Replay of a matching spawn fingerprint returns the same `spawn_prepared` plus the canonical reservation identity. `AttachTerminal` carrying that `reservation_id` then transfers ownership from the prepared entry to the frame connection. `release_observer{reservation_id, reserve_key}` is the authenticated, idempotent live-control cleanup: a repeated or stale-generation release is a no-op success, and it does not require tearing down the control connection. Control disconnect releases **only reservations that have no prepared registry entry**. A prepared-owned reservation stays charged through persist, bind, reconnect, commit, prepared `kill`, or `commit_deadline_ms` expiry. After `spawn_prepared`, `list.observer_bind` is `reserved` (not `none`) even when the control connection is gone; `none` means reserve never succeeded or the prepared entry was killed or expired. On frame detach, frame disconnect, or typed frame refusal a **bound** observer — prepared, uncommitted, or committed live native — transitions `bound → entitled` (zero queues, slot retained), keyed by `terminal_id`, `spawn_key`, and reservation generation. The reconnecting daemon rebinds that entitlement (`entitled` → `bound`) before `spawn_commit` for a prepared entry, and converts it to the committed entitlement on commit. The entitlement is released only on explicit prepared `kill`, terminal exit, or `commit_deadline_ms` expiry. An explicit live-control `release_observer` still drops a reservation that has no prepared entry and a committed entitlement the operator asked to drop; it is not the frame-loss path and it cannot drop a prepared-owned reservation while the prepared entry is still live. Every cleanup path is generation-guarded so a stale disconnect or stale `release_observer` cannot release or entitle a newer reservation of the same `terminal_id`. A native spawn that cannot reserve is refused typed **before** fork / `runtime.prepare_spawn`. Prepared plus committed native observer entitlements are capped at `max_attachments_total - 4` so the four host-health/`list`/lifecycle slots remain usable at native saturation. Every prepared registry entry counts toward `list` cardinality and the peak-memory / 2 MiB envelope proofs; it is not a free extra process outside that native-entitlement ceiling. A user attach omits `reservation_id` and cannot consume an internal slot. A user attach that would exceed the per-terminal cap or the remaining user total is refused typed. Peak host memory is therefore `max_attachments_total × (2 MiB delta + 64 KiB control) + max_attached_terminals × tmux_attach_history_max_bytes + (max_attachments_total - 4) × native_scrollback_max_bytes` and is preserved because internal observers take reserved slots rather than adding a second unbounded pool, because each history cache is freed when its observer generation is reaped, and because each admitted live native terminal's emulator scrollback is oldest-first evicted at the line and byte ceilings below.
- **Dimension bounds (protocol source of truth).** Type and codec work live in `wire_types.rs` and `wire_codec.rs`; `wire.rs` remains the re-export facade. This leaf (and 3.4) re-runs `crates/gterminal/tests/source_size.rs` so every touched Rust source stays below 1,000 lines. Bounds are pinned after measuring **every negotiated host encoding**: `WORST_CELL_BYTES` is the maximum of the serialized size of the most expensive admitted cell under `FrameData` bincode **and** under `TerminalAnsi` (widest grapheme plus full attributes, including alternating-attribute runs). `MAX_CELLS = floor((MAX_FRAME_SIZE - FRAME_HEADER) / WORST_CELL_BYTES)`. Maximum rows and columns are then chosen so `max_rows * max_cols <= MAX_CELLS` (the previous 1024×2048 / 1,000,000 product is lowered if that product cannot encode as one 2 MiB keyframe in the worse encoding). `Hello` (cols/rows), `SetViewport`, and control `spawn`/`resize` are validated against them **before** any grid allocation, snapshot buffer, or `TIOCSWINSZ`, and violations return a typed error. `AttachTerminal` carries no dimensions after the read-only narrowing and is **not** a bounds ingress. Polled tmux geometry is a 3.4 ingress against the same integers. The frame-channel verb in that list is `SetViewport`, never a frame `Resize`: 3.2's narrowing removes `Resize` from the frame enum entirely, so naming it here as a validation site would reintroduce on paper the verb the protocol rejects on the wire. The Python mirror is owned by 2.2; the numbers are documented once here so the two implementations cannot drift silently. Delta overflow still recovers with a fresh keyframe of the *current* admitted grid; that keyframe is proven ≤ 2 MiB **in every negotiated encoding** by construction. The daemon→browser JSON hop is a second envelope (4.3): a legal host frame may still expand under JSON escaping, so 4.3 fragments `terminal_output` rather than silently raising `MAX_FRAME_SIZE`.
- **Operation identity for non-idempotent verbs.** `write`, `kill`, `resize`, and `spawn` change state, and a response lost to a disconnect is indistinguishable from a request never executed — for `write` specifically, the difference between "nothing typed", "payload typed without Enter", and "payload plus Enter" is the difference between a correct answer and a corrupted agent prompt. Deduplication therefore needs an identity the host can *reason about when it is absent*, which an opaque id cannot provide: with a bounded map of arbitrary ids, a never-seen id and an already-executed-but-evicted id are the same cache miss, so the host would have to either re-execute evicted retries or reject all new work. The identity is instead a **per-connection monotonic `operation_seq`**, assigned by the client, carried on every state-changing request, and paired with a request fingerprint (verb plus payload hash) so a mismatched replay is caught rather than silently confirmed. State-changing requests are serialized per connection and the client issues at most one at a time. The host keeps a `high_seq` watermark, an `evicted_below` watermark, and a bounded map (last 64) of `operation_seq → outcome`. The decision is total under concurrent or out-of-order arrival: `seq == high_seq + 1` is the only new request that executes; `seq > high_seq + 1` is refused `operation_gap` without advancing the watermark, so the missing predecessor may arrive and the refused request may be retried; a retained sequence returns its recorded outcome without re-executing and refuses typed if its fingerprint differs; a sequence at or below the eviction watermark is refused `operation_expired`. The per-connection serialization covers pipelined decoder tasks as well as ordinary client calls, so two handlers cannot both observe the same watermark and execute out of order. Because the ledger is keyed on the connection rather than the terminal, a terminal that exits does not erase the outcome of the `kill` or `spawn` that concerned it, and the daemon can still resolve an in-flight verb after the fact. A reconnect starts a fresh connection with a fresh sequence space, so the ledger's job is exactly-once *within* a connection and it says nothing at all about a retry that crosses a reconnect.

**What the boundary can and cannot promise, stated rather than implied.** A per-connection ledger plus "reconciliation covers the boundary" is only true for verbs whose effect is observable in terminal state, and it is worth being blunt about which ones those are, because the difference decides whether a caller may retry. `spawn` is resolvable: the daemon holds the `terminal_id` and `spawn_key`, and `list` says whether the terminal exists, so the reconciliation matrix (3.1) converges the row without a second spawn. `kill` and `resize` are resolvable and idempotent-by-target: killing a dead terminal and resizing to the size it already has are both no-ops, so a blind retry after reconnect is safe. `write` is **not** resolvable, and no mechanism short of a durable cross-connection ledger would make it so — the bytes are gone into a PTY and nothing in `list` or a snapshot can distinguish "the payload was typed and the program consumed it" from "the payload never arrived" in the general case. So this plan does not pretend otherwise: a `write` whose response is lost across a reconnect is **indeterminate**, the control client must not blind-retry it, and the caller's recovery is to re-read a snapshot and decide from observed terminal state. Declining to promise cross-reconnect exactly-once for `write` is a deliberate choice over building a persistent operation log: a durable ledger keyed by client identity would be real mechanism — storage, retention, cross-restart identity — bought to paper over a case a recapture-and-re-evaluate loop handles correctly.

Because `write` is a control verb, the only host client that can ever see this outcome is the daemon, which is what makes it tractable: it surfaces as the `IndeterminateWrite` member of the backend-neutral `WriteOutcome` 2.2 defines, and every consumer above it follows the single abstain-and-re-observe rule 2.4 states. This channel is one of **two** producers, not the only one — a tmux `send-keys` can also apply its effect and lose its answer (2.2) — which is why the outcome type and its latch live in the 2.2 write coordinator rather than in the native runtime. Local `gclient` and browser tabs do issue writes, but only through the daemon: 2.5 correlates each `terminal_input` / `terminal_paste` with `terminal_write_outcome` so those clients can distinguish `Delivered` from `IndeterminateWrite` and must not blind-retry.

**The host registry is unique by daemon `terminal_id` and `spawn_key`.** `operation_seq` deduplicates one connection; `attempt_generation` fences database transitions; neither stops an old connection from finishing a spawn after a retry has opened a new connection with the same durable identity. The host therefore keys its in-memory registry atomically by the daemon `terminal_id` **and** `spawn_key`. A replay whose request fingerprint (argv/env/cwd/dims) matches returns the existing host terminal and `spawn_prepared`/`spawn` outcomes without creating a second PTY. A conflicting fingerprint is refused typed (`spawn_conflict`). A `list` miss is not permission to create a duplicate. The cross-connection race — old spawn completing after a new-connection retry — is a required test.
- **Control protocol (JSON lines, new, small).** Newline-delimited JSON over `gterm-control.sock` for the daemon (Python speaks JSON, not bincode); herdr's 8.8k-line schema'd API is not imported. The socket, the `hello` handshake, the read-only `ping`/`list` verbs, and host-level `host_shutdown` are created in 3.1, which needs them for supervision, rollback, replacement, and full stop; this deliverable adds the working terminal-scoped verbs on top and pins `host_shutdown` in the golden corpus. `host_shutdown` remains a 3.1 verb: 3.2 must not invent a second host-drain operation. Requests carry `id` for correlation: `spawn{terminal_id, spawn_key, reservation_id, reserve_key, argv, env, cwd, rows, cols, commit_deadline_ms}` → `spawn_prepared` (echoing the canonical `{reservation_id, reserve_key, reserve_generation}`) then, after the daemon `spawn_commit`, `{host_terminal_id}` (3.1 barrier; `commit_deadline_ms` is the host-side prepared-entry expiry); the spawn fingerprint includes `reservation_id` and `reserve_key` alongside argv/env/cwd/dims; `kill{host_terminal_id, grace_ms}` (a prepared entry is a valid target: `kill` closes the exec barrier, reaps the process group, and removes the registry entry without waiting for deadline expiry); `resize{...}`; `snapshot{host_terminal_id, mode: "text"|"ansi", lines, max_bytes}` (bounded, see below); `write{host_terminal_id, kind: "text"|"key"|"paste", encoding: "utf8-b64", data, submit}` (the attention-respond primitive with the same literal/paste/named-key semantics as tmux injection; `data` is base64 of the raw UTF-8 bytes; `paste` applies herdr 0.8.0 `paste_payload` from the host's live `MODE_BRACKETED_PASTE`); `reserve_observer{terminal_id, reserve_key}` → `{reservation_id}` (daemon-only internal slot; replayable by `reserve_key`; not a lease); `release_observer{reservation_id, reserve_key}` (idempotent live-control release; not a lease); `subscribe_events` → the **lifecycle-only** event stream (exit, resize, title), each event carrying a per-terminal monotonic `seq` for gap detection — it is deliberately not an output tap, since continuous output belongs to the frame protocol (see 4.2). State-changing requests additionally carry `operation_seq` per the identity rule above.
- **`list` must be able to reconstruct every state the event stream carries.** `subscribe_events` is lossy by design — a subscriber can be closed for lag, and a daemon can be down — so any state that only ever arrives as an event is a state that can go permanently stale. Title is the sharp case: a title-only change during a disconnection produces no later event, so there is no `seq` gap to notice, and even a noticed gap cannot be repaired if the snapshot response omits the field. `list` therefore returns, per terminal, `{host_terminal_id, terminal_id, spawn_key, title, rows, cols, pgid, start_time, last_seq, commit_state, observer_bind, observation_state, observation_reason, observation_generation}` — every field the lifecycle stream can change, plus the highest `seq` the host has emitted for that terminal, plus whether the spawn is `prepared` or `committed` (3.1), plus the durable `{pgid, start_time}` pair used for `killpg` (not a host-process `pid`), plus the `spawn_key` that with `terminal_id` is the host-registry and daemon-resume identity (2.1), plus a generation-fenced `observer_bind` of `none` / `reserved` / `bound` / `entitled` with `reservation_id` and `reserve_generation`, plus observation health using this leaf's `observation_state` / `observation_reason` / `observation_generation` (3.4 maps poll outcomes onto those fields). `pid` is not a list field. A reconnecting daemon must be able to persist killpg identity and prove observer bind from `list` alone. Commit replay, adoption, retry settlement, and destructive cleanup require **both** `terminal_id` and `spawn_key` to equal the durable row; a `list` row missing either is not a match.

**The `list` line is one 2 MiB JSON line, so every field that can grow must be capped.** OSC titles and tmux `#{pane_title}` are unbounded at the source. The host normalizes titles to `title_max_bytes` (**1024** UTF-8, same constant as 2.1) on a code-point boundary before they enter the registry, `list`, or `subscribe_events`. `observation_reason` is this leaf's closed enum (or JSON `null` when live), never a free-form command or error string. Other list fields are fixed-width identifiers, integers, or closed enums. Host registry cardinality is at most `max_attachments_total + max_attached_terminals` (default 128 + 64). The acceptance proves `maximum_rows × worst_encoded_row + envelope < 2 MiB` with escaping-heavy 1024-byte titles **and** the longest reason-code string (`geometry_exceeds_max_cells`) on every row at that cardinality. Chunking is not added: the product fits. An oversize title is truncated, never a reason to drop the row or close the control connection.

**A bound internal observer keeps a zero-queue entitlement after its frame connection drops, including a prepared entry.** After that internal frame disconnect the host retains an `entitled` slot for that `terminal_id`/`spawn_key`/reservation generation — no queues are allocated, user attachments are not preempted, and `list` reports `observer_bind: entitled`. The reconnecting daemon rebinds that entitlement atomically (`entitled` → `bound`) before commit (prepared) or before any new user attach is admitted (committed). Commit converts the prepared entitlement into the committed one. `reserve_observer` for a new spawn still fails typed when `max_attachments_total - 4` prepared-or-committed native entitlements are already held, including when remaining user-visible slots are occupied by prepared entitlements. The four reserved lifecycle slots stay usable (`ping`, `list`, `host_shutdown`, and host-health). Prepared entries count in `list` cardinality. The entitlement is dropped only by prepared `kill`, terminal exit, or `commit_deadline_ms` expiry. `terminal_id` and `spawn_key` are echoed so adoption can reconcile in both directions (3.1); `last_seq` lets a reconnecting subscriber detect that it missed events at all; and title and dimensions let it repair what it missed without a replay buffer. Reconciliation over `list` runs on startup, on every reconnect, and on the health interval, so the worst-case staleness of any lifecycle-carried field is one interval.

  The live `subscribe_events` stream is itself bounded. Each subscriber has a 256-entry **and** 256 KiB queue. Replaceable title and resize events for the same terminal coalesce to the latest. Exit is never dropped: if an exit cannot be queued the subscriber is closed typed. Any other overflow also closes the subscriber typed. After close or reconnect the daemon recovers from `list` + `last_seq`; it does not require a replay buffer. Host memory for a lagging daemon is therefore capped by that queue, not by event rate.
- **Resident native scrollback is bounded independently of the snapshot response.** Ghostty's default ring is not a host-memory contract. `TerminalHostConfig` owns `native_scrollback_max_lines` (default **10000**, floor **500**, ceiling **50000**) and `native_scrollback_max_bytes` (default **8 MiB**, floor **256 KiB**, ceiling **32 MiB**). The host evicts from the **oldest** end when either ceiling is crossed, and the same `{truncated, dropped_bytes, total_bytes}` accounting snapshot already carries is updated for the evicted prefix. `SetScrollOffset` clamps to the *retained* history after eviction, never to a discarded offset. The worst admitted live-native count is `max_attachments_total - 4` (the four host-health/`list`/lifecycle slots), so resident emulator memory is in the peak formula above. A sustained-output test must plateau at those ceilings rather than grow for the life of the process.

**Snapshots are byte-bounded.** `snapshot` with `mode: "ansi"` returns retained scrollback, and the JSON-lines transport must reject oversized lines to stay memory-safe — a legitimate long history would otherwise either trip the guard (losing capture-before-kill exactly when it matters, at process death) or force an unbounded allocation. The response is therefore capped independently of the resident ring: `max_bytes` defaults to **256 KiB** (below the 2 MiB control-line max) and `max_lines` to **500**, both clamped before allocation; the host truncates from the **oldest** end so the most recent output always survives, and the response carries `{truncated: bool, dropped_bytes, total_bytes}` so the caller records a faithful artifact rather than a silently short one. Deliberately not chosen: a paginated cursor protocol. Capture-before-kill and detector input both want the tail, one bounded response satisfies both, and a cursor would add multi-round-trip state to a path that runs during process teardown.
- **Golden wire corpus.** Three processes in two languages share these two boundaries — the Rust host, the Rust client, and the Python daemon (which speaks JSON control *and*, for the web proxy in 4.3, consumes bincode frames). Rust-internal round-trip tests cannot catch cross-language byte drift, so `crates/gterminal/tests/fixtures/wire_golden/` holds the committed corpus. This deliverable **creates every file listed in Targets**: frame `hello`, `welcome`, `attach_terminal`, `set_viewport`, `set_scroll_offset`, `scroll_offset_applied`, `detach`, `frame`, `terminal_ansi`, `graphics`, `attach_history`, `terminal_exited`, `error_frame`; control `hello`, `ping` (includes `host_pid`), `list`, `host_shutdown`, `spawn`, `spawn_prepared`, `spawn_commit`, `kill`, `resize`, `snapshot`, `write`, `write_paste_on`, `write_paste_off`, `subscribe_events`, `reserve_observer`, `release_observer`. `Graphics` and `AttachHistory` are live server frame variants and are pinned here; a corpus that only covers `Frame`/`Terminal`/`TerminalExited` would leave declared shapes unfrozen. 3.4 regenerates only the pane-identity / mode-flag / refusal shapes named there against these same files; 4.1 and 4.3 consume those owned bytes and do not add a second corpus. Rust asserts its encoder reproduces the fixtures byte-for-byte and its decoder accepts them; the Python side asserts the same in 4.1 and 4.3. The corpus is fixtures plus focused tests — deliberately not a schema-generation subsystem or an IDL. Changing any wire shape means regenerating the corpus in the same change, which is what makes the version-1 freeze enforceable.
- Both protocols and their versioning/compat rules are documented in `docs/contracts/gterm-protocols.md` (the contract-drift precedent from `docs/contracts/gcode-cli.md`), including the read-only frame channel and daemon-only write rule, the backpressure contract, and the corpus regeneration procedure.

**Acceptance:**

- 3.2.1 - Frame protocol round-trips: attach, detach, `SetViewport`, `SetScrollOffset`/`ScrollOffsetApplied`, per-observer sizing and independent scroll offsets at two attachments off one terminal, and both encodings. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/frame_protocol.rs::attach_viewport_and_observer_sizing`.
- 3.2.2 - On top of the 3.1 authenticated connection, the control protocol serves spawn/`spawn_prepared`/`spawn_commit`/kill/resize/snapshot/write/`reserve_observer`/`release_observer`/subscribe_events over JSON lines with correlation ids; `host_shutdown` remains the 3.1 verb on the same socket. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::control_surface_round_trip`.
- 3.2.3 - A `Hello` with a wrong protocol version or invalid local token is rejected with a typed error before any terminal access. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/frame_protocol.rs::hello_rejects_bad_version_and_token`.
- 3.2.4 - Both protocols are documented with versioning rules, the read-only frame channel and daemon-only write rule, the backpressure contract, and the corpus regeneration procedure. file: `docs/contracts/gterm-protocols.md`.
- 3.2.5 - The committed golden corpus round-trips in Rust byte-for-byte for every listed frame and control message, including `Graphics`, `AttachHistory`, `SetScrollOffset`, `ScrollOffsetApplied`, `ping.host_pid`, and `host_shutdown`, and decoding survives partial-read reassembly across arbitrary chunk boundaries while oversized and version-mismatched inputs are rejected with typed errors. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/wire_golden.rs::golden_corpus_bytes_and_fragmented_reads`.
- 3.2.6 - A stalled observer that stops reading does not slow or block the lease holder or a second healthy observer; the stalled attachment is resynchronized with a full keyframe after delta drop, and is closed with a typed lag error once it stays over bound past the timeout. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/frame_protocol.rs::slow_observer_resyncs_or_lags_out`.
- 3.2.7 - The frame protocol cannot mutate a terminal: its message enum contains no input, no PTY resize, no mode, and no takeover field; a `SetViewport` changes only that attachment's render size and issues no `TIOCSWINSZ`; a `SetScrollOffset` changes only that attachment's rows-from-live-edge and issues no PTY input, mouse-report, or `TIOCSWINSZ`; and a decoder fed a hand-built legacy `Input` or `Resize` frame rejects it as unknown rather than acting on it. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/frame_protocol.rs::frame_channel_is_read_only`.
- 3.2.8 - A permanently non-reading attachment cannot grow host memory without bound: its control queue overflow or exceeded delivery deadline closes the attachment with a typed error, host memory for it is released, and the writer plus healthy observers are unaffected; a reconnecting client recovers state from `list` plus a keyframe with no replay buffer involved. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/frame_protocol.rs::control_overflow_closes_attachment_bounded`.
- 3.2.9 - Dimension bounds are enforced at `Hello`, `SetViewport`, and control `spawn`/`resize` before any allocation or `TIOCSWINSZ`, rejecting zero, negative, above-maximum, and cell-product-overflow values with typed errors. `AttachTerminal` is not a dimension ingress. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/frame_protocol.rs::dimension_bounds_rejected_before_allocation`.
- 3.2.10 - The serialized `operation_seq` ledger decides every case without guessing: exactly the next sequence executes; a gap is refused `operation_gap` without advancing the watermark and succeeds after its missing predecessor; two concurrently dispatched requests cannot both execute against one watermark; a retained sequence returns the recorded outcome without re-executing and is refused typed when its fingerprint differs; an evicted sequence is refused `operation_expired`; and a `kill` outcome remains resolvable after its terminal has exited. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::operation_seq_ledger_is_total`.
- 3.2.13 - Across a reconnect the boundary behaves as specified per verb: a `spawn` interrupted before its response converges through reconciliation with no second terminal; a `kill` and a `resize` retried on the new connection are no-ops on an already-satisfied target; and a `write` interrupted before its response is reported indeterminate rather than retried — a source scan finds no automatic cross-connection write retry, and the client surfaces the state to its caller. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::reconnect_boundary_semantics_per_verb`.
- 3.2.14 - Writes are reachable only from the daemon's socket: a frame-socket connection has no verb that reaches a PTY, a control-socket connection is the sole writer path, no lease token or expiry exists in `wire.rs` or the control schema (source scan), and killing the daemon's control connection leaves every frame client still receiving frames and unable to write. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::only_the_control_socket_can_write`.
- 3.2.11 - `list` returns title, dimensions, `last_seq`, `commit_state` (`prepared` or `committed`), `pgid`/`start_time`, `spawn_key`, generation-fenced `observer_bind`, and observation health per terminal, and a client that misses exactly one title event with no following event still converges to the correct title from `list` alone. Commit replay, adoption, retry settlement, and destructive cleanup require `terminal_id` and `spawn_key` equality. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::list_recovers_every_lifecycle_field`.
- 3.2.12 - An ANSI snapshot of a scrollback larger than `max_bytes` (256 KiB default) returns a response inside the 2 MiB control-line limit, preserves the newest output, drops from the oldest end, and reports `truncated`, `dropped_bytes`, and `total_bytes`; a wide-Unicode history that expands past the cap behaves identically rather than tripping the oversized-line guard. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::snapshot_is_byte_bounded_and_reports_truncation`.
- 3.2.15 - Resource bounds are the numbers above: a 2 MiB+1 frame is rejected before allocation; a delta queue hitting 64 entries or 2 MiB drops and resyncs; a control queue hitting 16 entries, 64 KiB, or 2s delivery deadline closes the attachment; a 5s lag timeout closes it; a permanently blocked socket may miss the typed error and still sees EOF; eight viewers of one pane plus saturation across `max_attachments_total` stay within `max_attachments_total × (2 MiB + 64 KiB) + max_attached_terminals × tmux_attach_history_max_bytes + (max_attachments_total - 4) × native_scrollback_max_bytes`; a ninth viewer of one pane is refused typed. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/frame_protocol.rs::resource_bounds_are_numeric_and_eof_on_blocked_peer`.
- 3.2.16 - A grid at `MAX_CELLS` filled with maximally expensive cells encodes a keyframe strictly below 2 MiB as `FrameData` **and** as `TerminalAnsi` (including alternating-attribute Unicode); `spawn`/`Hello`/`SetViewport` reject a product above `MAX_CELLS` before allocation; delta overflow of that same grid still recovers with a keyframe the peer accepts in the negotiated encoding. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/frame_protocol.rs::worst_case_keyframe_fits_max_frame_size`.
- 3.2.26 - `AttachHistory` is a separate frame: a maximum-size history payload (256 KiB UTF-8 plus truncation metadata) is emitted and accepted before a subsequent `MAX_CELLS` keyframe; packing that history into the keyframe is rejected; each frame independently stays strictly below `MAX_FRAME_SIZE`. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/frame_protocol.rs::attach_history_then_max_keyframe_fits`.
- 3.2.17 - `subscribe_events` stays inside 256 entries / 256 KiB: title and resize coalesce, an exit is never dropped, overflow closes the subscriber typed, and a reconnect converges from `list` + `last_seq` with host memory released. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::subscribe_events_is_bounded_and_recovers_from_list`.
- 3.2.18 - The host registry is unique across connections: a replay of the same `terminal_id`/`spawn_key`/fingerprint (argv/env/cwd/dims plus `reservation_id`/`reserve_key`) returns the existing terminal and the canonical reservation identity; a conflicting fingerprint is `spawn_conflict`; an old-connection spawn completing after a new-connection retry still yields one PTY. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::spawn_identity_is_unique_across_connections`.
- 3.2.19 - Internal observers scale with live native terminals: five concurrent native streams under user-attachment saturation each have a daemon output observer bound to a control `reserve_observer` reservation; a sixth native spawn that cannot reserve a slot is refused typed **without preempting** an existing direct gclient or proxy/browser attachment; a frame attach without a reservation cannot consume an internal slot; peak memory stays `max_attachments_total × (2 MiB + 64 KiB) + max_attached_terminals × tmux_attach_history_max_bytes + (max_attachments_total - 4) × native_scrollback_max_bytes`. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/frame_protocol.rs::internal_observers_scale_with_live_native_terminals`.
- 3.2.20 - Control disconnect after `spawn_prepared` and before `spawn_commit` keeps the prepared entry and blocked child claimable until `commit_deadline_ms`; `list.observer_bind` stays `reserved` (the prepared entry owns the reservation; the dropped control connection does not free the slot); a new authenticated control connection claims the owner, `list` exposes `pgid`/`start_time` and that `reserved` bind, and a matching `spawn_commit` replay commits once only after persist and attach of that same reservation. Deadline expiry reaps the unexecuted child, removes the prepared entry, drops the prepared-owned reservation, and reports the identity as absent (not committed) on `list`. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::prepare_expires_or_replays_after_control_loss`.
- 3.2.25 - After the internal observer frame disconnects on a committed live native terminal, `list` reports `observer_bind: entitled` with no queue allocated; the reconnecting daemon rebinds that entitlement before a new user attach is admitted; restart under full user saturation still resumes `terminal_output`. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::committed_observer_entitlement_rebinds_under_saturation`.
- 3.2.31 - Repeated distinct native spawns that reserve, prepare, bind, then lose the frame keep a zero-queue entitlement per prepared entry; the same ceiling holds for the earlier cut — repeated distinct control connections that `reserve_observer`, receive `spawn_prepared`, and disconnect **before** `AttachTerminal` keep a prepared-owned `reserved` slot each. A further spawn is refused typed before fork once exactly `max_attachments_total - 4` prepared-or-committed entitlements are held; `ping`, `list`, and `host_shutdown` still succeed on those four reserved lifecycle slots; `list` cardinality includes every prepared row; deadline expiry or prepared `kill` frees one slot that a later spawn can reserve; a rebind of the same `terminal_id`/`spawn_key`/generation commits without consuming a second slot. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::prepared_frame_loss_retains_entitlement_and_saturates`.
- 3.2.33 - Every `TerminalHostConfig` multiplicand in the peak-memory or list-envelope proof has a documented default, floor, and ceiling: `max_attachments_per_terminal` 1..8, `max_attachments_total` 4..128, `max_attached_terminals` 1..64, `tmux_attach_history_lines` 1..2000, `tmux_attach_history_max_bytes` 1 KiB..256 KiB, plus the existing native-scrollback ranges. Below-minimum and above-maximum values are typed startup refusals in Python and in the host; admitted-maximum values rerun 3.2.15 / 3.2.26 / 3.2.30 memory, frame, and list-envelope proofs. The host-side suite is `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::host_config_ranges_reject_and_admit_maximum`. test: `tests/config/test_terminal_host_config.py::test_host_config_ranges_reject_and_admit_maximum`.
- 3.2.32 - `control_host_shutdown.json` is in the byte-exact corpus; the Python control client (4.1) decodes the same bytes. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/wire_golden.rs::golden_corpus_covers_host_shutdown`.
- 3.2.21 - After measuring `FrameData` and `TerminalAnsi`, `wire_types.rs` constants re-exported from `wire.rs` and `src/gobby/terminals/dimensions.py` carry the same min/max rows, cols, and cells; this leaf's Rust sources stay below 1,000 lines. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/wire_golden.rs::python_dimensions_match_wire_constants`.
- 3.2.22 - Observer reservations follow reserved(control) → reserved(prepared entry at `spawn_prepared`) → bound → entitled on frame loss for prepared and committed entries: a same-connection `reserve_observer` replay with the same `reserve_key` returns the same `reservation_id`; `spawn` names `reservation_id` and `reserve_key` and `spawn_prepared` transfers that exact reservation to the prepared registry entry; a distinct-key second reservation for the same `terminal_id` is not transferred; a stale generation is refused typed; concurrent replay of the named reservation returns the same identity without a second slot; `AttachTerminal` then transfers ownership to the frame connection; control disconnect releases only reservations with no prepared entry (including an unused distinct-key reservation) and does not drop the prepared-owned one; a prepared-owned `reserved` slot survives control loss and is attached on reconnect rather than re-reserved; a prepared/uncommitted bound observer becomes `entitled` on frame loss and is rebound before commit; a committed live native bound observer becomes `entitled` on frame loss; only prepared `kill`, exit, deadline expiry, or explicit `release_observer` drops that entitlement; stale disconnect and stale release callbacks are generation-guarded. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::reserve_observer_state_machine`.
- 3.2.23 - Live-control cleanup does not require disconnect: `release_observer` keyed by `reservation_id` and `reserve_key` is idempotent for repeated and stale-generation calls; `kill` of a prepared `host_terminal_id` closes the barrier, reaps the group, and removes the prepared entry while the control connection stays up. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::release_observer_and_prepared_kill_without_disconnect`.
- 3.2.24 - A 1 MiB raw write/paste of worst-case JSON-escaping UTF-8 is delivered (or refused typed at the 1 MiB raw cap) without tripping the 2 MiB line close; the encoded JSON line is preflighted; oversized spawn `argv`/`env`/`cwd` is `request_too_large`; golden write fixtures pin `encoding: "utf8-b64"`. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::encoded_control_line_stays_inside_max`.
- 3.2.27 - Authenticated `ping` carries `host_pid` as a JSON number equal to the serving process; the golden `control_ping.json` freezes that field; a ping missing `host_pid` is a typed Rust decode error. The Python decoder and its missing-field refusal are 4.1.14. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::ping_carries_host_pid`.
- 3.2.28 - `SetScrollOffset` is attachment-local: two native attachments hold independent offsets; a later joiner starts at the live edge until it sets its own; live output while scrolled away does not snap the offset; jump-to-bottom is `rows_from_live_edge: 0`; reconnect restores from a fresh attach at the live edge; applied/max travel on `ScrollOffsetApplied` and are pinned in the golden corpus; an offset past retained scrollback after oldest-first eviction clamps to the new max. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/frame_protocol.rs::set_scroll_offset_is_attachment_local`.
- 3.2.29 - Native scrollback plateaus: sustained output past `native_scrollback_max_lines` and past `native_scrollback_max_bytes` evicts oldest rows, updates `{truncated, dropped_bytes, total_bytes}`, keeps host memory at or below the peak formula, and leaves `SetScrollOffset` unable to address evicted history. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::native_scrollback_plateaus_at_configured_ceilings`.
- 3.2.30 - Host `list` stays inside the 2 MiB control-line cap at `max_attachments_total + max_attached_terminals` rows with escaping-heavy 1024-byte titles **and** the longest `observation_reason` code (`geometry_exceeds_max_cells`) on every row; a longer OSC or tmux title is truncated to `title_max_bytes` on a code-point boundary and still appears; a free-form reason string is not a legal encoding; the complete encoded envelope is strictly below 2 MiB. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::list_envelope_fits_under_line_cap`.
- 3.2.34 - Control `spawn` carries `reservation_id` and `reserve_key` in `control_spawn.json` and in the request fingerprint: a spawn missing either field, naming a reservation for a different `terminal_id`, or naming a stale generation is refused typed before fork; two live distinct-key reservations for one `terminal_id` plus a spawn that names only the second transfers that second reservation and leaves the first connection-owned; disconnect then releases only the unnamed one; a matching replay returns the canonical reservation identity without a second PTY. test: `crates/gterminal/tests/control_protocol.rs::spawn_selects_named_reservation`.

- `crates/gclient/src/views/mod.rs`
- `crates/gclient/src/persist.rs`
- `crates/gclient/src/input.rs`
- `crates/gclient/src/teardown.rs`
- `crates/gclient/src/copy_mode.rs`
- `crates/gclient/tests/ws_golden.rs`
- `crates/gclient/tests/teardown.rs`
- `crates/gclient/tests/copy_paste.rs`
- `crates/gclient/NOTICE.md`
- `crates/gclient/LICENSE`
- `crates/gclient/UPSTREAM.md`
- `Cargo.toml` (workspace `members`)
- `Cargo.lock`
- `src/gobby/install/version_pins.py`

New crate `crates/gclient` (package `gobby-client`, binary `gclient`, built without `vt-engine` — no Zig, C1) — a Gobby terminal workspace that **imports herdr's generic UI chrome and makes it Gobby's**: `src/ui/` from the fork point (sidebar, tabs, navigator, status bar, keybind help with live filtering, dialogs, scrollbar, widgets, ~13.5k LOC) lands as `crates/gclient/src/ui/`, rebranded and de-agent-ified the same way 1.3 carves the core — herdr's agent-detection-fed indicators, plugin menu entries, worktree/session-persistence surfaces, onboarding, release-notes, and mobile modules are dropped; data sources are rewired from herdr's app state to the Gobby daemon (roster, attention, tasks). Reused from `gobby-terminal`: `layout` (BSP splits), `protocol` (frame client), input capture/encoding; from `gobby-core`: `bootstrap`/`daemon_url`/`local_token` for daemon discovery and auth. `crates/gclient/Cargo.toml` depends on `gobby-terminal` with `default-features = false` (workspace path in-tree; the same version string `cargo publish` will require on crates.io — 3.6.6). The app shell (`src/app/`) and daemon data plane are written fresh; herdr's `src/app/` orchestration is not imported.

**Imported UI files finish below 1,000 lines in this leaf.** herdr `ui/sidebar.rs` (~2.8k) and `ui/panes.rs` (~1.5k) are split after import: rows extract to `crates/gclient/src/ui/sidebar_rows.rs`, layout extract to `crates/gclient/src/ui/pane_layout.rs`. Every hand-maintained file under `crates/gclient/src/` must close below 1,000 lines.

- **Design system, not herdr's skin.** `crates/gclient/src/theme.rs` defines the Gobby terminal token map derived from `.impeccable.md` and `web/src/styles/tokens.css`: brand accent hue 125, state palette (info 250 blue / warning 75 amber / destructive 350 magenta-pink / success as lightness-differentiated brand hue) mapped to 24-bit RGB with an ANSI-256/16 degradation table that orders state by lightness first (deutan-safe — hue is never the only signal, per the hard color constraints), dark default with light as a first-class peer, and voice rules applied to every label, dialog, and error (no emoji in errors, no exclamation marks). Herdr's `terminal_theme.rs` mechanism carries the tokens; the values are Gobby's. Implementation of this deliverable reads `.impeccable.md` and loads the `impeccable` skill before styling work, per the repo design contract. The imported settings surface is scoped to **client-local preferences only** (theme, keybinds, layout defaults under `~/.gobby/client/`); daemon configuration is not editable from gclient in this epic.

- **Daemon data plane** (`src/daemon/`): REST via `reqwest` (rustls) — `GET /api/terminals` (2.5 cursor pages, default `pending|live`), `GET /api/attention/roster`, `POST /api/attention/{entry}/respond|seen`, spawn/terminate via the existing agent HTTP surface; WS events via `tokio-tungstenite` on `/ws` with `Authorization: Bearer` — implementing the subscribe-first reconciliation handshake exactly as the sibling plan pinned it (subscribe, buffer attention events, fetch roster, discard `seq <=` cursor within epoch, apply the rest, refetch on epoch change). The workspace roster walks `GET /api/terminals` pages until the cursor is exhausted; it does not request retained history unless the operator opens a history view.
- **Workspace UI** (`src/app/`, `src/views/`): project selection; agent roster sidebar with lifecycle + attention indicators (blocked reason/kind, seen stamping); BSP tabs/splits/panes hosting terminal views; a respond affordance that answers structured attention prompts (options/text/key) via the daemon API — never by raw keystroke injection.
- **Terminal views**: attach per terminal for **frames only** through `crates/gclient/src/frame_source.rs`, a small frame-source trait whose only implementation in this epic is the local Unix-socket frame client; a daemon-WS remote source is deferred to D2 (the multi-machine story), and nothing outside the trait may assume a socket path. The frame channel is read-only and has no input verb (3.2), so a terminal view is a renderer, not a writer. Taking control and writing are both daemon round trips over the same REST/WS data plane this crate already uses for roster, spawn, and respond: the focused pane asks the daemon's lease surface for control, and captured keystrokes are sent to the daemon, which writes them to the terminal through the owning runtime. There is no token to hold and no attachment to upgrade; the host is never asked for permission because it grants none. Unfocused panes render without requesting control, and the pane sends `terminal_release_control` on focus change (2.5.19) so the attachment stays attached and rendering. When another surface takes the lease, the pane renders a visible read-only indicator from the daemon's `terminal_lease_lost` event rather than silently swallowing keystrokes, and offers an explicit take-back. The reducer ignores `terminal_attach_result`, `terminal_control_result`, and `terminal_lease_lost` whose `lease_generation` is below the highest generation already applied for that `attachment_id`, so a delayed grant cannot make a displaced pane writable. `terminal_attachment_finalized` is applied idempotently by exact `attachment_id`: it marks that id non-live, releases any in-flight fragment accounting for it, and is a no-op if the id is already non-live. It does **not** require a greater `lease_generation`. A fragment, output, or lease notification for a finalized id is dropped and creates no state. If the daemon is unreachable the pane keeps rendering frames from the host and shows read-only, which is the honest state rather than a degradation. Input capture (crossterm raw mode, bracketed paste, kitty enhancement flags where available) forwards to the daemon for the focused terminal; detach on pane close leaves PTYs running. Outer-terminal bracketed-paste decoding reuses herdr 0.8.0 `raw_input.rs`: an incomplete `\x1b[200~…` is not flushed on read timeout. `terminal_attach` from this crate always sends `frame_delivery: "direct"`. On daemon-WS reconnect the crate re-registers every live pane, treats old `attachment_id`s as dead, stays observe-only until re-registered, then reacquires control through take-control. gclient's terminal-WS encoder and decoder obey 2.5's `terminal_ws_safe_integer_max` for `message_seq` and `lease_generation`: they compare those fields as exact integers, refuse `2^53` as `safe_integer_overflow`, and never emit strings or floats for them.

  **Direct frame failure notifies the daemon.** This crate is the only process that sees host-frame EOF, typed lag close, epoch loss, or other frame-stream failure on a `direct` attachment. Before it reattaches or discards the pane, it sends `terminal_detach{terminal_id, attachment_id}` on the still-healthy daemon WebSocket so 2.5's attachment finalizer can release the lease. A test keeps the daemon WS connected, kills only the frame stream while the pane holds control, and proves finalization, a stale old `attachment_id`, and fresh-attach-before-control.

  **Frame attach checks `Welcome.host_epoch` before `AttachTerminal`.** The shared frame-source trait takes `expected_host_epoch` from `AttachLocator.frame_host_epoch` (2.1.20): native from the row, tmux from the currently adopted host. After `Welcome`, compare immediately; on mismatch close with typed `HostEpochChanged` and send **no** `AttachTerminal`. This is the same matrix 4.1 already requires of the Python client. A host replacement that recycles `host_terminal_id` must not bind.
- **Scrollback, copy, and paste** (`src/copy_mode.rs`). Attachment-local and lease-independent for *read*: keyboard and mouse navigation, Unicode-safe selection, clipboard copy, a visible new-output indicator while scrolled away from the live edge, and jump-to-bottom. Native panes move within host scrollback through attachment-local `SetScrollOffset{rows_from_live_edge}` (3.2) and render from the `ScrollOffsetApplied` ack plus subsequent keyframes; they do not invent a second host API. External tmux panes seed copy-mode from the standalone `AttachHistory` frame 3.4 emits to **this** attachment before its first keyframe (creator, concurrent loser, and later joiner alike) and scroll that payload locally. Two observers may sit at independent offsets. Copy does not resize a PTY, send input, or mutate tmux. For wrapped-line selection, cherry-pick herdr `952729ee` (#2735, post-`v0.8.0`) so logical lines survive extract — that is a copy-mode fix, not a paste-to-PTY fix. Tmux `AttachHistory` is captured with `capture-pane -J` so soft wraps stay distinct from hard newlines in that payload. **Paste-to-PTY is lease-gated:** a crossterm `Paste` or explicit paste key on a focused pane that holds the lease sends `terminal_paste` with the raw clipboard text and the next `client_write_seq`; the daemon/`write_paste` applies herdr 0.8.0 `paste_payload` and answers `terminal_write_outcome`. Without the lease the paste is refused and the pane stays read-only. An `indeterminate` outcome enters uncertain read-only for that attachment and is not resent. Paste into copy-mode/search and other modal text inputs stays local (herdr `paste_into_active_text_input`). Image/remote clipboard bridging is not imported.
- **Persistence** (`src/persist.rs`): local JSON workspace snapshot per project at `~/.gobby/client/<project_id>/workspace.json` (atomic tmp+rename), referencing durable `terminal_id`s; restore re-attaches to live terminals and drops rows for dead ones.
- **One RAII terminal guard.** Reuse herdr's teardown behind one idempotent guard created in `teardown.rs` *before* the first raw-mode / alt-screen / kitty-flag mutation. Drop restores modes in reverse order. The panic hook and handled SIGINT/SIGTERM call the same guard. 3.5's startup checks run before this guard is armed.
- **Licensing artifacts for this crate** (moved here from 1.5, which cannot own files under a directory a later phase creates): `crates/gclient/LICENSE` (Apache-2.0 text) and `crates/gclient/NOTICE.md` in the same shape 1.5 establishes for `gobby-terminal` — attribution for the herdr-derived portions this crate actually contains (the imported `src/ui/` chrome, BSP layout reuse, input-capture patterns, workspace-snapshot shapes), and the UI module map joins `UPSTREAM.md`'s fork manifest — plus `license = "Apache-2.0"` in `crates/gclient/Cargo.toml`.

`crates/gclient` is added to the root workspace `members` list in this deliverable, for the same reason 1.2 adds `crates/gterminal`: the list is explicit, and a non-member package cannot be built with `cargo -p`, tested by workspace `nextest`, or packaged by the release workflow. The `MANAGED_BIN_VERSION_PINS` entry for `gclient` also lands here rather than in 3.6, because the installer freshness contract (3.6) must exist as soon as the binary does; 3.6 keeps release-asset publication, Homebrew, and the installer fan-out.

The P3 goal is the whole client loop, so spawn and terminate are proven as client workflows rather than as unexercised data-plane calls: 3.3.5 pins one reducer/event-loop-level pass over select → spawn → reconcile → attach → terminate against scripted daemon and frame endpoints, using the same in-process harness as the other client tests instead of a separate E2E stack (E1 owns the real-daemon version). That pass is deliberately **backend-agnostic**: it attaches to a scripted frame server rather than to a real tmux embed, because the tmux attach primitive does not exist until 3.4 and asserting "default-tmux attach" at this boundary would be asserting something this leaf cannot build. gclient's tmux-specific attach behavior is accepted in 3.4, which owns the primitive and, with it, the client-side invoker and golden-wire update.

**Acceptance:**

- 3.3.1 - `gclient` renders roster + attention from live daemon data with the subscribe-first handshake (no lost or reordered attention transitions across the roster fetch). test: `crates/gclient/tests/reconciliation.rs::subscribe_first_no_regression`.
- 3.3.2 - Split/tab/pane management drives per-terminal attach with focus-follows-control: every pane attaches read-only, the focused pane takes control through the daemon's lease surface and routes its keystrokes to the daemon, focus change sends `terminal_release_control` and keeps the pane attached, a `terminal_lease_lost` event renders a read-only state with a take-back affordance, a delayed `terminal_control_result` with a lower `lease_generation` is ignored, an equal-generation `held` or already-released `terminal_control_result` is applied, and an unreachable daemon leaves panes rendering frames while read-only. A source scan finds no host-directed write in the crate. test: `crates/gclient/tests/workspace.rs::focus_moves_control_through_the_daemon`.
- 3.3.3 - An actionable attention prompt is answerable from the client through the daemon respond API, including stale-episode (409) surfacing. test: `crates/gclient/tests/attention_flow.rs::respond_via_daemon_api`.
- 3.3.4 - Workspace snapshots persist and restore by durable terminal id, dropping dead terminals gracefully. test: `crates/gclient/tests/persist.rs::restore_drops_dead_terminals`.
- 3.3.5 - One event-loop-level pass drives the full client loop against scripted endpoints: select a project, spawn through the daemon agent API (no backend field — backend selection arrives in 4.2), reconcile the returned terminal into a new pane, attach to a scripted frame server and render its first frame, terminate through the daemon API, and observe the pane removed on the lifecycle event while a second pane stays attached and streaming. No real tmux embed is involved; that path is 3.4's. test: `crates/gclient/tests/client_loop.rs::select_spawn_attach_terminate_loop`.
- 3.3.6 - `gobby-client` ships a LICENSE and a NOTICE with Upstream and Modifications sections covering its herdr-derived portions, declares `license = "Apache-2.0"`, and records its UI-module accept/reject map in `crates/gclient/UPSTREAM.md`. file: `crates/gclient/NOTICE.md`.
- 3.3.7 - `crates/gclient` is a root workspace member so `cargo build -p gobby-client` and workspace `nextest` resolve it without `zig` on PATH, and `MANAGED_BIN_VERSION_PINS` carries a `gclient` floor the installer uses. file: `Cargo.toml`.
- 3.3.8 - The imported UI chrome is carved like the core: no module under `crates/gclient/src/ui/` references herdr agent detection, plugins, worktrees, integrations, persistence, onboarding, release notes, or mobile concepts (carve-guard source scan), and the sidebar/navigator/status render from Gobby roster and attention data. test: `crates/gclient/tests/ui_carve_guard.rs::no_herdr_concepts_in_imported_chrome`.
- 3.3.9 - The theme module carries the Gobby token map: state colors match the `.impeccable.md` palette (info 250 / warning 75 / destructive 350 / success by lightness), every state indicator carries an icon or position cue so state survives a monochrome rendering, the ANSI-256/16 degradation orders state by lightness, and both dark and light themes render. test: `crates/gclient/tests/theme.rs::tokens_match_design_contract_and_survive_monochrome`.
- 3.3.10 - Terminal views resolve their frame stream only through the frame-source trait, whose sole implementation is the local Unix-socket client; a source scan finds no direct socket dial outside it. file: `crates/gclient/src/frame_source.rs`.
- 3.3.11 - `gclient` attaches with `frame_delivery: "direct"`, decodes every 2.5 terminal-WS golden fixture byte-for-byte, and after a scripted daemon-WS drop re-registers each live pane with a fresh `attachment_id` while rendering observe-only until take-control succeeds. test: `crates/gclient/tests/ws_golden.rs::direct_attach_and_reconnect_reregisters`.
- 3.3.12 - The RAII guard restores termios and terminal modes on normal quit, an injected post-raw-mode startup failure, panic, and handled SIGINT/SIGTERM; a second drop is a no-op. test: `crates/gclient/tests/teardown.rs::guard_restores_on_quit_failure_panic_and_signal`.
- 3.3.13 - Scrollback/copy is attachment-local: two observers sit at independent offsets, wide-grapheme selection copies the logical line (herdr #2735), live output continues with a new-output indicator, no PTY resize, input, or tmux mutation occurs, and a later-joining pane seeds copy-mode from the `AttachHistory` frame it received rather than requiring `created: true`. test: `crates/gclient/tests/copy_paste.rs::scrollback_copy_is_lease_independent`.
- 3.3.20 - Native copy-mode uses `SetScrollOffset`: a later joiner, two independent offsets, live output while scrolled, reconnect, and jump-to-bottom (`0`) each render from host scrollback without PTY input, mouse-report, or `TIOCSWINSZ`; tmux copy-mode still uses `AttachHistory` and distinguishes a soft-wrapped wide-grapheme line from a hard newline. test: `crates/gclient/tests/copy_paste.rs::native_set_scroll_offset_and_tmux_wrap_history`.
- 3.3.14 - Paste-to-PTY uses herdr 0.8.0 `paste_payload` via `terminal_paste`/`write_paste`: with the lease and bracketed paste on, a multiline clipboard arrives as one `\x1b[200~…\x1b[201~` unit and does not submit each line; without the lease the paste is refused and the PTY is unchanged; oversize is refused typed; `terminal_write_outcome` correlates the paste's `client_write_seq`; modal/copy-search paste stays local. test: `crates/gclient/tests/copy_paste.rs::paste_is_lease_gated_and_bracketed`.
- 3.3.15 - Direct frame attach supplies `expected_host_epoch` from `AttachLocator.frame_host_epoch` (native row or adopted host for tmux), compares it to `Welcome.host_epoch` before any `AttachTerminal`, and on mismatch closes with `HostEpochChanged` without sending attach; a replacement-plus-id-reuse fixture never attaches the stale locator on either backend. test: `crates/gclient/tests/workspace.rs::frame_attach_refuses_epoch_mismatch_before_attach`.
- 3.3.16 - Every hand-maintained file under `crates/gclient/src/` is below 1,000 lines after the UI splits. test: `crates/gclient/tests/source_size.rs::no_src_file_at_or_above_1000_lines`.
- 3.3.17 - A direct frame EOF, typed lag close, or epoch loss while the daemon WebSocket stays connected sends `terminal_detach{terminal_id, attachment_id}` before any reattach: the old attachment is finalized, its `attachment_id` is stale, the lease is released, and control cannot be reacquired until a fresh frame attach succeeds. test: `crates/gclient/tests/workspace.rs::direct_frame_eof_detaches_before_reattach`.
- 3.3.18 - gclient decodes `message_seq`, `lease_generation`, and `client_write_seq` as JSON numbers in `[0, terminal_ws_safe_integer_max]` (`2^53-1`): `2^53-2` and `2^53-1` remain distinct; a fixture or emit of `2^53` is refused `safe_integer_overflow`; the crate never emits strings or floats for these fields. test: `crates/gclient/tests/ws_golden.rs::seq_and_lease_generation_are_safe_integers`.
- 3.3.19 - On a still-open daemon WebSocket, `terminal_attachment_finalized` for a live attachment that is mid-fragment marks that `attachment_id` non-live, releases its reassembly accounting, and a subsequent stale fragment for the same id creates no state. The event is applied by exact `attachment_id` even when `lease_generation` did not advance. test: `crates/gclient/tests/ws_golden.rs::finalized_mid_fragment_drops_stale_slice`.
- 3.3.21 - The workspace roster requests `GET /api/terminals` with the default `pending|live` filter and follows cursors until exhausted; an unpaged dump is not used; retained history is a separate explicit query. test: `crates/gclient/tests/workspace.rs::roster_follows_paginated_pending_live`.
- 3.3.22 - gclient decodes `client_write_seq` and `terminal_write_outcome` from the 2.5 goldens: `2^53-2` and `2^53-1` remain distinct and `2^53` is refused; `delivered` leaves the holder writable; `indeterminate` enters uncertain read-only and does not resend input or paste; a refused lease write stays observe-only; `write_seq_conflict`, `write_seq_expired`, and `write_seq_capacity` clear the in-flight mark without resending and without a second PTY mutation. test: `crates/gclient/tests/ws_golden.rs::write_outcome_enters_uncertain_readonly`.

### 3.4 tmux panes observed through the host [category: code] (depends: 3.3, 2.5)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `crates/gterminal/src/host/embed.rs`
- `crates/gterminal/src/host/frames.rs`
- `crates/gterminal/src/protocol/wire.rs`
- `crates/gterminal/src/protocol/wire_types.rs`
- `crates/gterminal/src/protocol/wire_codec.rs`
- `crates/gterminal/tests/source_size.rs`
- `crates/gterminal/tests/wire_golden.rs`
- `crates/gterminal/tests/fixtures/wire_golden/hello.bin`
- `crates/gterminal/tests/fixtures/wire_golden/attach_terminal.bin`
- `crates/gterminal/tests/fixtures/wire_golden/attach_terminal_created.bin`
- `crates/gterminal/tests/fixtures/wire_golden/terminal_ansi.bin`
- `crates/gterminal/tests/fixtures/wire_golden/error_self_view.bin`
- `crates/gterminal/tests/fixtures/wire_golden/error_copy_mode.bin`
- `crates/gterminal/tests/fixtures/wire_golden/error_stale.bin`
- `crates/gterminal/tests/fixtures/wire_golden/error_capacity.bin`
- `crates/gterminal/tests/fixtures/wire_golden/attach_history.bin`
- `docs/contracts/gterm-protocols.md`
- `crates/gclient/src/tmux_identity.rs`
- `crates/gclient/src/frame_source.rs`
- `crates/gclient/src/views/mod.rs`
- `crates/gclient/tests/source_size.rs`
- `src/gobby/servers/routes/terminals.py`
- `src/gobby/terminals/tmux_runtime.py`
- `src/gobby/terminals/host_manager.py`
- `src/gobby/config/terminal_host.py`
- `tests/terminals/test_host_manager.py`
- `crates/gterminal/tests/embed.rs`

tmux-backed terminals (Gobby-spawned runs still on tmux, and externally discovered sessions) render in gclient through the same host path — one rendering pipeline for every backend (decided over a second daemon-WS rendering path in the client):

- **No tmux client, and no tmux mutation either.** The obvious implementation — an ephemeral grouped session that selects the recorded pane and attaches — is wrong on both counts, and both were reproduced against a live tmux server. Grouped sessions have independent *current windows* but they **share each window object, including its single active pane**, so selecting the recorded `pane_id` inside the group moves the owner's active pane (observed: owner active pane changed from `%0` to `%1`). And every attached client, grouped or not, participates in window sizing for the lifetime of the attachment, so a differently sized viewer recomputes the shared window even if it never asks to resize (observed: a 120x39 owner window became 80x19). No tmux option fixes either, because `window-size` and `aggressive-resize` are window properties a group shares. So the viewer creates no tmux client.

  A second design — `pipe-pane -O` for a live byte stream plus `capture-pane` for an initial keyframe — was then tried and also rejected, because splitting observation across two mechanisms creates defects that no amount of care removes. There is no atomic operation joining the two, and live testing shows the seam both ways: capture-then-pipe drops output written between the two calls, and pipe-then-capture renders it twice. `pipe-pane` is also a *mutation of a shared resource with no ownership token* — `#{pane_pipe}` is a bare boolean, so a check-then-install has an unavoidable race, and an install always closes whatever pipe was already there, meaning a viewer can silently destroy a pipe the pane's owner installed for their own purposes. And `capture-pane -p -e` emits rendered screen text with no cursor position, no mode state, and no alternate-screen flag, so it cannot serve as the checkpoint that a subsequent byte delta is interpreted against; a redraw arriving after the keyframe can land at the wrong cursor. Each of those is fixable in isolation and unfixable together: a sequence-numbered atomic cutover, an ownership protocol tmux does not expose, and a stateful checkpoint protocol would all have to be invented.

- **One observation mechanism: bounded capture polling.** The design therefore uses `capture-pane` for *both* initial and ongoing state, which deletes the cutover, the exclusive resource, and the keyframe-versus-delta contract in one move. While at least one attachment exists, the host polls the pane with a **single batched tmux invocation** that reads the server generation, the pane's full observable state, and the screen together:

  ```
  tmux -S <socket> display-message -p -t <pane_id> \
    '#{pid} #{start_time} #{pane_width} #{pane_height} #{cursor_x} #{cursor_y}
     #{cursor_flag} #{cursor_very_visible} #{alternate_on} #{keypad_cursor_flag}
     #{keypad_flag} #{bracket_paste_flag} #{mouse_standard_flag} #{mouse_button_flag}
     #{mouse_any_flag} #{mouse_all_flag} #{mouse_sgr_flag} #{wrap_flag} #{origin_flag}
     #{insert_flag} #{scroll_region_upper} #{scroll_region_lower} #{pane_in_mode}
     #{cursor_shape} #{cursor_blinking} #{cursor_colour} #{mouse_utf8_flag}
     #{pane_dead}' \
    \; display-message -p -t <pane_id> $'GTERM_TITLE_LEN=#{n:pane_title}\nGTERM_TITLE=#{pane_title}' \
    \; capture-pane -p -e -N -t <pane_id>
  ```

  **The header is fixed-domain only.** `pid`, times, dimensions, flags, and `pane_dead` are numeric or enumerated. The header is exactly those N whitespace-separated fields, then a newline, then the `GTERM_TITLE_LEN=…` / `GTERM_TITLE=…` block (length-prefixed so embedded newlines and tabs cannot steal fields), then a guaranteed `GTERM_CAPTURE\n` sentinel the host inserts between the title block and `capture-pane` output by wrapping the batch in a tiny `sh -c` that prints the sentinel — or, equivalently, `capture-pane` is the last command and the parser consumes exactly `title_len` bytes of title then treats the remainder as the screen. Implement the length-prefix + remainder split. A title containing spaces, tabs, ESC, or a fake `#{pid} …` line must not change geometry, generation, or liveness. `#{pane_title}` is **not** in the numeric header.

  Every numeric/flag value in a poll still comes from one server-state read, so geometry can never describe a different screen than the one returned beside it — a claim the round-4 review reproduced on tmux 3.7b, racing 3,000 batched polls against alternating resizes without a single disagreement between the reported dimensions and the captured screen. The host renders the captured text into a pane-sized grid, diffs it against the previous poll, and emits ordinary `FrameData` cell deltas — the frame protocol is unchanged, and the deltas are *computed by the host from complete observations* rather than reconstructed from a byte stream, which is a better fit for the existing cell-diff encoding than raw bytes would have been. The tmux footprint is **zero** — no client, no pipe, no option, no selection change, nothing to restore on detach, and nothing an owner can observe except the read-only commands in their server's log.

  The cost is stated rather than hidden: a viewer sees the pane at poll granularity, so output that appears and is overwritten inside one interval is never rendered, and live scrollback beyond the visible pane is not streamed.

**One-time attach-history is a specified capture, not an implication.** Capture runs once per observer-registry generation, on the `created: true` attach that creates that generation: the host issues one additional `capture-pane -p -e -J -S -<N>` against that pane before the live visible-screen poll loop starts. `-J` joins soft-wrapped rows so `AttachHistory` keeps wraps distinct from hard newlines; the UTF-8 byte cap is applied after that join. `N` is `tmux_attach_history_lines` on `TerminalHostConfig`, default **500**, floor **1**, ceiling **2000**. The response is UTF-8-byte-capped at `tmux_attach_history_max_bytes`, default **256 KiB**, truncated from the oldest end on a code-point boundary. The host retains that bounded `AttachHistory{text, truncated, dropped_bytes, total_bytes}` value on the observer and **emits a copy to every attachment** — creator, concurrent loser, and later joiner — as a standalone frame **before that attachment's first keyframe**. It is never packed into `FrameData` (3.2.26; 3.2 corpus regenerated in this leaf). Each of those two frames is independently bounded by `MAX_FRAME_SIZE`. Subsequent polls remain visible-screen only (`capture-pane -p -e -N` with no `-S` and no `-J`). `-N` keeps trailing spaces so right-edge cells with a non-default background or style survive; default-cell padding cannot reconstruct them. AttachHistory stays on `-J` (no `-N` required there) because join-wrapped-lines already preserves trailing spaces while joining wraps. A second observer of the same generation does not recapture. The cached payload counts toward host memory (`max_attached_terminals × tmux_attach_history_max_bytes`) and is freed when that observer generation is reaped (last detach, or a new generation after detach-to-zero). gclient and the browser proxy seed copy-mode from the `AttachHistory` / `terminal_attach_history` they actually receive. Oversize or tmux history-limit eviction sets `truncated` and unknown counters the same way 2.2 snapshots do. For a read-mostly viewer of a pane another program owns, that is the right trade — the alternative was byte fidelity purchased with a destructive mutation and a lossy seam. Gobby-owned tmux terminals are unaffected by the trade in practice because they are migrating to the native backend anyway (P4), and external panes are exactly the case where non-interference matters more than frame-perfect fidelity.

- **The screen is not the whole state, and the missing bits are the ones that change invisibly.** An earlier form of this batch read only dimensions, cursor position, `pane_dead`, `alternate_on`, and the title, on the assumption that `capture-pane -e` carries everything else. It does not, and the gap is not academic: the round-4 review hid the cursor with DECTCEM in a live pane and observed `#{cursor_flag}` flip while the captured bytes and every field in that batch stayed byte-identical. A renderer diffing consecutive observations would therefore draw a cursor the application had turned off, indefinitely, with nothing to notice. The same class covers the input modes — application-cursor-keys, application-keypad, bracketed paste, and the mouse reporting modes — which change no cell either: observers still need those flags on the frame stream, while `TmuxTerminalRuntime` encodes keystrokes from an at-write pane query (2.2.19), not from this poll cache.

  The fix costs one longer format string, because tmux exposes all of it.
