- `src/gobby/runner_broadcasting.py::*` — scope-reason: terminal_backend ingress and native-parity edits enumerated in the body
- `src/gobby/mcp_proxy/tools/agents_query_tools.py::*` — scope-reason: terminal_backend ingress and native-parity edits enumerated in the body

- **Backend selection.** `spawn_agent` accepts `terminal_backend: "tmux"|"native"` (default from `TerminalConfig.default_backend`, 2.2, which stays `"tmux"` until 5.3), and selection is recorded on the terminal row. A cross-surface request field has to be added at every ingress that constructs a spawn, not only where spawns execute, so this deliverable owns all three schema owners: the MCP tool signature and schema factory (`mcp_proxy/tools/spawn_agent/_factory.py:303`, where `spawn_agent`'s parameters and docstring define the tool schema), the HTTP request model (`AgentSpawnRequest`, `servers/routes/agent_spawn.py:45`, including its batch form), and the dispatch action (`SpawnAgentAction`, `dispatch/actions.py:10`, plus **both** of its constructors, `src/gobby/dispatch/_rule_actions.py:202` and `src/gobby/dispatch/_planning_enhancement.py:91`). Each surface validates the value against the two allowed backends and rejects anything else typed, rather than defaulting silently. The planning-enhancement constructor is called out explicitly because it is the one that is easy to miss — it builds a `SpawnAgentAction` from a different code path than the rule actions, so a sweep that covers only rule constructors would let enhanced planning spawns silently lose the requested backend.
- **A field on the action is not a field that arrives.** Adding `terminal_backend` to `SpawnAgentAction` accomplishes nothing unless the code between the action and the effect forwards it, and in this codebase that code enumerates fields by hand: `src/gobby/dispatch/spawn.py` destructures the action attribute by attribute into its `spawn_agent_impl(...)` call (`:322`, passing `agent_lookup_name=action.agent_slug`, `task_id=action.task_id`, `model=action.model_override`, `reasoning_effort=action.reasoning_effort` at `:326`-`:341`), so an unlisted field is dropped silently with no type error anywhere — the dispatcher would accept a native-backend spawn request and produce a tmux terminal. `dispatch/spawn.py` therefore validates and forwards the field, and the acceptance drives the full dispatcher path rather than only constructing the action. The other two `spawn_agent_impl` callers are inventoried at the same time: `src/gobby/servers/routes/agent_spawn.py:339` is already a schema owner above and forwards it there, and `src/gobby/scheduler/executor.py:328` forwards the configured default explicitly rather than relying on a parameter default, so scheduled spawns cannot silently diverge from the daemon's configured backend once 5.3 flips it.
- **`SpawnRequest` is the next typed boundary, not a comment.** `spawn_agent_impl` does not call `execute_spawn` with kwargs. It constructs `src/gobby/agents/spawn_models.py::SpawnRequest` and passes that object. That model has no `terminal_backend` today. Adding the field only on `spawn_agent_impl` and only reading it inside `execute_spawn` from a side channel would drop the resolved value. This leaf therefore adds `terminal_backend: Literal["tmux", "native"]` to `SpawnRequest`, populates it at backend resolution in `spawn_agent_impl` (explicit request or `TerminalConfig.default_backend`), and `execute_spawn` reads **only** that field. A source scan of `SpawnRequest(` constructions must populate it. The exact resolved value — including a scheduled default and an explicit `"native"` — is asserted across that boundary.
- **SRT.** The provider command passes through the single 2.3 chokepoint at the runtime seam — wrapped exactly once, after construction, immediately before `runtime.prepare_spawn` — so the native path adds no wrap site of its own; the wrapped argv/env goes to control `spawn`. SRT preflight failure aborts the launch with no unsandboxed fallback (existing semantics). No sandbox policy changes are needed: sandboxed processes never dial host sockets (C1), and the PTY is provided by the host exactly as tmux provides one today.
- **Hooks and detection.** CLI hook registration is environment-driven and backend-agnostic (unchanged). Idle/prompt/stall detection, attention episodes, and auto-answer paths operate on native runs through the 2.4 runtime calls (`snapshot` text feeding the detection registry, `write_*` for injections) — the same detectors, manifests, and episode CAS.
- **Output streaming.** Continuous output is a frame-protocol concern, not a control-protocol one (2.2, 3.2): `subscribe_events` carries lifecycle only. Native observer reservation, prepared bind/rebind, commit, and failure cleanup live in the shared row-owning primitive `execute_spawn` (2.3) — the same helper `terminal_create` calls. That primitive calls control `reserve_observer` (3.2) **before** `runtime.prepare_spawn` (capacity hold keyed by `terminal_id`/`reserve_key`), copies `{reservation_id, reserve_key}` onto `TerminalSpawnRequest` so control `spawn` names that exact reservation, then — after `prepare_spawn` returns an attachable `host_terminal_id` and `record_process` has persisted — opens a daemon-side frame attachment with the `TerminalAnsi` encoding through the **shared frame client created in 4.1** that presents the returned `reservation_id` on `AttachTerminal`, confirms the bind, and only then calls `commit_spawn`. `runner_broadcasting.py` does not reserve, prepare, or commit; it only relays `terminal_output` from the observer the primitive already bound. A native spawn that cannot reserve that slot is refused typed before `runtime.prepare_spawn`, including a `terminal_create` whose backend is native or whose default has flipped (5.3). A failed persist, failed bind, or caller cancellation on a still-live control connection aborts without disconnecting: before `spawn_prepared`, `release_observer` the still-unbound reservation; after `spawn_prepared`, `kill` the prepared `host_terminal_id` (closes the barrier, reaps the group, and drops the prepared-owned reservation). Control disconnect after `spawn_prepared` does not free that slot. Reconnect of a prepared entry consults `list.observer_bind`: `bound` is reused, `entitled` is rebound atomically before commit, `reserved` (including a prepared-owned slot that survived control loss) is attached, `none` re-reserves then attaches; commit is refused until that bind is proven. After a successful `spawn_prepared`, `none` does not occur unless the prepared entry was killed or expired. A prepared frame-loss entitlement occupies a slot, so a further spawn is refused before fork once `max_attachments_total - 4` prepared-or-committed entitlements are held. The observer is therefore subscribed before the child can emit or exit. Relay into the existing `terminal_output` broadcast is unchanged from the FIFO path. Its tmux half — the five `tmux_session_name` readers and the `terminal_id` broadcast keying — is migrated in 2.5 alongside the rename, which is why this deliverable **depends on 2.5**: it edits the same file and builds against the post-rename broadcast API, so running the two concurrently would either conflict in `src/gobby/runner_broadcasting.py` or compile this leaf's native source against a broadcast signature 2.5 is about to replace. With the edge in place this deliverable adds only the native source behind an unchanged broadcast call; 4.3 attaches the same client for the browser proxy and neither leaf writes its own decoder. Ordering, cancellation, and reconnect semantics are therefore the frame protocol's, already defined and tested in 3.2: bounded queues, keyframe resync after a delta drop, typed close on lag, and state relearned from `list` plus a keyframe on reconnect. `wait_for_output` (`agents_query_tools.py`) polls `runtime.snapshot` for native runs instead of tmux capture.
- **Lifecycle finalization survives missed events.** Exit events from `subscribe_events` drive the same run-completion path pane-death synthesis drives for tmux (session_end hook synthesis), and capture-before-kill uses `snapshot_full`. A live delivery alone is not enough — an exit landing while the daemon is restarting or the control client is reconnecting would otherwise be lost permanently — so finalization has three converging sources: the subscription is opened **before** the terminal is spawned and its per-terminal `seq` numbers make a gap detectable; every reconnect and daemon start reconciles against control `list`, finalizing any `live` row the host no longer knows (3.1's matrix); and the host-health interval runs the same reconciliation periodically, so a missed exit is finalized within one interval at worst. Duplicate exit deliveries are idempotent because finalization is a CAS off the terminal row.

**Acceptance:**

- 4.2.1 - A native launch runs a real provider CLI in a host PTY with SRT wrapping applied before terminal creation and no policy widening. test: `tests/agents/test_native_spawn.py::test_srt_wrapped_native_launch`.
- 4.2.2 - Attention end-to-end on a native run: prompt detected from host snapshots, episode opened, respond injected via control write with CAS semantics, episode cleared. test: `tests/agents/test_native_spawn.py::test_attention_episode_native`.
- 4.2.3 - `terminal_output` streaming (via the daemon frame observer reserved per 3.2.19), `wait_for_output`, capture-before-kill, and exit finalization all work on native runs, including five concurrent native streams under user-attachment saturation, and the emitted `terminal_output` event sequence is observably equivalent to the tmux FIFO path for the same program output. test: `tests/agents/test_native_spawn.py::test_streaming_wait_capture_exit`.
- 4.2.4 - Backend selection is explicit per spawn, recorded on the terminal row, and defaults to tmux. file: `src/gobby/agents/spawn_executor.py`.
- 4.2.5 - `terminal_backend` is accepted, validated, and carried through every ingress surface — the MCP tool schema, the HTTP `AgentSpawnRequest` (single and batch), and the dispatch `SpawnAgentAction` through **both** its rule-action and planning-enhancement constructors — with a valid value preserved end to end and an invalid value rejected typed at each. test: `tests/agents/test_backend_ingress.py::test_backend_field_at_every_spawn_ingress`.
- 4.2.7 - The field survives forwarding, not just construction: a rule-action and a planning-enhancement spawn each driven through `dispatch/spawn.py` reach `spawn_agent_impl` with the requested backend and produce a terminal row on that backend; a scheduled spawn forwards the configured default explicitly; and a source scan finds no `spawn_agent_impl` call site that omits the field, including the 2.3 split `_execution.py` and every direct-call or fake suite named in Targets. test: `tests/dispatch/test_spawn_forwarding.py::test_terminal_backend_reaches_the_effect`.
- 4.2.12 - `SpawnRequest.terminal_backend` is the only value `execute_spawn` reads for backend selection: an explicit `"native"` and a scheduled default each appear unchanged on the constructed `SpawnRequest` and on the resulting terminal row; every `SpawnRequest(` construction named by a source scan populates the field, including the direct constructors and helper expansions in `tests/agents/test_spawn_executor.py`, `tests/agents/test_spawn_executor_droid.py`, `tests/agents/test_srt_spawn.py`, and `tests/agents/test_verified_review_regressions.py`. test: `tests/agents/test_backend_ingress.py::test_spawn_request_carries_resolved_backend`.
- 4.2.8 - Truncation metadata reaches storage on the native path, not just the boundary: a capture-before-kill of a scrollback of wide Unicode large enough to exceed the host's byte cap persists `truncated` true with `dropped_bytes` and `total_bytes` counted in UTF-8 bytes, the stored text decodes cleanly because the cut landed on a code-point boundary, and the same capture under the cap persists complete with zero drop. test: `tests/agents/test_native_spawn.py::test_oversized_unicode_capture_metadata_reaches_storage`.
- 4.2.6 - A native run's exit is finalized exactly once in each of four cases: live event delivery; exit occurring while the daemon is down (reconciled at next start); exit occurring while the control client is disconnected (reconciled on reconnect); and an event dropped with no reconnect (reconciled by the periodic sweep). Duplicate exit deliveries do not double-finalize. test: `tests/agents/test_native_spawn.py::test_exit_finalization_survives_downtime_and_duplicates`.
- 4.2.9 - The internal observer is bound before commit: a child that writes its first bytes and exits as soon as `spawn_commit` releases it is fully present in `terminal_output` and finalized once; a failed `AttachTerminal` after persist sends no commit, `kill`s the prepared entry and `release_observer`s on the still-open control connection, and leaves the row unpromoted. test: `tests/agents/test_native_spawn.py::test_observer_bound_before_spawn_commit`.
- 4.2.10 - Reconnect after loss before persist, after persist before bind, and after bind before commit each re-establishes persist and observer bind from `list` before `commit_spawn`; a still-bound reservation is reused, an `entitled` prepared slot is rebound, a prepared-owned `reserved` slot that survived control loss after `spawn_prepared` is attached without a second reserve, and only a true `none` bind re-reserves. test: `tests/agents/test_native_spawn.py::test_prepared_reconnect_rebinds_before_commit`.
- 4.2.13 - Repeated prepared frame-loss under user saturation keeps each prepared entitlement; the same ceiling holds for repeated distinct connections that reserve, receive `spawn_prepared`, and disconnect before bind: a further native spawn is refused typed before `prepare_spawn` once `max_attachments_total - 4` entitlements are held; the four lifecycle slots remain usable; deadline expiry or prepared `kill` frees one slot that a later spawn can reserve. test: `tests/agents/test_native_spawn.py::test_prepared_frame_loss_saturates_then_expires`.
- 4.2.14 - Native observer reserve/bind/commit/failure cleanup is inside `execute_spawn`, not `runner_broadcasting`: a source scan finds no `reserve_observer` at a spawn ingress other than that primitive; a native-default `terminal_create` saturates at `max_attachments_total - 4` and is refused typed before fork; prepared `kill` or `commit_deadline_ms` expiry frees one slot that a later `terminal_create` can reserve. test: `tests/servers/test_terminal_ws_create.py::test_native_create_saturates_then_expires`.
- 4.2.11 - After a daemon restart that drops the internal observer frame on a committed live native terminal at full user-attachment saturation, the daemon rebinds the host `entitled` slot and `terminal_output` resumes; no user attachment is preempted. test: `tests/agents/test_native_spawn.py::test_committed_observer_rebinds_under_user_saturation`.
- 4.2.15 - `execute_spawn` forwards the reserved `{reservation_id, reserve_key}` on native `prepare_spawn`: a distinct-key second reservation for the same `terminal_id` is not the one transferred; a stale-generation or missing identity is refused typed before fork; a matching replay returns the canonical reservation without a second PTY; control disconnect after a named `spawn_prepared` still leaves `observer_bind: reserved` and releases only an unused distinct-key reservation. test: `tests/agents/test_native_spawn.py::test_spawn_carries_reservation_identity`.

### 4.3 Web terminal parity through the host for both backends [category: code] (depends: 4.1, 2.5, 3.4)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `src/gobby/servers/websocket/tmux.py::*` — scope-reason: web-proxy migration to the shared frame client and PTY-bridge retirement
- `src/gobby/terminals/host_client.py`
- `src/gobby/terminals/frame_client.py`
- `src/gobby/agents/tmux/pty_bridge.py::*` — scope-reason: web-proxy migration to the shared frame client and PTY-bridge retirement
- `src/gobby/agents/tmux/__init__.py::*` — scope-reason: web-proxy migration to the shared frame client and PTY-bridge retirement
- `tests/agents/test_tmux.py::*` — scope-reason: web-proxy migration to the shared frame client and PTY-bridge retirement
- `tests/agents/test_tmux_integration.py::*` — scope-reason: constructs TmuxPTYBridge; retire or re-express with 4.3
- `web/src/components/activity/terminal/TerminalView.tsx::*` — scope-reason: web-proxy migration to the shared frame client and PTY-bridge retirement
- `web/src/hooks/useTmuxSessions.ts::*` — scope-reason: fragment reassembly lives in the terminal-WS reducer, not the view
- `web/src/hooks/__tests__/useTmuxSessions.test.ts::*` — scope-reason: fragment reassembly, interleaving, and cleanup tests
- `tests/servers/test_native_web_proxy.py`
- `tests/servers/test_web_tmux_through_host.py`

The web terminal views terminals through the daemon as proxy (no browser→host connection): `terminal_attach` makes the daemon open a frame-protocol attach to the host through the shared frame client (4.1) negotiating the `TerminalAnsi` encoding, and relays frames as the existing `terminal_output` events — an `AttachHistory` frame is forwarded as `terminal_attach_history{terminal_id, attachment_id, text, truncated, dropped_bytes, total_bytes}` before that attachment's first `terminal_output` keyframe and is never merged into it; `terminal_attach` from the browser uses `frame_delivery: "proxy"` and is observe-only; `terminal_input`/`terminal_paste`/`terminal_resize` are checked against the daemon's lease record and then dispatched **through the `TerminalRuntime` for that row's backend** — never on the frame attachment, which has no write verb (3.2). `terminal_set_viewport` is not lease-gated and is forwarded only as that tab's host-frame `SetViewport`. `terminal_set_scroll_offset` is the same attachment-local, not-lease-gated forward of `SetScrollOffset` for native panes; tmux observers keep scrolling the `AttachHistory` they already hold. The dispatch is per-backend because the two backends have different write paths and the host has none for tmux: a `native` terminal's input and resize go to the host on the control channel, while a `tmux` terminal's go to `TmuxTerminalRuntime`'s daemon-side `send-keys -t <pane_id> -H` and `resize-pane`, exactly as 3.4 requires — the host observes tmux panes and never writes to them. Both paths return the 2.2 `WriteOutcome`. The proxy emits 2.5 `terminal_write_outcome` with the request's `client_write_seq`: `delivered` keeps the holder writable, `refused` is typed, and `indeterminate` (lost tmux `send-keys` or lost native control response) puts that attachment in uncertain read-only without resending. Write outcomes do not consume lifecycle-reserve capacity. The wterm/ghostty renderer consumes the ANSI stream unchanged (same VT family end-to-end). Per-connection cleanup detaches the daemon's host attachment by calling the 2.5 attachment finalizer — including host-frame EOF, typed lag close, relay overflow, and host loss, not only daemon-WS close.

**The tmux bridge is retired for this surface, so one terminal has one transport.** 2.5 already made the daemon lease the single authority, which stopped a web tab and a gclient pane from both believing they had control. What it could not fix yet is the *transport*: `TmuxPTYBridge` runs its own `tmux` client per browser connection, which is exactly the shape 3.4 proved unsafe — a second client participates in window sizing and can move the owner's selection. With 3.4's capture-poll observer available, the browser's tmux attachments route through the same host path as gclient's: `TmuxPTYBridge` is deleted along with its export, and `src/gobby/servers/websocket/tmux.py` resolves every attach — tmux and native alike — to a host attachment. The result is one observer per physical pane shared by every viewer, one lease record, and one external-geometry policy, with a browser resize on an `external` terminal refused by the daemon and never reaching tmux, identically to gclient. This is the leaf where the cross-surface single-authority claim becomes true for tmux, which is why it depends on 3.4.

**Deleting a class means deleting the tests that construct it.** `TmuxPTYBridge` is referenced in fourteen places in `tests/agents/test_tmux.py` and again in `tests/agents/test_tmux_integration.py` — plus its export in `src/gobby/agents/tmux/__init__.py` and its two construction sites in `websocket/tmux.py`. A retirement that removed the class and left that suite behind would not be a partially-done refactor; it would be an import error on collection. Each bridge test is therefore either **re-expressed** against the frame-client path when it asserts behavior the new transport must still exhibit (a browser attach renders the pane, a disconnect releases everything, input reaches the pane) or **deleted** when it asserts bridge-internal mechanics that no longer exist (its per-connection tmux client, the session-option manipulation 2.5 already removed for external panes). The acceptance requires zero references to the symbol anywhere in `src` or `tests`.

Two policies bind this surface specifically, because it is the second surface that can hold the daemon's lease:

- **Read-only by default, over the 2.5 lease messages.** A web `terminal_attach` grants no write rights; the daemon moves its lease record to that attachment only when the browser sends `terminal_take_control`, answering with `terminal_control_result` that includes `lease_generation`, and a displaced holder — a gclient pane, another tab — receives `terminal_lease_lost` with the same generation and renders read-only, so a takeover is visible instead of silently dropping typed input. The browser reducer ignores any of those authority notifications whose generation is below the highest already applied for that `attachment_id`. `terminal_attachment_finalized` is applied by exact `attachment_id` and does not require a greater generation. `terminal_input`/`terminal_paste`/`terminal_resize` from an attachment that does not hold the lease are refused with a typed reason rather than discarded. Every one of those decisions is made by the daemon against one record; the host is never consulted, which is what keeps a focused gclient pane and an open web tab from ping-ponging authority, and it is why the wire shapes had to exist in 2.5 rather than being implied by policy.
- **Backpressure at both hops.** The daemon's host attachment is an ordinary frame client under the 3.2 bounded-queue contract. The daemon→browser relay is **connection-wide**, not attachment-local: one outbound **frame** queue (64 entries **and** 2 MiB serialized) plus a **5s** send timeout owned by the WebSocket. Frame-queue overflow or socket send timeout closes that WebSocket and runs the 2.5 finalizer on **every** attachment on it (`relay_overflow` / `proxy_lag`). Finalizing only the attachment whose enqueue observed a full shared queue would leave the socket unwritable for the others. A second browser tab is a different connection and is unaffected. Lifecycle events (`terminal_attachment_finalized`, `terminal_lease_lost`, `terminal_control_result`) do not share that frame queue: they use reserved outbound capacity with explicit bounds — `terminal_ws_lifecycle_reserve_max_entries` = **16**, `terminal_ws_lifecycle_reserve_max_bytes` = **64 KiB**, send deadline **2s**. Frame traffic cannot consume that reserve. If the reserved send times out, cannot be enqueued, or exceeds those entry/byte ceilings, the hop is a connection-wide failure and the daemon closes the WebSocket so every attachment and reassembly is torn down by the 2.5 finalizer. After a host-side delta drop the daemon relays the resynchronization keyframe while the connection is still writable; after a browser-side close the tab reconnects and relearns state. A slow or wedged browser therefore degrades only its own connection. Frames the daemon relays are decoded with the golden-corpus-verified frame decoder, pinning the Python frame path against Rust byte drift.

  **JSON is a second envelope and must be bounded after complete serialization.** A host frame legal under `MAX_FRAME_SIZE` can expand again inside `terminal_output` / `terminal_attach_history` JSON (quotes, backslashes, controls, CJK). Before each send the proxy serializes the complete event, including envelope and escaping, and preflights that byte length. A message whose complete JSON is already strictly below 2 MiB is sent unfragmented. A message that would be ≥ 2 MiB is emitted as the 2.5 `terminal_ws_fragment` envelope — not an ad-hoc `{seq, more}` pair. Each fully wrapped fragment is preflighted and stays strictly below 2 MiB.

  The producer is `src/gobby/servers/websocket/tmux.py` (and the shared frame-client hop in `frame_client.py` / `host_client.py`). The sole browser decoder is `web/src/hooks/useTmuxSessions.ts`: it concatenates `payload` bytes for one `(attachment_id, message_seq)` and applies the reconstructed `event` only when `more` is false and indexes are contiguous from 0. Reassembly state is keyed by `attachment_id` then `message_seq`. Aggregate decoded payload bytes for one in-flight message must stay ≤ `terminal_ws_fragment_max_reassembly_bytes` (**16 MiB**). A host frame strictly below 2 MiB can expand ~6× under JSON escaping (`\u00XX` per byte) plus the `terminal_output` / `terminal_attach_history` envelope, so a producer-valid complete event can approach 12 MiB; 16 MiB is the proved shared producer/reducer ceiling that covers every legal complete event. The producer never emits a logical event whose reconstructed JSON exceeds that constant; the reducer rejects one byte over the same limit as `fragment_too_large`. The same reducer also keeps a per-WebSocket running total of decoded in-flight bytes across every live attachment, capped at `terminal_ws_fragment_max_socket_reassembly_bytes` (**64 MiB**). That total is reserved before each append; overflow is typed `fragment_socket_budget` and applies nothing. One browser WebSocket may own many attachments, and `max_attachments_total` (128) × 16 MiB would otherwise retain nearly 2 GiB before per-message limits fire. Buffers start or extend only for a currently live `attachment_id`; a slice after detach, `terminal_attachment_finalized`, finalizer, or an unknown id is dropped and does not recreate state. The browser learns server-initiated observe-only finalization from `terminal_attachment_finalized` on the still-open socket — it does not infer death from a missing lease notification. Accounting is released on completion, sequence error, timeout, lag, detach, `terminal_attachment_finalized`, finalizer, and disconnect. `message_seq` comparisons use the 2.5 safe-integer contract. The first fragment of a `message_seq` starts a `terminal_ws_fragment_reassembly_timeout_ms` timer (default **5000**, floor 100, ceiling 30000). Sequence error (index gap or duplicate, `event`/`terminal_id` mismatch across slices, a new `message_seq` on the same attachment while one is incomplete), timeout, or overflow drops that buffer, emits typed `fragment_sequence` / `fragment_timeout` / `fragment_too_large` / `fragment_socket_budget`, and applies nothing. Completion, sequence error, typed lag close, disconnect, and the 2.5 attachment finalizer all clear that attachment's buffers. Two attachments interleave independently. Back-to-back messages on one attachment increment `message_seq`. A fragmented `terminal_attach_history` must fully reassemble and apply before that attachment's first keyframe. Maximum alternating-attribute Unicode grids are a required test through `TerminalAnsi` and this hop.

**Acceptance:**

- 4.3.1 - The web terminal attaches (`frame_delivery: "proxy"`), streams, types into, pastes into, resizes, set-viewports, and detaches from a native terminal via the daemon proxy with no frontend renderer changes beyond backend-neutral plumbing. test: `tests/servers/test_native_web_proxy.py::test_web_attach_native_terminal`.
- 4.3.2 - WS connection close, host-frame EOF, typed lag close, relay overflow, and host loss each call the 2.5 attachment finalizer: the daemon's host frame attachment and its lease record entry are released, and control requires a fresh attach. test: `tests/servers/test_native_web_proxy.py::test_disconnect_releases_lease`.
- 4.3.3 - A web attach starts read-only, takes control only via `terminal_take_control` (answered by `terminal_control_result` carrying `lease_generation`), renders read-only on `terminal_lease_lost` when a gclient takeover displaces it, ignores a delayed grant with a lower generation, and refuses input from a non-holding attachment with a typed reason; and the write is dispatched by backend — a native terminal's input reaches the host on the control channel, a tmux terminal's reaches the pane through `TmuxTerminalRuntime` with no host write involved, and in neither case does the frame attachment carry input. test: `tests/servers/test_native_web_proxy.py::test_web_read_only_until_explicit_takeover`.
- 4.3.4 - The daemon relays through the shared 4.1 frame client with no second decoder in the repo; a stalled browser connection resynchronizes via keyframe while writable, then frame-queue overflow or send timeout closes **that** WebSocket and finalizes every attachment on it; the host, a lease holder on a different connection, and a second browser tab stay up; daemon memory for the stalled connection is released. test: `tests/servers/test_native_web_proxy.py::test_shared_decoder_and_slow_browser`.
- 4.3.5 - `TmuxPTYBridge` has zero references anywhere in `src` or `tests` — class, export, construction sites, `tests/agents/test_tmux.py`, and `tests/agents/test_tmux_integration.py` all migrated or deleted — a browser attach to a tmux terminal creates no additional tmux client and mutates nothing on the pane, and with a browser tab and a gclient pane viewing the same external pane simultaneously the owner's window geometry and active pane are unchanged, both surfaces share one observer, takeover works in both directions through the daemon lease record, and a browser resize on the `external` terminal is refused by the daemon and issues no tmux command. test: `tests/servers/test_web_tmux_through_host.py::test_browser_and_gclient_share_one_observer`.
- 4.3.6 - Two browser observers of one terminal can `terminal_set_viewport` independently while a lease-holder `terminal_resize` is the only path that changes native PTY size or is refused on an `external` tmux pane; a browser `terminal_paste` without the lease is refused and does not reach `write_paste`. test: `tests/servers/test_native_web_proxy.py::test_viewport_independent_of_resize_and_paste_is_leased`.
- 4.3.7 - The proxy forwards `AttachHistory` as `terminal_attach_history` before that attachment's first `terminal_output` keyframe (creator, concurrent loser, later joiner); a maximum-size history plus a `MAX_CELLS` `TerminalAnsi` keyframe are two logical events; each event's complete JSON is preflighted; a message ≥ 2 MiB is emitted as `terminal_ws_fragment` slices whose fully wrapped JSON stays strictly below 2 MiB; a maximum alternating-attribute Unicode grid survives `TerminalAnsi` and this hop. test: `tests/servers/test_native_web_proxy.py::test_attach_history_then_max_keyframe_is_fragmented_under_cap`.
- 4.3.8 - Fragment reassembly is owned by `useTmuxSessions.ts`: back-to-back messages on one attachment increment `message_seq` and apply in order; two attachments may interleave slices without mixing payloads; a complete fragmented `terminal_attach_history` applies before that attachment's first keyframe; an index gap, a second `message_seq` while one is incomplete, a timeout, a decoded payload of `terminal_ws_fragment_max_reassembly_bytes + 1` (16 MiB + 1), lag close, and disconnect each drop the buffer and apply nothing. test: `web/src/hooks/__tests__/useTmuxSessions.test.ts::test_fragment_reassembly_and_cleanup`.
- 4.3.9 - An end-to-end near-2-MiB escape-dense `TerminalAnsi` host frame (worst-case JSON expansion approaching 12 MiB) is fragmented by the producer, reassembled by `useTmuxSessions.ts` under the shared 16 MiB ceiling, and applied as one event; a reconstructed payload one byte over that ceiling is refused `fragment_too_large` and applies nothing. test: `tests/servers/test_native_web_proxy.py::test_escape_dense_near_2mib_frame_reassembles_and_one_byte_over_is_rejected`.
- 4.3.10 - Per-WebSocket decoded-reassembly bytes across all live attachments stay ≤ `terminal_ws_fragment_max_socket_reassembly_bytes` (64 MiB): the reducer reserves the incoming slice before append and refuses overflow `fragment_socket_budget` without applying an event; a fragment whose `attachment_id` is not live starts no buffer; accounting is released on completion, sequence error, timeout, lag, detach, `terminal_attachment_finalized`, finalizer, and disconnect. A multi-attachment saturation that would exceed 64 MiB and a fragment after finalizer each apply nothing. Adjacent `message_seq` values `2^53-2` and `2^53-1` remain distinct; a producer value of `2^53` is refused before emit. test: `web/src/hooks/__tests__/useTmuxSessions.test.ts::test_socket_reassembly_budget_and_stale_fragments`.
- 4.3.11 - An observe-only proxy attachment finalized mid-fragment by an attachment-local cause (`proxy_frame_eof` / `host_loss`) emits `terminal_attachment_finalized` on the still-open WebSocket via reserved lifecycle capacity; the reducer marks the id non-live by exact `attachment_id`, releases that attachment's reassembly bytes, and a subsequent stale fragment for the same id creates no state. Frame-queue overflow and reserved-send failure are connection-wide (4.3.12, 4.3.14), not this clause. test: `web/src/hooks/__tests__/useTmuxSessions.test.ts::test_observe_only_finalized_drops_stale_fragments`.
- 4.3.12 - Reserved lifecycle capacity is 16 entries / 64 KiB / 2s: a saturated frame/relay queue still delivers `terminal_attachment_finalized` on the reserve and the reducer marks that id non-live; reserve overflow, a 17th reserved entry, or a wedged reserved send closes the whole WebSocket so every attachment and reassembly is torn down. test: `tests/servers/test_native_web_proxy.py::test_lifecycle_reserved_send_and_wedged_control_closes_socket`.
- 4.3.14 - Connection-wide frame overflow: two attachments on one WebSocket, the enqueue that fills the shared frame queue closes the socket and finalizes both ids; a maximum-cardinality host-loss burst that exceeds the lifecycle reserve also closes the socket rather than leaking reserved buffers. test: `tests/servers/test_native_web_proxy.py::test_frame_queue_overflow_closes_socket_for_all_attachments`.
- 4.3.13 - The proxy forwards native `SetScrollOffset` / `ScrollOffsetApplied` as `terminal_set_scroll_offset` / `terminal_scroll_offset_applied` per attachment, and a tmux `AttachHistory` that contains one soft-wrapped wide-grapheme line plus one hard newline still distinguishes those cases after browser-proxy delivery. test: `tests/servers/test_native_web_proxy.py::test_scroll_offset_and_wrapped_attach_history`.
- 4.3.15 - A lease-holder `terminal_input` and `terminal_paste` on both backends report `terminal_write_outcome` through the real daemon-to-browser hop: a delivered keystroke stays writable; a withheld tmux `send-keys` reply and a dropped native control write each emit `outcome: "indeterminate"` with the same `client_write_seq`; the reducer enters uncertain read-only and does not resend; a subsequent slice or keystroke is not treated as delivered. test: `tests/servers/test_native_web_proxy.py::test_write_outcome_indeterminate_tmux_and_native`.
- 4.3.16 - The 2.5.37 write-seq ledger holds through the real tmux and native hop: eviction of a completed seq then replay is `write_seq_expired`; two simultaneous same-seq same-fingerprint writes join one admission; a 65th distinct in-flight seq is `write_seq_capacity`; reusing one seq for a different kind or payload is `write_seq_conflict`; both reducers stay put and do not resend. Browser reducer-state closure is the 2.5.37 `useTmuxSessions.test.ts` case; this clause is the real daemon-to-browser hop. test: `tests/servers/test_native_web_proxy.py::test_write_seq_ledger_tmux_and_native`.

## P5: Parity, Discovery, and Default Flip

`kind: framing`

**Goal**: Prove both backends equivalent under one contract suite, prove external discovery, then flip the native default for Gobby-managed launches.

### 5.1 Cross-backend runtime contract suite [category: test] (depends: 4.2, 4.3, 3.6, 5.2)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `tests/terminals/test_runtime_contract.py`
- `tests/terminals/conftest.py`
- `.github/workflows/terminal-parity-weekly.yml`

**This leaf owns the weekly producer 5.3 consumes.** It therefore depends on every executable producer that workflow invokes: 4.2 (this contract's native runtime), 5.2 (external e2e), 4.3 (web-proxy focused tests), and 3.6 (`cargo package` smokes). `.github/workflows/terminal-parity-weekly.yml` is a scheduled workflow (`cron` weekly, plus `workflow_dispatch`) with a `macos-latest` and `ubuntu-latest` matrix. Each cell builds `gterm` (Zig/`vt-engine` as in 3.6), then runs those focused producers **and** the 3.6 package-install smoke: the gterm/gclient installer fallback-chain cases in `tests/cli/test_cli_install.py` (the same chain 3.6.1 owns). That smoke is the package-install evidence 5.3 requires; it is not implied by the 3.6 `cargo package` producer. Expansion cannot close this leaf until those commands exist. Each run records workflow name, ISO year-week slot, run URL, commit sha, UTC timestamp, platform, and that cell's package-install result. Existing `rust-ci.yml` stays push/PR Ubuntu-only; tag-driven release workflows stay tag-driven. Landing the file here makes two weekly slots observable after this leaf merges; 5.3 depends on this producer and does not create it.

One parametrized suite runs the same assertions against `TmuxTerminalRuntime` and `NativeTerminalRuntime` (native cases skip with a clear reason when the `gterm` binary is absent; CI builds it): spawn → prompt-ready; literal/submit/named-key input; ANSI + wide-Unicode + emoji-grapheme rendering via snapshot; resize propagation (`$COLUMNS` probe); snapshot/snapshot_full fidelity; clean exit vs terminate-with-grace; detach/reattach continuity; daemon-restart continuity (terminal survives, row reconciled); multi-viewer isolation; attention respond CAS including `stage: partial` classification. Because output streaming is deliberately outside the runtime contract (2.2), the suite asserts it as **observable event parity** instead of a shared method: the same program output produces an equivalent `terminal_output` event sequence on both backends, one through the tmux FIFO reader and one through the daemon frame observer (4.2). Includes the tmux-defect regressions as **native-only guarantees**: authoritative single size under multiple observers and single protocol-reply ownership (from 3.4.3, asserted here at the contract level).

**Acceptance:**

- 5.1.1 - The parametrized suite passes for both backends in CI, with the native leg exercising a real built `gterm`. test: `tests/terminals/test_runtime_contract.py::test_contract_matrix`.
- 5.1.2 - Daemon-restart continuity holds for both backends: terminals survive, rows reconcile, streams resume, and the native leg proves the host was adopted rather than replaced (same `host_epoch`, no terminal orphaned). test: `tests/terminals/test_runtime_contract.py::test_daemon_restart_continuity`.
- 5.1.3 - `.github/workflows/terminal-parity-weekly.yml` is scheduled weekly, runs on `macos-latest` and `ubuntu-latest`, builds `gterm`, and invokes the 5.1, 5.2, 4.3, and 3.6 focused producers **and** the 3.6 installer fallback-chain package-install smoke on each platform, emitting the slot/run/commit/platform/package-install fields 5.3.4 records. file: `.github/workflows/terminal-parity-weekly.yml`.

### 5.2 External-session discovery and attach end-to-end [category: test] (depends: 3.4, 2.5, 2.4)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `tests/e2e/test_external_terminal_attach.py`

Against an isolated test daemon (`GOBBY_TEST_PROTECT=1`, temp state/ports/sockets): launch a scripted CLI session in an external tmux server (default socket) the way a user would — **with its own client attached at a deliberately different geometry, in a session that has multiple windows and multiple panes**, since a single-pane session with no live client cannot exercise either failure this deliverable must rule out; verify SessionStart seeding creates the `external` terminal row (2.5.4 path); list it via `GET /api/terminals`; attach through the host's capture-poll observer (3.4) driving frames to a scripted frame client; respond to a detected attention prompt on the interactive session via the respond API; detach; verify the external session is untouched — still alive, the owner's window geometry byte-identical to before the attach, the owner's current window and active pane unchanged, the pane's full property set (including `#{pane_pipe}`, which is never set) identical to a never-viewed control pane, no additional tmux client ever created, and no injected artifacts beyond the answered prompt; expire it and verify liveness CAS transitions the row. The scenario also resizes the owner's client mid-attach, since an owner resize while a differently sized viewer is connected is the case a client-based design silently loses, and renames the session mid-attach, since a rename is the case an identity built on names silently loses.

**Acceptance:**

- 5.2.1 - The full external flow — discover, list, observe-attach, attention respond, detach, expire — passes against the isolated daemon without relaunching the external CLI. test: `tests/e2e/test_external_terminal_attach.py::test_external_discovery_attach_respond`.
- 5.2.2 - With an owner client attached at a different size to a multi-window, multi-pane session: frames render the discovered pane, input reaches that pane, the owner's window dimensions and current window/active-pane selection are unchanged across attach and detach, the pane's property set matches a never-viewed control pane, an owner resize during the attach leaves the owner authoritative and propagates to the viewer, a session rename during the attach keeps the same terminal row and `terminal_id`, and a viewer `SetViewport` changes only the viewer's render size while issuing no tmux command. test: `tests/e2e/test_external_terminal_attach.py::test_external_owner_geometry_and_selection_preserved`.
- 5.2.3 - Non-interference extends to modes and to failure: hiding the cursor and toggling application-cursor mode in the owner's program are both observed by the viewer with no cell change, the owner entering tmux copy mode is surfaced as copy-mode rather than rendered as program output, and a tmux invocation forced to fail transiently against the still-live pane leaves the row `live` and the attachment open with a stale indicator rather than emitting an exit. test: `tests/e2e/test_external_terminal_attach.py::test_modes_and_transient_failures_on_a_live_external_pane`.

### 5.3 Native default flip [category: config] (depends: 5.1, 5.2, 4.3, 3.6)

`kind: deliverable`

Targets:
- `src/gobby/config/terminals.py`
- `src/gobby/install/shared/config/config.yaml::*` — scope-reason: default-backend flip and gate check
- `docs/evidence/native-backend-flip.md`
- `tests/terminals/test_backend_selection.py`
- `docs/guides/gterminal-development-guide.md`

Flip `TerminalConfig.default_backend` (2.2) to `"native"` for Gobby-managed launches once the flip gate holds: the 5.1 contract suite and 5.2 e2e green on macOS and Linux CI for two consecutive weekly runs with zero open critical (`priority <= 1`) terminal-labeled bugs. tmux remains the automatic backend for externally discovered sessions (ownership `external`) and stays selectable per spawn. The flip is one config-default change plus documentation of the rollback (set default back to `"tmux"`; running native terminals finish in place — symmetric to the forward migration). The rollback note is added to the guide 3.6 creates, which is one of the two reasons this leaf depends on 3.6.

**This leaf consumes weekly evidence; it does not create the producer.** 5.1.3 owns the scheduled weekly workflow. This leaf implements the default flip, the fixture-tested gate checker, and the committed evidence artifact. Two real weekly slots cannot be produced inside this leaf's own PR; 5.3.3/5.3.4 pin the checker against fixtures, and the flip ships when a conforming artifact from the 5.1 producer exists.

**The gate covers every surface the new default reaches, not just the parity suites.** Changing the default changes what an *installed user* gets, so the two contract suites are necessary but not sufficient: without 4.3, a newly managed native run cannot be viewed in the shipped web terminal at all, and without 3.6 the `gterm` host is not a packaged managed binary, so a fresh install has no host to launch into. Both are therefore dependency edges, and the flip evidence must carry their focused CI results and one package-install result alongside the 5.1/5.2 runs. E1 stays the post-flip full-stack verification rather than the gate — it depends on all five phases including this one, so it cannot gate a change inside P5; the gate is exactly the recorded 5.1, 5.2, 4.3, and 3.6 evidence, which is checkable before the default moves.

A gate stated only in prose cannot be checked by the person doing the flip or by review, so the evidence is a committed artifact with a machine-checkable shape: `docs/evidence/native-backend-flip.md` records, per qualifying run, the workflow name, the **scheduled weekly slot identifier** (the ISO year-week the scheduled workflow fired for), the run URL, commit sha, UTC timestamp, the platforms that passed, and that slot's **package-install** result (the 5.1.3 / 3.6 installer fallback-chain smoke on macOS and Linux); plus the 4.3 and 3.6 focused-result lines; plus one line recording the terminal-labeled `priority <= 1` open-bug count, the exact task query used, and the UTC timestamp at which that query ran.

The check is a focused test rather than a CI-API client, and it enforces the gate's *temporal* meaning rather than merely counting entries — two distinct dates is not the same requirement as two consecutive weekly runs, and a bug count from any date is not the same as a current one. It fails unless: two qualifying runs exist whose weekly slot identifiers are **adjacent**, defined by converting each ISO year-week to its Monday date (UTC) and requiring a difference of **exactly seven days** (so `2025-W52`→`2026-W01` and a 53-week year's `W53`→`W01` pass, while a skipped week across the year boundary fails); **each** of those two slots independently contains successful macOS **and** Linux cells **and** that slot's required 4.3, 3.6, and package-install evidence; and the bug-count timestamp is at or after the later run's timestamp and within **24 hours** after that later timestamp, **inclusive**, UTC. Complementary one-platform-per-slot pairs fail. A slot that has both platforms but is missing that slot's 4.3, 3.6, or package-install line fails. Negative fixtures pin each of those rejections, including just-inside, exact-boundary, and just-outside freshness, plus ISO 52-to-01 / 53-to-01 positives and a skipped-boundary negative. That keeps the gate executable and reviewable with no new subsystem, and it fails closed — a flip landed without conforming evidence turns the suite red.

**Acceptance:**

- 5.3.1 - The shipped default backend is `native` for gobby-owned launches, with the gate evidence linked in the change and rollback documented. file: `src/gobby/install/shared/config/config.yaml`.
- 5.3.2 - Explicit per-spawn backend selection and external-session tmux handling are unchanged by the flip. A default-native `terminal_create` still goes through the 4.2.14 shared primitive and is refused before fork at `max_attachments_total - 4`. test: `tests/terminals/test_backend_selection.py::test_flip_preserves_explicit_and_external`.
- 5.3.3 - With the default `native`, the gate check rejects each of: runs one day apart, runs in non-adjacent weekly slots (including a skipped week across a year boundary), a macOS-only slot paired with a Linux-only slot, adjacent slots where either slot is missing a platform, a 4.3 / 3.6 / package-install line missing from either slot, a bug count timestamped before the later run, a bug count just outside the 24-hour inclusive UTC freshness window, and a same-year numeric `W(n)`/`W(n+1)` pair that is not actually consecutive Mondays — and accepts a fully conforming artifact in which each slot has both platforms and that slot's focused evidence, plus positive ISO `52→01` and `53→01` fixtures and an exact-boundary 24-hour freshness fixture. test: `tests/terminals/test_backend_selection.py::test_flip_gate_rejects_every_nonconforming_artifact`.
- 5.3.4 - The evidence artifact records workflow, weekly slot id, run URL, commit sha, UTC timestamp, platforms, and the per-slot package-install result per qualifying run, the 4.3 and 3.6 focused-result lines, and the dated open-bug count with its query and query timestamp. file: `docs/evidence/native-backend-flip.md`.

## E1: End-to-End Verification [category: test] (depends: P1, P2, P3, P4, P5)

`kind: deliverable`

Target:
- `tests/e2e/test_terminal_client_stack.py`

One isolated-daemon run (`GOBBY_TEST_PROTECT=1`, temporary state, ports, and sockets — never the user's daemon or host) exercising the full stack: spawn one tmux-backend and one native-backend agent run through the same spawn surface; verify terminal rows, roster/`/api/terminals` contents, and attention episodes for both; drive a scripted gclient frame session attaching to both terminals (native direct, tmux via the capture-poll observer), asserting that the frame channel carries frames only, that keystrokes routed through the daemon reach the right terminal, and that detach/reattach works; answer one attention prompt per backend through the respond API; view the native terminal through the web proxy path simultaneously with the gclient observer and exercise the cross-surface lease policy in both directions — both surfaces start read-only, gclient takes control through the daemon and receives input, the web tab explicitly takes over and the gclient pane renders `terminal_lease_lost` read-only, then gclient takes it back — asserting after each transition that input reaches exactly the lease holder's terminal and no keystroke is silently dropped; **outages are split by transport**: stopping the daemon process leaves `gclient` attached directly and read-only (frames keep flowing from the host; writes cannot be issued) while the browser proxy disconnects/finalizes and later reattaches when the daemon returns; a targeted lease- or write-handler fault (HTTP/write path down, WebSocket relay still up) makes both surfaces read-only while both continue receiving frames — do not stop only HTTP and claim the WebSocket is gone, and do not stop the whole daemon and claim the browser still receives frames; attach additionally to a third terminal — an external tmux session that already has its own client at a different size — from the browser tab and the gclient pane at once, asserting they share one observer, that its geometry and active-pane selection are unchanged throughout including across an owner resize, that its pane properties match a never-viewed control pane, and that no extra tmux client is ever created; retry a write on a live connection and assert it executes exactly once, including a matching-fingerprint `client_write_seq` replay that does not mutate the PTY a second time and a conflicting-fingerprint reuse that is `write_seq_conflict`; then force a control-client *reconnect* mid-write and assert the write is reported indeterminate and not re-sent while a `kill` and a `resize` in the same situation are safely retried; a native-default `terminal_create` at the `max_attachments_total - 4` ceiling is refused before fork; disconnect the control client after `spawn_prepared` and before `spawn_commit` and assert the child stays blocked and unpromoted, `list.observer_bind` stays `reserved` (the slot stays charged), then on reconnect persist process, attach that same reservation, and only then `commit_spawn` before `commit_deadline_ms`; let a second prepared spawn expire after control drop and assert the unexecuted child is reaped; disconnect the control client while a committed spawn's result is in flight and assert the young pending row is not exited and the spawn completes `live`; restart the daemon and verify both terminals reconcile with the host adopted at its original epoch and streams resume, including one native run whose exit lands *during* the restart window and is finalized exactly once by reconciliation; kill the host and verify orphan/interrupt/resume semantics for the native run while the tmux run is unaffected; finish both runs and verify capture-before-kill artifacts and lifecycle finalization. Rust-side: `cargo nextest run -p gobby-terminal -p gobby-client` green with the `gterm-pty` group serialized.

**Acceptance:**

- E1.1 - The isolated-daemon end-to-end run covering both backends, both clients (gclient frames + web proxy) sharing one observer per physical terminal with every write crossing the daemon, cross-surface lease takeover in both directions, transport-split outages (daemon-down: gclient direct frames continue read-only and the browser proxy finalizes; write-handler fault with relay up: both surfaces read-only and both still receive frames), an externally owned session whose geometry, active-pane selection, and pane properties survive being viewed and resized, exactly-once write retry within a connection including matching-fingerprint `client_write_seq` replay and `write_seq_conflict` on payload reuse, indeterminate-not-retried write across a reconnect, a native-default `terminal_create` refused before fork at `max_attachments_total - 4`, a prepared-uncommitted spawn remaining claimable across control drop until deadline with `observer_bind: reserved` still charged, persist, attach of that same reservation, and `commit_spawn` on reconnect, a prepared spawn reaped on deadline expiry, and a committed in-flight spawn surviving a reconnect, attention respond, daemon restart with host adoption and exit-during-restart reconciliation, and host-crash recovery passes; all referenced Rust and Python focused suites are green. test: `tests/e2e/test_terminal_client_stack.py::test_terminal_client_stack_end_to_end`.

## D1: Plugin system for gclient and the Gobby CLI/daemon API

`kind: deferred`

Herdr's plugin system (manifest-driven `herdr-plugin.toml` packages: external argv processes in any language declaring actions, event hooks, and panes, with the entire CLI/socket API as the plugin surface) is deliberately not imported in this epic. The Gobby version is its own plan: it hosts on the public Gobby CLI + daemon API (never Python internals), lands client-side in Rust so it survives the daemon migration (C1 topology), and borrows herdr's manifest schema, `plugin_command.rs`, `plugin_paths.rs`, `persist/plugin_registry.rs`, `cli/plugin.rs`, and the actions/event-hooks/panes model from the fork clone.

```yaml
deferral:
  task_ref: "#20201"
  reason: "The plugin host needs gclient and the public daemon API surface from this epic to exist first, and its API design deserves its own elicitation and plan."
  owner: "josh"
  original_acceptance_items:
    - D1.1
```

## D2: Remote gclient attach — daemon-WS frame source and attach capabilities

`kind: deferred`

The multi-machine story (hub daemon on one machine, node daemons + gterm hosts on others, gclient roaming over Tailscale) needs a second frame-source implementation that consumes frames through a daemon WS proxy, plus the short-lived per-terminal attach-capability tokens C1 defers. 3.3's frame-source trait is the seam this lands behind; the shipped web terminal already covers remote *viewing* through the daemon WS proxy in the meantime.

```yaml
deferral:
  task_ref: "#20202"
  reason: "No remote/web-direct attach surface exists in this epic; local Unix sockets with same-user trust cover every milestone-1 flow, and the remote source arrives with the hub/node mode split."
  owner: "josh"
  original_acceptance_items:
    - D2.1
```

## D3: Hosted terminal-relay privacy stance

`kind: deferred`

In the hosted topology (gobby.ai-run hub), remote web viewing relays terminal bytes browser → hub → node daemon → host. Terminal bytes can contain secrets, so the hosted milestone must ship an explicit privacy stance — opt-in relay, end-to-end encryption between browser and node daemon, or hosted-mode viewing restrictions — enforced by the relay design rather than stated in prose.

```yaml
deferral:
  task_ref: "#20203"
  reason: "The relay this governs is a hosted-milestone surface; this epic's web viewing is same-trust-domain (local daemon proxy) where the question does not arise."
  owner: "josh"
  original_acceptance_items:
    - D3.1
```

## V1 Plan Changelog

`kind: verification`

Coordinator naming correction (Stage-0 vs evolution): `gobby` is today's Python daemon and Click CLI. This epic ships `gclient` (`gobby-client`) as a separate TUI binary. `gobby` does not exec the TUI; there is no `gobby client` wrapper. Renaming `gclient` → `gobby` and replacing the Python daemon with `gdaemon` are evolution, not this plan. 3.5 is gclient's own startup (daemon health + host diagnostic) before raw mode.

Pre-dispatch cancellation may transition pending to exited; after dispatch, shield settlement long enough to consume the response or retain an indeterminate pending outcome, resolve by spawn_key/list, then promote or reap. Add tests for every timing window.","location":"P2 / § 2.3, with recovery in §§ 3.1, 4.1, and E1","prevention":"For effectful async calls, test cancellation before dispatch, after request bytes are written, after effect creation, while awaiting response, and during persistence promotion.","principle":"Cancellation after an external effect is dispatched must preserve enough durable identity to settle, reconcile, or reap that effect.","root_cause":"The outcome matrix defines cancellation only between row creation and backend spawn, leaving later cancellation windows without a state or cleanup contract.","section_id":"2.3","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"unhandled-edge","check_key":"host-crash-process-tree-cleanup","description":"A gterm crash can orphan rows as decided while an agent process tree survives untracked. Since live native PTYs are not reconstructed, those processes have no remaining owner or durable control path.","finding_id":"F5-host-crash-process-tree","fix":"Specify a crash-safe native process-tree death mechanism for macOS and Linux, retaining the settled orphaned/interrupted row transition. Without checking Welcome.host_epoch, gclient may attach the stale locator to a different terminal in the new host epoch.","finding_id":"F6-gclient-host-epoch-check","fix":"Make expected_host_epoch a required input to the shared frame-source attach contract for both Rust and Python. Compare immediately after Welcome and before AttachTerminal, close with HostEpochChanged on mismatch, and add a Rust replacement-plus-id-reuse test proving no attach request is sent.","location":"P3 / § 3.3, interacting with §§ 2.1, 3.1, 3.2, 4.1, 4.3, and E1","prevention":"Compare each language implementation against the same attach-state matrix: locator fetch, Welcome epoch validation, attach, host replacement, id reuse, and reconnect.","principle":"Every client that resolves a host-scoped locator must verify the expected host epoch before sending the attach request.","root_cause":"The Python frame client has the epoch comparison, while the earlier Rust gclient direct-frame contract and acceptance omit it.","section_id":"3.3","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"unhandled-edge","check_key":"tmux-poll-machine-framing","description":"Tabs, newlines, control sequences, or header-like capture text can corrupt parsing and misclassify geometry, modes, liveness, title, or generation even though the tmux invocation itself is atomic.","finding_id":"F7-tmux-poll-framing","fix":"Keep the atomic header limited to fixed-domain numeric and enumerated fields with an exact field count and guaranteed boundary. Refresh pane_title separately or encode it reversibly. Add adversarial title/control-character and header-collision capture tests.","location":"P3 / § 3.4, interacting with §§ 2.1, 3.2, 5.2, and E1","prevention":"For every mixed control/data response, identify which fields are attacker- or terminal-controlled and test delimiters, embedded newlines, escape bytes, and data that resembles the header.","principle":"Terminal-controlled text cannot share an ambiguous delimiter with control metadata.","root_cause":"The one-invocation tmux poll concatenates whitespace-delimited metadata containing free-form pane_title with raw capture output without escaping, length, or a collision-proof boundary.","section_id":"3.4","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"weak-testability","check_key":"frame-resource-bounds","description":"Entry-count bounds alone do not cap memory when keyframes are large. Implementers cannot derive a common protocol limit or prove peak memory across max_attached_terminals, and typed lag-error delivery cannot be guaranteed after the outbound path is blocked.","finding_id":"F8-concrete-resource-bounds","fix":"Pin maximum serialized frame/control-line sizes, snapshot max_bytes/max_lines, per-attachment delta and control byte budgets, browser-relay budget, delivery timeout, and lag timeout. Clamp caller inputs before allocation, define lag error as best-effort followed by EOF, and test maximum-size frames with permanently blocked sockets.","location":"P3 / § 3.2, interacting with §§ 2.2, 2.5, 4.1, 4.3, and E1","prevention":"For each queue and serialized message surface, record exact byte and entry limits, allocation clamps, timeout constants, overload action, and a maximum-load test.","principle":"A bounded-resource requirement is executable only when byte budgets, capacities, deadlines, and terminal blocked-peer behavior are concrete.","root_cause":"The plan repeatedly says queues, messages, snapshots, relays, and lag handling are bounded while leaving their limits unspecified and promising an error that a permanently blocked peer may never receive.","section_id":"3.2","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"unhandled-edge","check_key":"lease-disconnect-finalization","description":"A direct gclient crash can leave a dead lease holder. If REST dimension mutation is genuinely required, define that endpoint and payload explicitly before retaining a REST bounds test.","location":"P2 / § 2.5 and P3 / § 3.2","prevention":"Cross-check each bounds assertion against the exact request schema and HTTP method before declaring its ingress test.","principle":"Acceptance must place validation at operations that actually carry the validated fields.","root_cause":"Dimension-bound acceptance names AttachTerminal, whose declared message contains only host_terminal_id, and a GET-only REST surface with no dimension input.","section_id":"2.5","severity":"blocking"}],"verdict":"needs_review"},"session_id":"f4469e9b-2b63-4149-ad3f-1718ca640d75"}

Did not split compact_continuation (one send_keys site). Did not assign every imported vendor file.

The reaper can observe no effect just before the retry creates one, CAS the row to exited, and leave the live retry to lose settlement against an exited or orphaned row; the existing lost-CAS rule covers same/conflicting live identities only.","finding_id":"F16-retry-attempt-fence","fix":"Add a durable attempt generation or refreshed attempt_started_at set before each retry dispatch, require the reaper and reconciler to CAS the observed generation, define settlement when reload finds exited or orphaned, and test every retry-versus-reaper window.","location":"P2 / § 2.3, interacting with §§ 3.1, 4.1, and E1","prevention":"Enumerate retry/reaper interleavings before dispatch, during effect creation, after effect visibility, and during settlement; make every reaper CAS identify the specific inactive attempt it evaluated.","principle":"A stale-row reaper must distinguish an expired inactive attempt from a currently active retry before mutating durable spawn state.","root_cause":"Retries reuse the same terminal row and spawn key while staleness remains based on the original row age, with no refreshed attempt marker or generation.","section_id":"2.3","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"unhandled-edge","causal_finding_id":"F5-host-crash-process-tree","causal_section_ids":["3.1"],"check_key":"host-crash-process-tree-cleanup","description":"Walking the dead host PID with pgrep -P or /proc cannot reliably find children after reparenting, so the promised SIGHUP-ignoring leader and grandchild can survive while the row is orphaned. The test can pass only when it races before reparenting.","finding_id":"F17-crash-stable-process-identity","fix":"Record a stable per-terminal process group or equivalent identity before host failure, signal that identity with PID-reuse guards through TERM and KILL, define the policy for descendants that deliberately create a new session, and test cleanup after gterm has fully exited.","introduced_in_round":1,"location":"P3 / § 3.1, interacting with §§ 4.2, 5.1, and E1","prevention":"For every owner-crash cleanup design, verify that cleanup identity survives owner exit, child reparenting, PID reuse, and the full TERM-to-KILL interval.","principle":"Crash cleanup must use process identity that remains discoverable after the owning process dies and children are reparented.","root_cause":"The Round 1 repair discovers PTY children and descendants only after gterm is observed dead, when parent-child relationships may already have disappeared.","section_id":"3.1","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"unhandled-edge","causal_finding_id":"F8-concrete-resource-bounds","causal_section_ids":["3.2","3.4"],"check_key":"frame-resource-bounds","description":"max_attached_terminals=64 caps distinct pane observers and intentionally permits multiple viewers to share each pane, while § 3.2 allocates a 2 MiB delta queue plus control queue per attachment and claims peak memory is bounded by 64 times that budget. Unlimited viewers of the same terminal evade the cap, and user attachments can also consume capacity needed by daemon observers.","finding_id":"F18-attachment-memory-cap","fix":"Add separate per-terminal and global attachment-count caps plus a global queued-byte budget, reserve capacity for daemon lifecycle/output observers, define typed refusal behavior, and test many viewers on one pane plus cross-terminal saturation.","introduced_in_round":1,"location":"P3 / §§ 3.2 and 3.4, interacting with §§ 4.2–4.3","prevention":"For every memory formula, map each allocation to its cardinality dimension and test simultaneous saturation across terminals, viewers, and privileged internal observers.","principle":"A memory bound must cap the multiplicity that actually allocates each bounded queue.","root_cause":"The Round 1 cap limits distinct attached terminals for tmux poll rate, while frame memory is allocated once per viewer attachment.","section_id":"3.2","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"unhandled-edge","causal_finding_id":"F8-concrete-resource-bounds","causal_section_ids":["3.2"],"check_key":"frame-keyframe-size-closure","description":"Delta overflow requires a fresh full keyframe, yet the maximum valid grid can contain 1,000,000 cells and the plan provides no worst-case encoding bound under MAX_FRAME_SIZE=2 MiB. A valid terminal can therefore enter a state whose required resync frame the protocol must reject.","finding_id":"F19-keyframe-size-bound","fix":"Choose and document a cell/dimension limit whose worst-case serialized keyframe is proven below 2 MiB, reject larger grids before allocation, and add worst-case keyframe plus delta-overflow recovery tests using maximally expensive cell content.","introduced_in_round":1,"location":"P3 / § 3.2, interacting with §§ 2.2, 3.4, 4.1, and 4.3","prevention":"For every protocol size limit, serialize the worst admitted state and prove its recovery/keyframe representation stays within the same bound.","principle":"Every admitted terminal state must have a valid bounded resynchronization representation.","root_cause":"The Round 1 2 MiB frame cap is combined with a 1,000,000-cell admitted grid without proving a full encoded keyframe fits.","section_id":"3.2","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"missing-requirement","check_key":"lease-holder-release-transition","description":"Section 3.3 requires gclient to release control on focus change while the pane stays attached and rendering. idempotent terminal_release_control scoped by terminal_id and attachment_id with a typed result, include it in the shared golden corpus and exhaustive unions, and test release racing takeover, disconnect, stale attachment ids, and repeated release.","location":"P2 / § 2.5, interacting with § 3.3 and E1","prevention":"Build a state-transition table for observe, held, released, displaced, disconnected, and reconnected states and map every edge to a concrete message and test.","principle":"Every promised lease state transition requires an explicit wire operation and typed result.","root_cause":"The protocol defines take-control, result, lease-lost, and disconnect cleanup while omitting voluntary release from a still-attached client.","section_id":"2.5","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"unhandled-edge","check_key":"lifecycle-event-backpressure","description":"A slow or disconnected daemon can accumulate exit, resize, and title events on subscribe_events without any numeric entry/byte bound or producer behavior. list-based recovery is defined after reconnect, yet the plan never says when the live stream drops, - resolution_notes: Folded residual and fixer-induced defects. 2.2 acceptance is now leaf-local (Python dimensions, four carriers, tmux coordinator/latch); host-config, native races, and wire matching moved to 3.1/3.2/4.1. 3.1.14 is a host_manager persist-before-commit test. Prepared spawn has a host commit deadline and connection owner; daemon/control loss reaps the unexecuted child; list/adoption distinguish prepared vs committed; spawn_commit may replay. WriteCoordinator.run_sequence holds the lock across multi-step wake/prompt/plan-keystroke flows. Control reserve_observer admits the daemon internal observer before native spawn (not a host lease token). 5.3 owns a weekly macOS/Linux workflow that produces the flip-gate evidence. Adversary coverage validation failed shadow_manifest_mismatch; fence records that protocol failure and proceeds needs_review on the five findings.

- reviewer_session: ff1c2fc7-bb85-4deb-952e-2d7476d48fb0

- resolution_notes: Folded all seven residual and fixer-induced defects at review cap. 3.1.5 is host/epoch/PID/row adoption only; frame-stream resume stays 5.1.2. Prepared spawn stays claimable until commit_deadline_ms with atomic owner transfer; disconnect does not reap; deadline expiry does; adoption never promotes prepared-only. Weekly workflow producer moved to 5.1; 5.3 consumes evidence and flips the default. reserve_observer is reserved, then bound, then released, with reserve_key replay; control disconnect releases only unbound reservations. run_sequence cancel-before-first is clean; after first dispatch the sequence latches indeterminate and sends no remainder. Lease grant/release/takeover share the coordinator lock; queued write/resize revalidate attachment_id and lease_generation immediately before the backend effect. Internal observer binds after persist and before spawn_commit; bind failure aborts and reaps.

- resolution_notes: Folded all eight residual and newly spotted defects on the operator-extended sixth round. 5.1 now depends on 5.2, 4.3, and 3.6 so the weekly workflow cannot land before those producers. TerminalRuntime is prepare_spawn/commit_spawn; reconnect persist and observer bind-or-reuse before commit; list carries start_time and observer_bind. release_observer plus kill of prepared entries on live control. Daemon-stop resume metadata is terminal_id plus spawn_key. 2.4 owns the wake, compact_self, and sessions-terminal test seams. Host no longer preempts user attachments; reserve_observer fails typed under saturation. Lease notifications carry lease_generation, captured under the coordinator lock; reducers ignore stale generations. Write/paste data is utf8-b64 with encoded-line preflight.

- resolution_notes: Folded all fifteen residual findings. 1.1 cargo-package tests moved to 1.2; 1.1 closes on vendor Zig plus build.rs source checks. Golden corpora now pin detach.json, resize.json, and graphics.bin. AttachLocator.frame_host_epoch is request-time: native from the row, tmux from the adopted host. First tmux attach captures a bounded -S history window. Resume metadata is exactly terminal_id plus spawn_key. 3.1 depends on 2.4. Remaining tmux test seams and HookManagerFactory injection land in 2.4. Seven spawn_agent_impl suites join 4.2. test_distribution.py joins 3.6. Discovery waits for a matching pending managed row. Committed native terminals keep a zero-queue observer entitlement across daemon-frame drop. The attachment finalizer covers frame EOF, lag, relay, and host loss. list uses pgid plus start_time. tmux observation_state reconverges from list after daemon downtime.

Test restart under full user saturation.","location":"Phase 3–5 / §§ 3.2, 4.1, 4.2, 5.1, E1","prevention":"Walk capacity accounting across every owner disconnect/reconnect state for both prepared and committed terminals.","principle":"One internal observer per committed live native terminal must remain recoverable across daemon restart at full attachment capacity.","root_cause":"A bound reservation is released when the old daemon frame connection closes, while user attachments cannot be preempted and reconnect rebind is defined only for prepared spawns.","section_id":"4.2","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"unhandled-edge","check_key":"frame-close-lease-finalization","description":"A direct gclient or proxy browser attachment can lose its frame stream yet remain the daemon's lease holder, leaving other viewers blocked and permitting blind input from a pane that no longer renders.","finding_id":"F64","fix":"Invoke the generic attachment finalizer on frame EOF, typed lag close, relay failure, host loss, detach, and daemon-WS close; release under the coordinator lock, emit the new generation, and require a fresh frame attach before control can be reacquired.","location":"Phase 2–4 / §§ 2.5, 3.2, 3.3, 4.3, E1","prevention":"For each attachment resource, enumerate control-channel and data-channel termination independently and route all combinations through one finalizer.","principle":"Every attachment termination path must finalize its daemon lease under the same lock and generation.","root_cause":"Lease cleanup is attached to terminal_detach and daemon-WebSocket close, but host-frame EOF, lag closure, relay overflow, or host loss can end rendering while that WebSocket remains alive.","section_id":"2.5","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"traceability","check_key":"prepared-list-process-group-identity","description":"Reconnect is required to reconstruct process-group cleanup from list alone, but the contract is internally inconsistent about pid versus pgid. The text also does not map confirmed-absence TerminalExited delivery versus orphaned row state consistently.","finding_id":"F66","fix":"Define tmux observation states and their frame/lifecycle-to-row mapping, add state/reason/generation to list, reconcile it on reconnect, and test persistent failure and recovery during daemon downtime.","location":"Phase 2–5 / §§ 2.1, 3.2–3.4, 5.2","prevention":"For each host-owned state transition, verify live delivery, missed-event detection, snapshot reconstruction, and row-state mapping.","principle":"Any state transition that can occur while the daemon is absent must be reconstructible from the reconnect snapshot.","root_cause":"The tmux poller can mark persistent transient failure as orphaned, but subscribe_events carries only exit/resize/title and list omits observation health, failure reason, and generation.","section_id":"3.4","severity":"blocking"}],"reviewer_run":"726f3591-eaf4-4337-b665-263ff00939c4","reviewer_session":"33c60527-193a-43fa-bbbe-8fa9f60992bc","round":7,"round_number":7,"verdict":"needs_review"},"session_id":"f4469e9b-2b63-4149-ad3f-1718ca640d75"}

Round 8 needs_review: accepted F67–F71 (five blocking). 2.5 owns the final repo-wide field-gate test and expected remainder. MCP send_keys/compact_self/capture_output construction hops (_factory.py, registries.py, http.py, stdio) propagate the composition-root terminal services. AttachHistory is a standalone frame before the first keyframe so history plus MAX_CELLS stays inside MAX_FRAME_SIZE. Direct gclient sends terminal_detach on frame EOF/lag/epoch loss so the daemon finalizer can run. tmux orphaned_observation stays on the recoverable health plane; durable terminals.state remains live until confirmed absence.

Round 9 needs_review: accepted F72–F83 (twelve blocking). C1 drops evolution.md naming and later daemon topology. MCP identity chain is daemon-HTTP only; stdio is proxy-only. 3.2 and 3.4 own wire_types.rs, wire_codec.rs, and source_size.rs. tmux generation mismatch is confirmed absence and mark_exited. AttachHistory is cached per observer generation and replayed to every attachment; the proxy emits terminal_attach_history. terminal_attach is observe-only. One TerminalEffectBridge settles hook timeout and cancel. Committed native observers become entitled on frame loss. list carries spawn_key. 3.4 owns frame_source.rs and hello.bin. MAX_CELLS is derived from FrameData and TerminalAnsi; the browser hop fragments JSON.
