The terminal-WS union and golden corpus contain no history event, so Python and TypeScript must invent an unversioned shape or lose truncation metadata.","finding_id":"F76","fix":"Define terminal_attach_history{terminal_id, attachment_id, text, truncated, dropped_bytes, total_bytes}, add its JSON fixture to 2.5 Targets, implement the Python emitter and TypeScript reducer, and test maximum history followed by the first keyframe through the proxy.","location":"P2–P4 / §§ 2.5.17, 3.2.26, 3.4.15, and 4.3","prevention":"For each bridge, enumerate every input variant and map it to an owned output shape, encoder, decoder, fixture, and ordering test.","principle":"Every typed host-frame value crossing a second protocol boundary needs an explicit versioned shape and corpus entry on that boundary.","root_cause":"AttachHistory was added to the bincode frame corpus, while the proxy requirement only says to forward it as a typed WebSocket event and never defines that JSON event.","section_id":"4.3","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"unhandled-edge","check_key":"tmux-attach-history-contract","description":"Only the first tmux attachment captures and receives AttachHistory. A browser-first attach or the losing side of concurrent first attaches can leave a later gclient without the history its copy mode promises, even though attach succeeds.","finding_id":"F77","fix":"Capture once per observer-registry generation, retain the bounded AttachHistory value on that observer, and emit it to every attachment before that attachment's first keyframe. Count the cache in host memory bounds, free it when the observer is reaped, and test browser-first, concurrent, later-join, and new-generation cases.","location":"P3–P4 / §§ 3.2.26, 3.3.13, 3.4.7, 3.4.15, and 4.3","prevention":"Test creator, concurrent loser, later joiner, and detach-to-zero/new-generation paths for every create-or-join attachment resource.","principle":"Per-attachment initialization data must reach every successful attachment regardless of which concurrent client created the shared observer.","root_cause":"The plan couples history emission to created:true while prohibiting later recapture and never defines bounded cache-and-replay for joiners.","section_id":"3.4","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"unhandled-edge","check_key":"lease-acquisition-single-transition","description":"terminal_attach accepts mode=control, yet every behavioral section says attachment begins read-only and control is acquired through terminal_take_control. The control value has no grant, held, takeover, lease_generation, or cleanup semantics and creates a second unreviewed lease path.","finding_id":"F78","fix":"Remove mode from terminal_attach and terminal_attach_result and make all attaches observe-only. Keep terminal_take_control as the sole acquisition transition, then update Python, Rust, TypeScript, and the terminal-WS golden fixtures.","location":"P2–P4 / §§ 2.5.7, 2.5.9, 3.3.2, and 4.3.3","prevention":"Enumerate every protocol enum value through success, refusal, takeover, generation ordering, disconnect, and golden-corpus cases.","principle":"Every accepted wire enum value must map to one explicit authority transition and typed outcome.","root_cause":"terminal_attach retained mode observe|control from an earlier design after control acquisition moved exclusively to terminal_take_control.","section_id":"2.5","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"unhandled-edge","check_key":"sync-async-effect-settlement","description":"SessionCoordinator is synchronous while TerminalRuntime writes are async. The plan leaves the bridge unspecified, so a timed-out or cancelled Future can still mutate the terminal after the hook reports failure and retries, bypassing the shared indeterminate-write rule.","finding_id":"F79","fix":"Specify one injected bridge used only from non-loop hook threads, with a bounded wait and explicit effect settlement before return. Route post-dispatch uncertainty through IndeterminateWrite and the unresolved-action latch, own the affected bridge/tests in 2.4, and add focused timeout, cancellation, late-completion, and loop-misuse cases.","location":"P2 / §§ 2.2 and 2.4.14","prevention":"For every sync-to-async mutation bridge, test loop-thread misuse, pre-dispatch timeout, post-dispatch timeout, cancellation, late completion, and retry suppression.","principle":"A synchronous wrapper around an asynchronous terminal mutation must settle timeout and cancellation without allowing an unobserved late effect.","root_cause":"The F68 repair names a sync-to-async seam but does not define its loop, thread, timeout, cancellation, or indeterminate-effect contract; the current generic bridge cancels and returns failure after a timed-out wait.","section_id":"2.4","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"unhandled-edge","check_key":"committed-observer-rebind-under-saturation","description":"Section 3.2 says a bound reservation is released on frame detach or disconnect, then says that same event on a committed live native terminal must retain an entitled zero-queue slot. Implementers can release the slot needed for saturated restart recovery or leak an entitlement for an uncommitted spawn.","finding_id":"F80","fix":"Split cleanup by commit state: prepared or uncommitted bound observers release on frame loss; committed live native observers transition bound to entitled. Only terminal exit or explicit release removes that entitlement. Add generation-race tests for stale disconnect and release callbacks.","location":"P3–P4 / §§ 3.2.22, 3.2.25, and 4.2.11","prevention":"Cross-product observer bind state, spawn commit state, disconnect source, explicit release, terminal exit, and stale cleanup generation.","principle":"A cleanup event must have one state transition after commit-state qualification.","root_cause":"The committed-observer entitlement repair was added beside the older unconditional bound-to-released frame-disconnect rule.","section_id":"3.2","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"unhandled-edge","check_key":"host-list-spawn-identity-parity","description":"The plan makes terminal_id plus spawn_key the host registry and daemon-resume identity, and 2.1 says the host echoes spawn_key in list. Section 3.2's exhaustive list row omits it, so reconnect cannot cross-check the pair or prove a replay resolves the intended spawn.","finding_id":"F81","fix":"Add spawn_key to host list rows, control_list.json, 3.2.11, Python decoding, and reconciliation. Require terminal_id and spawn_key equality before commit replay, adoption, retry settlement, or destructive cleanup.","location":"P2–P4 / §§ 2.1, 2.3.15, 3.2.11, and 4.1","prevention":"Diff every registry key and durable resume key against spawn responses, list rows, golden fixtures, decoders, and reconciliation comparisons.","principle":"Reconciliation must observe the complete durable identity used to deduplicate the effect it is reconciling.","root_cause":"The host registry and resume contracts were repaired to terminal_id plus spawn_key, but spawn_key was omitted from the exhaustive list response.","section_id":"3.2","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"traceability","check_key":"golden-corpus-declared-shape-parity","description":"Section 3.4 changes Hello and AttachTerminal inputs but omits crates/gclient/src/frame_source.rs, the sole gclient handshake implementation, and hello.bin, the golden fixture for the changed Hello shape. The leaf cannot implement or pin its own recursive-view contract.","finding_id":"F82","fix":"Add crates/gclient/src/frame_source.rs and crates/gterminal/tests/fixtures/wire_golden/hello.bin to 3.4 Targets. Update the trait and encoder for optional client tmux identity and locator-aware attach, regenerate the fixture, and include it in 3.4.10.","location":"P3 / §§ 3.3.10, 3.4.4, and 3.4.10","prevention":"For every wire-shape change, trace the type through all encoders and list each changed fixture and implementation file in the owning leaf.","principle":"A leaf that changes a wire handshake must own the real client encoder and every golden fixture whose bytes change.","root_cause":"Section 3.4 introduces client tmux identity in Hello and locator-aware AttachTerminal after 3.3 has placed all socket dialing and handshake encoding in frame_source.rs, but 3.4 targets only the caller view and selected host fixtures.","section_id":"3.4","severity":"blocking"},{"category":"unhandled-edge","check_key":"composite-frame-size-budget","description":"A grid legal under the FrameData-derived MAX_CELLS bound may exceed MAX_FRAME_SIZE when encoded as TerminalAnsi, and a legal host ANSI frame can expand again inside terminal_output JSON. The plan defines neither a bound for those composed forms nor fragmentation, so the proxy can reject a legal frame or exceed its claimed queue/message cap.","finding_id":"F83","fix":"Derive MAX_CELLS from the worst serialized negotiated host encoding or define bounded frame fragmentation. Then preflight or chunk the complete JSON terminal event, including envelope and escaping, and test maximum alternating-attribute Unicode grids through TerminalAnsi and the browser proxy.","location":"P3–P4 / §§ 3.2.15, 3.2.16, and 4.3.4","prevention":"For every bridge, calculate and test the maximum complete serialized message for every encoding at every hop.","principle":"Every negotiated encoding and enclosing transport envelope must be bounded after complete serialization, including escaping and metadata.","root_cause":"The cell bound is derived only from FrameData bincode size and the relay copies the same 2 MiB number to a JSON WebSocket hop without composing TerminalAnsi or JSON expansion.","section_id":"3.2","severity":"blocking"}],"plan_hash":"54a6c4321612dbcc587828422f4e3ae78e601700ca8a831c47ba654cfeacd1c3","reviewer_run":"9eadccb5-f67e-4db2-a1f5-569fbf7df452","reviewer_session":"920da161-457c-4df8-8073-f4185472230c","round":9,"round_number":9,"verdict":"needs_review"},"session_id":"f4469e9b-2b63-4149-ad3f-1718ca640d75"}

- resolution_notes: Folded all three Round 9 residuals. TerminalEffectBridge is an early-return post-dispatch waiter: the hook waiter returns IndeterminateWrite after the dispatch marker; a runner-owned shielded Task keeps the per-terminal lock through settlement, observes late failure, and drains on loop shutdown. terminal_ws_fragment is the canonical WS envelope in 2.5 fixtures; 4.3's useTmuxSessions.ts reducer reassembles with 8 MiB / 5s bounds and clears on complete, sequence error, lag, and disconnect. 3.4 reruns crates/gclient/tests/source_size.rs for handshake and view edits.

- resolution_notes: Accepted all four. 2.5 now targets the four tests plus TerminalSessionList.tsx and pins terminal_attachment_finalized (2.5.27). 2.5.26 is Python/TS only; gclient safe-integer checks moved to 3.3.18. 4.3.11 tests observe-only mid-fragment then stale slice. 2.1 adds automatic_write_quarantined_at; 2.4.18 persists it before lock release, restores after restart, and clears on observation or operator write.

**Round 14** `kind: verification`

- reviewer_run: d8d21799-2968-4967-93d7-b72f918c1a63
- reviewer_session: e9b47e5a-1f12-4fc6-91d6-466c8409482a
- verdict: needs_review
- findings:
- F96 blocking: gclient finalizer and reassembly outcomes left in 2.5
- F97 blocking: observer finalization increments the holder's generation
- F98 blocking: quarantine clears on an ambiguous snapshot
- F99 blocking: terminal_attachment_finalized shares the saturated frame queue
- F100 blocking: 2.4 shutdown drain omits runner_lifecycle_shutdown.py
- resolution_notes: Accepted all five. 2.5.21 and 2.5.27 are server and browser only; gclient mid-fragment finalization is 3.3.19. lease_generation advances only when finalization removes the current holder; terminal_attachment_finalized applies by attachment_id; 2.5.28 covers observer-finalization and takeover-then-old-disconnect. Quarantine lifts only on effect-specific positive observation or an operator write. Lifecycle events use reserved outbound capacity; a wedged reserved send closes the WebSocket (4.3.12). 2.4 extracts runner_lifecycle_terminal_effects.py, targets runner_lifecycle_shutdown.py, and adds 2.4.20.

Accepted all eight. Split 2.5.28 into observer-finalization at N and a fresh B2 takeover-then-old-disconnect (2.5.29). Both reducers reject only lower lease_generation and apply equal-generation held/released results (2.5.21, 2.5.30, 3.3.2). Quarantine persists automatic_write_quarantine_action_key with the timestamp (2.1.21, 2.4.18). terminals.project_id is NOT NULL with isolation after session/run clear (2.1.22, 2.5.2). 1.2/3.3 Targets gained the named keep-set and UI files plus Cargo.lock; 1.1 vendor trees stay verbatim copies. 3.1 declares gterm with required-features vt-engine. 1.4 targets explicit_routing.rs. 3.2 peak memory includes max_attached_terminals times tmux_attach_history_max_bytes (3.4.18).

Saturating distinct terminals exceeds the asserted formula.","finding_id":"F108","fix":"Revise the aggregate bound and tests to include max_attached_terminals × tmux_attach_history_max_bytes, or charge each history cache against an existing bounded pool. Add saturation coverage with maximum-size histories across the maximum distinct attached tmux terminals and verify memory is released after detach.","location":"P3 / §§3.2 and 3.4","prevention":"For every aggregate formula, sweep adjacent deliverables for caches and multiply each allocation by its independent maximum cardinality.","principle":"An aggregate memory bound must include every allocation retained at the bounded concurrency limit.","root_cause":"Section 3.2 derives peak host memory solely from per-attachment queues, while §3.4 later adds a persistent per-observer history allocation.","section_id":"3.2","severity":"blocking"}],"reviewer_run":"3bb5e939-b650-43d0-9baa-0f9d944f4cd8","reviewer_session":"4c57d749-9ae3-4d0b-9623-021ad189e789","round":15,"round_number":15,"verdict":"needs_review"},"session_id":"f4469e9b-2b63-4149-ad3f-1718ca640d75"}

Round 16 accepted F109-F117. Active external locator project ownership is immutable (same-project replay converges; different-project discovery is typed project_ownership_conflict). unresolved_writes is bounded (256-byte keys, 32 entries, 64 KiB) with reserve-before-dispatch. Discovery producers receive the composition-root TerminalManager via EventHandlers and _app_lifecycle. Authenticated ping carries host_pid for adoption. Native copy-mode uses attachment-local SetScrollOffset. Tmux AttachHistory uses capture-pane -J. write_key queries pane flags at dispatch. Stage-0 release is the four macOS/Linux triples. Each 5.3 weekly slot must itself contain both platforms and that slot's focused evidence.

Accepted all 11 findings (F118–F128). F118: 3.2.27 is Rust ping/corpus only; Python decode and missing-field refusal moved to 4.1.14. F119: 1.2 Targets now include crates/gterminal/src/lib.rs as the crate-root module/re-export surface. F120: 4.2 owns spawn_models.py and SpawnRequest.terminal_backend as the spawn_agent_impl to execute_spawn boundary (4.2.12). F121: unresolved_writes is write-ahead before backend dispatch; Delivered/no-effect failure clears, hard death/cancel/indeterminate retain; tests 2.2.20 and 2.4.22. F122: native_scrollback_max_lines=10000 and native_scrollback_max_bytes=8 MiB with oldest-first eviction; peak formula adds (max_attachments_total-4) times that byte ceiling (3.2.29). F123: polled tmux geometry is a MAX_CELLS ingress; oversize is recoverable stale/geometry_exceeds_max_cells (3.4.20). F124: live poll is capture-pane -p -e -N; AttachHistory stays -J (3.4.21). F125: unknown-host cleanup lists the host first, READ COMMITTED lookup per identity, and the same lookup immediately before kill (3.1.18). F126: 5.1.3 runs the 3.6 installer fallback-chain smoke as the package-install producer; 5.3.4 records the per-slot result. F127: bug-count freshness is 24 hours inclusive UTC after the later run. F128: ISO adjacency is a Monday-date difference of exactly seven days, with 52-to-01 and 53-to-01 positives.

Accepted all seven findings (F129–F135). F129: gclient release preflights the exact gobby-terminal crates.io version and requires successful gterm publication before the gclient tag (3.6.6). F130: title_max_bytes=1024 with a CHECK and host-list envelope proof at max_attachments_total+max_attached_terminals (2.1.26, 3.2.30). F131: GET /api/terminals and terminal_list default to pending|live with cursor pages (100/500, 1 MiB) (2.5.2, 2.5.34, 3.3.21). F132: E1 outages are split by transport — daemon-down keeps gclient direct frames read-only and finalizes the browser proxy; write-handler fault with relay up keeps both surfaces read-only with frames. F133: the browser relay frame queue is connection-wide; overflow or send timeout closes the WebSocket; lifecycle reserve is 16 entries / 64 KiB / 2s (4.3.4, 4.3.12, 4.3.14). F134: GRANT SELECT,INSERT,UPDATE,DELETE ON terminals TO gobby_daemon_runtime plus runner_tests CRUD (2.1.25). F135: rollback_runner_resources drains a newly spawned gterm, preserves an adopted one, and closes native clients (3.1.19, 4.1.15).

Accepted all four findings (F136–F139). F136: useTmuxSessions.ts drains terminal_list pages with ID dedup and generation fencing (2.5.35). F137: test_version_pins.py is parameterized over gdaemon, gterm, and gclient Cargo package versions (3.6.7). F138: construction rollback is one ordered coroutine — stop producers, drain spawned gterm through a still-open control path, then close clients (3.1.20, 4.1.16). F139: observation_reason is a closed enum; 3.2.30 maximizes the longest reason with 1024-byte titles (3.4.22).

Accepted all five findings (F140–F144). F140: prepared frame-loss keeps a zero-queue entitlement until commit, kill, or deadline (3.2.31, 4.2.13). F141: terminal_input/paste carry client_write_seq and receive terminal_write_outcome; both reducers enter uncertain read-only on indeterminate (2.5.36, 3.3.22, 4.3.15). F142: rollback_runner_resources_async is awaited from GobbyRunner.create and both run_daemon failure branches (3.1.21, 4.1.18). F143: authenticated host_shutdown is a 3.1 control verb used by rollback, replacement, and full stop (3.1.22, 4.1.17). F144: observation_state/reason enums and max_attached_terminals/tmux_attach_history_* are owned by 3.2; 3.4 maps poll outcomes onto them.

F148: execute_spawn owns reserve/bind/commit; native-default terminal_create saturates at N-4 (4.2.14).
